# Decisions log

One line per meaningful choice: date — decision — reason.

- 2026-07-30 — Adopted brief v3 (`docs/mi-cuaderno-project-brief-v3.md`) as the contract; Phase 0 + 0.5 plan approved before any code. — §2 working agreement.
- 2026-07-30 — Repo is public, named `mi-cuaderno`. — GitHub Pages is free on public repos; personal data never enters the repo (it lives only in the device's browser storage).
- 2026-07-30 — Brief and prototype committed under `docs/`. — They contain no personal data and are the project's reference documentation.
- 2026-07-30 — Node.js 24 LTS installed via winget; single toolchain for both the app and the data pipeline. — One language (JS) everywhere; no Python dependency.
- 2026-07-30 — Tailwind v4 via the `@tailwindcss/vite` plugin. — Current stable major; no PostCSS config file needed.
- 2026-07-30 — PWA service worker uses `autoUpdate`. — App shell updates silently on redeploy; notebook data is unaffected because it lives in browser storage, not in the app files.
- 2026-07-30 — Manifest `theme_color`/`background_color` = paper `#FAF9F4`; icons are generated placeholders (blue tile, serif M, highlighter swipe). — Matches prototype design tokens; icons swappable later without config changes.
- 2026-07-30 — App-code license: deferred (repo currently has no LICENSE file). — Data licenses are the urgent part (§4); code license decision can wait for the owner.
- 2026-07-30 — `npm audit`'s 8 "high" findings left unfixed. — All are one dependency chain inside the build tool (`vite-plugin-pwa` → workbox → old `brace-expansion`); build-time only, nothing ships to the app; the offered fix downgrades the PWA plugin.

- 2026-07-31 — GitHub Pages had to be enabled once by hand (`gh api --method POST /repos/.../pages -f build_type=workflow`); the workflow's `configure-pages` with `enablement: true` cannot create the Pages site itself. — The built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` lacks that permission, so the first deploy failed with "Resource not accessible by integration". Subsequent pushes deploy automatically; `enablement: true` is kept because it succeeds once the site exists. Repo: `bpanozzo23-prog/mi-cuaderno`.

## Phase 0.5 — data spike (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — Dictionary source is `kaikki.org/dictionary/Spanish/…jsonl.gz` (English Wiktionary, English glosses), despite kaikki labelling it DEPRECATED. — It is the only kaikki file matching brief §4; the newer `downloads/es/` path is the *Spanish* Wiktionary with Spanish glosses and is not interchangeable. Re-check at each dataset refresh.
- 2026-07-31 — Canonical IDs preserve letter case in the lemma part. — Lowercasing conflated proper nouns with common ones (FIFA/fifa); removed 50 of 990 ID collisions. Case-insensitive matching belongs to search, not identity.
- 2026-07-31 — kaikki records sharing word+pos+etymology are MERGED into one entry, not given distinct IDs. — kaikki splits e.g. `gallo` (noun) across 3 records that are one dictionary entry; merging matches how the entry should read. Resolves the remaining 940 collisions.
- 2026-07-31 — Frequency aggregation uses an accent-SENSITIVE form index; search keeps the accent-insensitive one. — Sharing one index let the frequent adverb `así` credit its count to the rare verb `asir` (rank 121).
- 2026-07-31 — Ambiguity policy for token→lemma frequency is **citation-form-first**: if a token is some lemma's own dictionary form, only those lemmas score it. — Even-splitting handed the conjunction `pero`'s corpus count to the rare verb `perar`, ranking it 82nd. Citation-first drops `perar` to 14,462 and lifts the real `pero` to 53. Owner may override; noted in the spike report.
- 2026-07-31 — Form-index filters: reject `form_of` targets matching /^deprecated in \d{4}/, and forms tagged `inflection-template`, `class`, or `table-tags`. — These are prose and conjugation-table metadata (`es-conj`, `e-ie alternation`), not words. Kept deliberately narrow so real multiword idioms and reflexive forms (`me voy`) survive.
- 2026-07-31 — `unbzip2-stream` added as a dev dependency. — Tatoeba ships `.bz2`, which Node cannot decompress natively; a pure-JS decoder avoids depending on a system `bzip2` binary.
- 2026-07-31 — Full-dictionary size measured, not estimated: ~2.8 MB gzipped for 10k lemmas (entries 1.34 + conjugations 0.76 + search index 0.66). — Confirms the §11 chunked download is straightforward (~5 chunks of 600 KB).
## Phase 1a — data foundation (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — Dexie schema v1: `items (id, type, term, title, updatedAt, *tags, *linkedKeys)`, `events (id, at, localDate, itemKey, type)`, `prefs (key)`. — Multi-entry indexes on `tags` and `linkedKeys` make "items with tag T" and "items linking to key K" single indexed lookups instead of full scans; the latter is what computes backlinks and cleans up on delete.
- 2026-07-31 — Personal-layer timestamps are ISO-8601 strings, not epoch numbers. — They sort correctly as strings and are readable inside a backup file, which is the format the owner may one day have to repair by hand.
- 2026-07-31 — Reference-layer tables are deliberately absent from schema v1; Phase 2 adds them as separate stores. — Enforces the §5 rule that rebuilding the dictionary can never touch personal data.
- 2026-07-31 — Backup validation rejects an envelope whose `schemaVersion` is newer than the app's. — A newer file may contain fields this version would silently drop on import; better to ask the owner to update the app.
- 2026-07-31 — Duplicate *item* ids in a backup are a hard error; duplicate *event* ids are skipped. — §10 specifies skipping for events; for items there is no safe choice between two records claiming the same identity, so the import stops rather than guess.
- 2026-07-31 — Ajustes (settings) occupies the third tab until Phase 5, when Asistente joins. — Backup is the disaster-recovery mechanism (§10); it needs a permanent home from day one, not a menu.
- 2026-07-31 — Vitest + fake-indexeddb for tests (`npm test`). — fake-indexeddb runs the real Dexie code in Node, so the database tests exercise what ships rather than a mock.
- 2026-07-31 — `vite.config.js` dev server honours `process.env.PORT`. — Lets tooling assign a free port when the default 5173 is already taken.

## Phase 1c — search and linking (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — Links are stored once, on the item where the link was made; the reverse direction is computed from the `*linkedKeys` index. — The prototype wrote both sides, which cannot work in Phase 2: links will point at read-only dictionary entries that cannot store a reciprocal link. One mechanism has to serve both, so it is built that way now. The owner never sees which side stores it — both detail screens show one "Linked" list.
- 2026-07-31 — Search ranking reserves tier 3 for the Phase 2 inflected-form index, unused in Phase 1. — Keeps the tier numbers identical to brief §8, so personal and dictionary results interleave correctly when the reference layer arrives, with no renumbering.
- 2026-07-31 — An exactly typed accent outranks an accent-blind match (`sacó` typed in full ranks `sacó` above `saco`). — §8 orders exact above accent-normalized; comparing the raw strings before normalizing is what makes that distinction possible.
- 2026-07-31 — `search_miss` is logged only after typing settles (1.5 s) and stays unlogged for a query already recorded this page session. — §7 wants "words I couldn't find", not a keystroke log: typing "chamarra" letter by letter must produce one miss, not eight.
- 2026-07-31 — Searching ranks within the active type/tag filters rather than ignoring them. — A visible filter that search silently overrode would look broken.
- 2026-07-31 — Linking and unlinking do not log an `edit` event. — `edit` marks content the owner changed; links are bookkeeping, and logging them would inflate the activity feed.
- 2026-07-31 — `.gitignore` covers `mi-cuaderno-backup-*.json` and `before-import-*.json`. — Exports land in the working directory by default and contain the entire notebook; the repo is public.

## Phase 1d — tracking and Repaso (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — `SESSION_WINDOW_MINUTES = 30`, and the check-then-write in `logView` runs inside one Dexie transaction. — Without the transaction, two calls arriving together (React re-invoking the effect in development, or a double-tap) both read "no view yet" and both write one — the exact inflated count the window exists to prevent. Found in the browser, now pinned by a test.
- 2026-07-31 — `logEvent` takes the timestamp as an argument rather than reading the clock itself. — `logView` compares elapsed time against an injected clock; if the write then stamped a different clock, the two disagreed. A test caught it.
- 2026-07-31 — Repaso derives every number at render time from the event log; nothing is cached or counted incrementally. — §7 makes the log the single source of truth. A personal notebook is small enough that recomputing is free, and one code path means the screen can never disagree with the log.
- 2026-07-31 — Events belonging to deleted items stay in the log but are excluded from stats by filtering against the surviving item keys. — §7: keep the history, exclude it from active queues and statistics.
- 2026-07-31 — Repaso shows a "searched for, not found" list from `search_miss` events already in Phase 1d, ahead of its Phase 4 slot. — The events were being logged from 1c onward; surfacing them cost a few lines and made the log visible to the owner rather than invisible until Phase 4.

- 2026-07-31 — Phase 2 scope note: generating conjugations for verbs Jehle lacks requires orthographic rules (`-gar`/`-car`/`-zar`, stem changes), not just endings — `madrugar` → `madrugué`. — Recorded now so it is not discovered mid-Phase-2. (Superseded in Phase 2b: extraction from kaikki removed the need to write a generator at all.)

## Phase 2a — pipeline at full scale (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — The build pipeline lives in `pipeline/build/` with shared code in `pipeline/lib/`; `pipeline/spike/` is left untouched as the historical record. Raw downloads moved from `pipeline/spike/raw/` to `pipeline/raw/`, manifest included. — Moving the manifest preserves the recorded download dates and sha256s, so `DATA_SOURCES.md` provenance stays truthful without re-downloading 150 MB. The spike scripts would re-download if ever re-run; their committed outputs are what matters as the record.
- 2026-07-31 — The pipeline imports `normalize` from `src/lib/normalize.js` instead of keeping its own copy. — The pipeline builds the index the app searches; two copies of the normalizer would eventually disagree about ñ, and the failure would look like "search is broken" rather than "two functions drifted".
- 2026-07-31 — Big intermediates are JSONL, not one JSON object (`raw/_forms-search.jsonl`, `_forms-exact.jsonl`). — At ~1.2 M rows the single-object form has to be parsed in full before the next script can do one lookup; streaming keeps each step's memory proportional to what it actually keeps.
- 2026-07-31 — Parts of speech excluded from the dictionary: `character`, `punct`, `symbol`, `prefix`, `suffix`, `infix`, `interfix`, `romanization` (1,509 records). Proper nouns (`name`) are KEPT. — Affixes and single characters are not words a learner looks up; proper nouns are, and frequency ranking decides whether any earns a slot (exactly 0 did).
- 2026-07-31 — **Ambiguity policy refined to citation-form-first + proportional-to-evidence.** Ambiguous *inflected* tokens are now split in proportion to each candidate's score from *unambiguous* tokens, instead of evenly. — Full scale exposed what the spike's 186-record sample could not: rare words riding common ones' inflections. `podar` (to prune) reached rank 321 on *poder*'s "podamos", archaic `seer` reached 241 on *ser*'s "sea", and the neologisms `amigx`/`chicx`/`niñe` reached the top 700 on amigo/chico/niño's plurals. Proportional allocation drops them to 6468 / 4984 / 33155 while *raising* real verbs whose infinitive is rare (preocupar 269→257, soler 766→677) — their evidence comes from unambiguous forms like "preocupa".
- 2026-07-31 — Citation-form ambiguity still splits EVENLY; only inflected ambiguity is evidence-weighted. — Weighting it is actively wrong: invariable words have no inflections, so a conjunction's evidence score is structurally zero. Weighting citation forms sent `pero` (the conjunction, one of the commonest words in Spanish) from rank 52 to 5484, because `pero` the noun earns evidence from its plural "peros". Same failure for `adiós`. Evidence-weighting can answer "which lemma does this inflected form belong to"; it cannot answer "which part of speech did the speaker mean".
- 2026-07-31 — Only lemmas with a real kaikki lemma record are ranked (`raw/_lemma-keys.json` from step 02). — Inflection stubs sometimes name a target that has no record of its own; the English `your` arrived that way and reached rank 74, silently spending a top-10k slot on a lemma that can never become an entry. Removing phantoms dropped the ranked list from 70,850 to 61,236.
- 2026-07-31 — Senses carry a `labels[]` array (colloquial, slang, vulgar, reflexive, figuratively…) alongside `regionLabels[]`. — A learner needs to know that *güey* is vulgar slang and that a verb is reflexive; this is the same "adapt details, keep the shape" latitude §7 allows. kaikki's remaining tag vocabulary is inflection bookkeeping and stays out of the shipped data.
- 2026-07-31 — Unmarked senses sort before Spain-only senses, after Mexico and Latin America. — §3 asks for Mexico first; an unlabelled sense is general Spanish and is more useful to this owner than a Spain-only one.
- 2026-07-31 — Examples are chosen by score (citation form present, length near 45 characters) rather than "shortest wins" as in the spike. — Shortest-wins favours fragments; a sentence showing the word in its dictionary form teaches more. 83.5% of the 28,210 attached examples show the citation form; coverage is 95.9% of entries.

## Phase 2b — conjugations (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — **Conjugations for the 1,253 verbs Jehle lacks are EXTRACTED from kaikki's tagged `forms[]`, not generated from orthographic rules.** — Every Spanish verb record in kaikki already carries a fully tagged conjugation table written by Wiktionary editors who handled the stem changes, the `-gar`/`-car`/`-zar` spelling rules and every irregular. Extraction inherits all of that; the rule-based generator scoped in the spike report would have had to re-earn it. Verb coverage went from 30.7% to **99.3%** (1,795 of 1,807).
- 2026-07-31 — The extractor is validated cell by cell against Jehle on the 554 verbs both sources cover: **99.83% agreement across 57,580 cells**, gate was 99.5%. — This is the argument for trusting extraction, and no hand-written conjugator could have offered it. `pipeline/build/06-conjugate.mjs` exits non-zero below the gate, so a future dataset refresh cannot silently degrade.
- 2026-07-31 — Where the two sources disagree, **kaikki is the more accurate one**. — `criáis`→`criais` and `frió`→`frio` are the 2010 RAE spelling reform (Jehle predates it); `gradúéis` in Jehle carries two accents, which Spanish orthography does not allow; `arrepentáis` should be `arrepintáis`. Recorded because it bears on the licensing question below.
- 2026-07-31 — Negative imperatives extracted from kaikki are prefixed with "no " to match Jehle's convention. — kaikki stores the bare subjunctive ("abandones"); the negative imperative does not exist without its "no", and normalizing is what made the two sources comparable at all (it accounted for 2,729 of the original 2,746 disagreements).
- 2026-07-31 — Voseo forms (`vos-form`, `with-vos`) are skipped rather than written into the tú cell. — This is a Mexico-leaning notebook (§3) and vos would silently replace a form the owner will actually hear with one they will not.
- 2026-07-31 — The eight perfect tenses are composed as haber + past participle for **both** sources, rather than using Jehle's stored compound rows. — They are mechanically derivable, the composition agrees with Jehle on 99.8% of cells, and some Jehle rows are corrupt: `cepillar`'s past perfect reads "cepillía cepillado" where it should read "había cepillado". One code path means neither source can ship a mangled auxiliary.
- 2026-07-31 — Reflexive verbs put the pronoun before the auxiliary ("me he arrepentido"), detected from the verb's own conjugated forms rather than from a `-se` ending. — Spelling is not the signal: *coser* ends in -se and is not reflexive. Found by validating the composed perfects against Jehle, which is the reason perfects are validated at all.
- 2026-07-31 — Jehle cells that are empty or malformed are repaired from the kaikki table (158 cells). — `doler`'s negative imperatives are the literal string "no " with no verb after it. This repairs a defect; it does not change which source is primary.
- 2026-07-31 — Jehle remains the primary source for the 554 verbs it covers, as brief §4 specifies. — **Open question for the owner:** since kaikki matches Jehle to 99.83% and is more accurate where they differ, Jehle is now technically redundant. Dropping it would remove the CC BY-NC-SA **noncommercial** restriction from the entire dataset — the one licence the spike report flagged as constraining "what this app may ever become". Not acted on, because §4 locks Jehle in and this is a licensing decision, not a technical one.
- 2026-07-31 — 12 verbs still have no conjugation table (`había`, `persona`, `escucho`, `ponerle`, `hope`…). — These are kaikki records tagged `verb` that are not verb lemmas: inflected forms, a noun, and one English word. 0.1% of entries, each with a real gloss, so they are left rather than filtered by a rule that might catch genuine rare verbs.

## Phase 2c — packaging (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — The bundle ships as plain JSON chunks under `public/dict/<version>/`, committed to the repo and deployed with the app. — GitHub Pages gzips text over the wire, so 22.6 MB raw arrives as 3.3 MB with no custom compression code. Generating them in CI is impractical: the pipeline needs 150 MB of source downloads.
- 2026-07-31 — The form and English indexes are **sharded by first two letters** (326 + 330 shards) rather than stored one row per term. — Install time is dominated by IndexedDB writes: 656 shard rows land in seconds where 239,000 individual rows would take minutes, and a lookup still reads exactly one small row. The shard also doubles as a prefix bucket, which is what makes "saca" → *sacar* possible without a second index.
- 2026-07-31 — Three measured size cuts took the bundle from 4.0 MB to 3.3 MB, under the 3.5 MB budget: example licence + URL strings are constants stored once in the manifest (301 KB); `normalizedLemma` is not stored because the form index already maps it (208 KB); the eight perfect tenses are not stored because they are haber + participle without exception (322 KB). — Each was measured before being cut, not guessed. Every example still displays its ID, contributor, licence and link, which is what §4 requires the reader to see.
- 2026-07-31 — Perfect-tense composition lives in `src/lib/conjugation.js`, which the **pipeline imports**. — Same reasoning as `normalize`: the pipeline writes these tables and the app reads them, so one implementation and one test suite. The build validates the shared function against Jehle, so the app's rendering is covered by that 99.83% number too.
- 2026-07-31 — The per-cell `collapsed: true` flag is gone from conjugation data. — Which slots collapse is a display rule (§3) and belongs in the component; as data it was a constant repeated on ~106,000 cells.
- 2026-07-31 — `verify-package.mjs` round-trips the shipped chunks: verifies every sha256, reassembles the stores as the installer will, and answers the §12 searches from them. — Everything upstream tests the pipeline's intermediates; this tests what actually ships.

## Phase 2d — reference store and the §11 download (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — Reference data lives in **its own Dexie databases**, not in new tables of `mi-cuaderno`. — §5 says rebuilding the dictionary must never touch personal data. Separate databases make that structural rather than a rule to remember: no reference migration can see a personal record, and vice versa.
- 2026-07-31 — There are **two** reference databases, `mi-cuaderno-ref-a` and `-b`, with a localStorage pointer naming the live one. — §11 requires atomic version swaps and an interrupted download that leaves the previous version usable. An install writes into the inactive database; flipping one string is the entire commit, so there is no moment where the app reads a half-written dataset.
- 2026-07-31 — The active-slot pointer is localStorage, not IndexedDB. — `activeDb()` is called by every dictionary read, and making it async would infect every call site. Both desync directions degrade safely: a lost pointer offers a re-download, and a lost database reads as "not installed".
- 2026-07-31 — Each chunk's sha256 is verified before it is written, and each chunk is applied in one transaction. — A truncated download should fail loudly rather than become a dictionary with a hole in it that surfaces months later as a word mysteriously not being found.
- 2026-07-31 — Install progress is recorded per chunk, so an interrupted download resumes rather than restarting — but only when the pending install is the same `datasetVersion`. — Resuming into a slot holding a different version's rows would mix two datasets.
- 2026-07-31 — The service worker's `globIgnores` excludes `dict/**`, stated explicitly rather than relying on the default glob. — The dictionary is an explicit owner-initiated download that belongs in IndexedDB (§11); a Workbox default change must not silently start precaching 22 MB on first visit. Verified: the production build precaches 383 KB.
- 2026-07-31 — The About screen renders the §4 attribution from the **installed manifest**, not from a hard-coded list. — It can then never describe a different dataset than the one actually on the phone.
- 2026-07-31 — `localStorage` is polyfilled in `src/test/setup.js`. — The node test environment lacks it; polyfilling the environment is better than reshaping the app around the test runner.

## Phase 2e — two-layer search and dictionary UI (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — **A dictionary result whose entry is attached to a personal item is replaced by that item**, carrying the dictionary's match reason. — This is the seam (§5) paying off in both directions. It stops *sacar* appearing twice, once as the owner's word and once as the dictionary's; and it lets the reference layer find the owner's own words through inflections the personal layer cannot resolve — searching "madrugué" surfaces their note on *madrugar*, labelled "form of madrugar". A test written against the weaker behaviour (drop the duplicate, show nothing extra) is what exposed the missed opportunity.
- 2026-07-31 — Within one §8 tier, personal items rank above dictionary entries; tiers still decide first. — It is the owner's notebook, so their note on a word beats the dictionary's definition of it — but an exact dictionary match still outranks a personal page that merely mentions the word in its body, because tier 1 beats tier 6.
- 2026-07-31 — Prefix search matches only the **lemma**, not every form beginning with the query. — The shard is keyed on the first two letters, so it already holds every candidate and no second index is needed; but without the lemma filter, typing "sac" would bury *sacar* under its own conjugations. `saca` finds *sacar*; `saq` finds nothing, because "saque" is an inflection and not a word the owner was typing the start of.
- 2026-07-31 — English lookup **intersects** the postings of every query word rather than unioning them. — "take out" must find *sacar*, not everything meaning "take" or everything meaning "out". A gloss containing the whole phrase sorts above one containing the words scattered.
- 2026-07-31 — `search_miss` is logged against the **combined** result count, and suppressed while the dictionary query is in flight. — §7 wants "words I couldn't find". A word the dictionary answers is not one of them, and a slow IndexedDB read must not turn a found word into a miss on a slower phone. Verified in the browser: "chamba" logs nothing, "zzqxwv" logs exactly one.
- 2026-07-31 — Dictionary results are suppressed when the owner filters to *páginas* or to a tag. — Those filters mean "look through my own things"; reference words would be noise, and a tag filter cannot apply to an entry that has no tags.
- 2026-07-31 — Opening a dictionary entry logs a `view` event under its `dict:` key, through the same session window as a personal item. — §1 promises lookup history, and Phase 3's review queue will read it. `deriveItemState` already ignores events whose key is not a surviving personal item, so statistics were unaffected without changing them.
- 2026-07-31 — Dictionary search is debounced 140 ms and guarded against out-of-order replies. — The reference layer is asynchronous; without the guard a slow query for "sac" can overwrite the results for "sacar" typed after it.
- 2026-07-31 — Perfect tenses and vosotros are both collapsed behind toggles on the conjugation table. — §3 asks for vosotros collapsed; the perfect tenses are a further eighteen rows, and a learner wants the present before the future perfect subjunctive.

## Phase 2f — the seam, and acceptance (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — A `dictKey` that resolves only through the alias map is **rewritten on the item** the first time it is seen, without logging an `edit`. — §6 ships the alias map so a rebuild does not orphan personal items; rewriting means the item survives the *next* rebuild too, instead of depending on an alias chain that grows with every release. It is not an `edit` because the owner changed nothing — the dataset did.
- 2026-07-31 — "Not installed" and "orphaned" are different states, and only the second shows the §5 badge. — A warning about a dataset the owner never downloaded is noise they cannot act on. With no dictionary installed the attachment simply renders nothing.
- 2026-07-31 — An orphaned item offers both **Re-attach** and **Forget the link**. — §5 requires re-attachment; forgetting matters too, because a word that genuinely left the dictionary should be able to stop asking about it.
- 2026-07-31 — The owner can link a dictionary entry from any item, including pages. — §6 says `linkedKeys[]` may point at either layer, and §7 names page↔word explicitly; without a way to create one, the code that renders `dict:` links would have been unreachable. A grammar page linking to *ser* is the case that makes it worth having.
- 2026-07-31 — The §12 timing measurement **ships in the app** (Ajustes → Dictionary → "Test speed on this device") rather than being measured here and reported. — §12 asks for timing "measured on the owner's phone". A number from this development machine cannot answer that question, and instrumentation the owner can re-run after any future change is worth more than a number recorded once. Desktop baseline: app ready 80 ms, search 25 ms median / 64 ms slowest.
- 2026-07-31 — The speed test warms the cache with one throwaway query before timing. — The first dictionary query of a session pays for opening the database; including that cost would report a number the owner never actually experiences while typing.
- 2026-07-31 — `buildFixtureDictionary` gained `dropEntries` and `previousIds` options. — The orphan and alias paths (§5, §6) cannot be tested without simulating a rebuild that removes or moves an entry, and this is the seam the brief is most insistent about.
- 2026-07-31 — **GitHub Pages gzip confirmed on the live site**, closing the one assumption the Phase 2 plan flagged as unverified: chunk 0 is 1,809,305 bytes on disk and 546,540 on the wire, `content-encoding: gzip`. — This is what makes 22.6 MB of plain JSON cost 3.3 MB to download; the contingency (pre-gzipped `.gz` chunks plus `DecompressionStream`) is not needed. Full install over the network took 21 s.
- 2026-07-31 — Offline operation verified by breaking `fetch` outright rather than by trusting the service-worker config: "tuvimos" still resolves to *tener*. — §11's promise is that the dictionary works with no network, and the only convincing test of that is no network.
- 2026-07-31 — **The empty state names the dictionary, and offers to fetch it when this device lacks one.** — Found by the owner opening the app on their phone and seeing nothing. The dictionary is per-device by design (§11) and deliberately not browsable (§1 frames it as lookup, and 10,278 entries is a list nobody scrolls) — but those two together made it invisible: the screen said "add your first word" and gave no hint a dictionary existed, on a device that had not downloaded it. A failed search says the same thing, because that is the other moment the absence is felt.
- 2026-07-31 — The search placeholder changes to "Search the dictionary and your notes…" once a dictionary is installed. — The box is the only way into 10,278 words; it should say so, but only where it is true.

## Phase 2g — Jehle demoted, noncommercial restriction removed (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — **BRIEF AMENDMENT (owner's decision): §4 no longer names Jehle as the conjugation source.** Every shipped table is now extracted from kaikki; Jehle is a build-time validation reference whose content is never distributed. Dataset `kaikki-es-2026-07-25-r2`. — The whole distributed bundle is now CC BY-SA / CC BY, with **no noncommercial restriction** — the one licence the Phase 0.5 spike report flagged as constraining "what this app may ever become". The amendment is recorded inline in the brief with strikethrough so the original contract stays readable.
- 2026-07-31 — Decided on measurement, not preference: of the verbs Jehle covered, kaikki produces a table for **all 546, losing zero cells**. Coverage before and after is identical — 1,795 tables from 1,807 verbs. — The question "does dropping Jehle cost us anything" had a measurable answer, and it was no. The audit script counted 554 *entries* against 546 *lemmas*; the gap is verbs with two etymologies, not missing data.
- 2026-07-31 — Removing Jehle **improved** accuracy. `freír` preterite `frió`→`frio` and `criáis`→`criais` are the 2010 RAE spelling reform Jehle predates; `graduar` shipped `gradúéis` with two accents, which Spanish orthography does not permit; `arrepentirse` shipped `arrepentáis` instead of `arrepintáis`. — Jehle was primary for the verbs it covered, so those wrong forms were the ones on the phone. Keeping it was costing accuracy, not buying it.
- 2026-07-31 — Correcting an earlier overstatement: the 98 disagreeing cells were **not** all shipped differences. 61 are perfect tenses, which are composed from haber for both sources and so never shipped Jehle's version — including `cepillar`'s corrupt "cepillía cepillado". The real shipped delta is ~37 simple-tense cells across 19 verbs. — Same conclusion, smaller number; recorded because the larger one was used to argue the case.
- 2026-07-31 — Jehle's CSV is **optional** to the build: absent, the pipeline still produces a shippable dataset but prints that the tables are UNCHECKED, and the agreement gate is skipped rather than reported as a pass. — A build with no reference is not a passing build. The gate exiting non-zero only when a comparison actually happened keeps "unvalidated" distinguishable from "validated fine".
- 2026-07-31 — `sources.json` entries carry `distributed: false`; the manifest's attribution block and `DATA_SOURCES.md` both key off it. — The app's About screen must name the sources whose data the owner actually has. Listing Jehle there would credit them with data that is not on their phone, and DATA_SOURCES.md now states distributed-vs-build-time per source because that distinction is what decides which licences attach.
- 2026-07-31 — Two verifier checks were wrong and were replaced. `verbsComparedAgainstJehle` reported `jehleByVerb.size` (637 — just the database's size) rather than the number actually compared (546); and "the manifest states the noncommercial constraint" was a substring test for /NONCOMMERCIAL/i that **passed on a note saying there is no noncommercial restriction**. — The second is the worse bug: a check that cannot fail in the case it exists to catch. It is now an assertion that no distributed source carries an NC licence, plus one that no shipped table is Jehle-attributed.

## Phase 3a — the review schedule, derived (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — **Leitner box and due date are DERIVED from the review events, not stored.** `src/lib/review.js` replays each item's `review_pass`/`review_fail` history at render time, exactly as Repaso already derives views and tricky state. — §7 makes the log the single source of truth: "no running counters, and no stored state flags". A box number *is* the review history replayed, so storing it would create the first place in this app where a stored value and the log could disagree. §7 already requires the full grade history to be logged so a better scheduler can be fitted later — which only works if the history suffices to recompute everything, making a stored box a cache rather than a source.
- 2026-07-31 — **Consequence: no schema change.** `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1, so §5's migration plan and export-first reminder are not triggered. — Review events are ordinary events in a table backup already exports wholesale, and `metadata` already round-trips (`search_miss` has carried a query string since Phase 1c). Verified rather than assumed: a new backup test pushes review grades through JSON text and back, because if grade metadata were dropped, a restore would silently reset the owner's whole schedule.
- 2026-07-31 — Intervals are 1/2/4/8/16 days across five boxes, as named constants. — Classic doubling: a word just missed returns tomorrow, one passed five times waits over two weeks. §14 defers anything beyond Leitner, so nothing here tries to be cleverer.
- 2026-07-31 — Enrollment by lookups counts **distinct localDates**, not raw views: 3 days inside a trailing 30. — Returning to a word on three separate days is evidence it has not stuck; three visits in one sitting is one sitting. The 30-minute session window already thins those, but a day is the honest unit, and §7 makes localDate the unit daily queues group by. A test asserts three same-day views do *not* enroll.
- 2026-07-31 — **Views logged under an item's attached `dictKey` count as lookups of that item.** — The §5 seam applied to events, and the same move Phase 2e made in search, where a dictionary result whose entry is attached becomes the owner's own item. It is what lets a word the owner kept looking up before writing it down arrive in the queue already warm. Scoped to enrollment only: the lookup counts *displayed* on Detail and Repaso are untouched, keeping the change surgical.
- 2026-07-31 — Passing from the top box **graduates** a word out of the queue; it re-enters through the ordinary rules and resumes at box 5. Lookups counted towards re-entry start at the graduation, not before. — The queue should be the words currently giving trouble, not a tax that only grows. The cut-off matters because the trips to the page that the ladder just answered should not immediately re-enroll the word; a test with all three lookups inside the window, discriminating only on the cut-off, pins it.
- 2026-07-31 — Exception: while the tricky flag is on, a graduated word stays in rotation at box 5's 16-day cadence. — The owner's explicit highlighter outranks the scheduler until they take it off.
- 2026-07-31 — A failed word returns **tomorrow**, not later in the same session. — Box 1's honest cadence. Re-showing it minutes later tests short-term memory, which is the thing spacing exists to defeat, and §14 says no cleverness beyond Leitner.
- 2026-07-31 — A reviewed word leaves today's queue by date arithmetic alone; there is no "already reviewed today" list. — One mechanism, and no second structure that could fall out of step with the log.
- 2026-07-31 — Pages are excluded from the queue even when flagged tricky. — A page has no answer side to test yourself against, and §12 says *words*. Tricky pages keep their place in Repaso's existing tricky list; reviewing pages is Phase 4 material if the owner ever wants it.
- 2026-07-31 — `logReview(itemKey, passed, when)` is the only writer of review events, mapping pass→2 (good) and fail→0 (again) on §7's locked 4-point scale, with `GRADES` exported as named constants. — One writer means no screen can log a review without a grade, which is one third of §12's "done when".
- 2026-07-31 — `addDaysToLocalDate` does calendar arithmetic on date components at noon, not milliseconds. — A day must be a day across a daylight-saving change, and noon keeps zones that shift at midnight from landing on the wrong side of it.
- 2026-07-31 — `src/lib/review.js` is pure and takes `today` as an argument; it imports no database. — Same reasoning as `logEvent` taking its timestamp in Phase 1d: two clocks in one calculation eventually disagree. It also lets the tests stand at any date, which is the only way to test a multi-week schedule.

## Phase 3b — the session, and Para hoy (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — The review flow lives inside Repaso; there is no fourth tab. A "Para hoy" section sits above the existing stats, and starting a session swaps Repaso's own content for `ReviewSession`, the same way Cuaderno swaps its list for `Detail`. — *Repaso* means review; the tab was named for this, and the daily queue is the action the stats below it are a reflection on. The tab bar stays three-wide until Phase 5 adds Asistente (Phase 1a decision).
- 2026-07-31 — A session snapshots its card list (`review.due`, merged with each card's derived state) at the moment it starts, rather than reading `review.due` live. — Grading a card triggers `reload()`, which re-derives the whole schedule; without the snapshot the just-graded word's disappearance from `due` would also yank the *next* card out from under the owner mid-session, or reorder the deck under their thumb.
- 2026-07-31 — Grading writes through `logReview` alone, in a click handler, and the buttons disable between the tap and the advance. — One writer is what guarantees every review event carries a grade (brief §12). A click handler rather than an effect means Phase 1d's StrictMode double-fire hazard (which is why `logView` needed a transaction) does not apply here: nothing re-fires a click.
- 2026-07-31 — A failed word's summary line says "N to see again tomorrow" rather than offering to retry it in the same session. — Matches the derivation: a fail sends a word to box 1, due tomorrow (§14, no cleverness beyond Leitner), so re-offering it minutes later inside the UI would say something the schedule does not mean.
- 2026-07-31 — Verified in the browser against the dev server, not just tests: seeded a tricky word via IndexedDB directly (no dictionary needed), watched it appear in Para hoy due today, ran the session, revealed the card, graded it, and confirmed in IndexedDB that `review_pass` carries `{ grade: 2 }` with the correct `localDate` — then confirmed Para hoy read "Nothing left for today" and the activity feed showed "Reviewed madrugar" using the label Phase 1d had already written and left unused.

## Phase 3c — the dictionary rail (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — `newLexicalFromEntry(entry)` is extracted from `DictDetail.addToCuaderno` into `src/db/items.js` as a pure builder (term, first gloss, mapped pos, `dictKey` — no side effect, no reference-layer import). Both the entry's own "Add to my cuaderno" button and the new Repaso rail call it. — One function means the two places a word enters the cuaderno from the dictionary can never drift into carrying different fields across.
- 2026-07-31 — The rail lists entries the owner opened 3+ times in 30 days that are not yet attached or linked (`deriveDictSuggestions`, §14 threshold, not the review 3-distinct-*day* rule — a nudge, not an enrollment decision) and resolves each through the existing `getEntries`, dropping any key that fails to resolve. — Section 5's orphan handling again: a suggestion pointing at a dataset key that no longer resolves has nothing to act on, so it silently doesn't render, the same as `DictAttachment` does for an orphaned attachment.
- 2026-07-31 — Verified in the browser with a fixture reference entry written directly into `mi-cuaderno-ref-a` (no full dataset download needed for this check): the rail showed "You keep looking up **chamba** ×3", Add created a personal item carrying the dictionary's gloss, and the Detail screen confirmed the attachment. Also verified that three lookups logged at the same instant do **not** enroll the new item for review (0 distinct days), which is the review rule doing exactly what its test asserts — the suggestion rail's raw-view threshold and the review ladder's distinct-day threshold are deliberately different measures, and this is what tells them apart in practice, not just in a unit test.

## Phase 4 — scope (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — **Owner's decision: Phase 4's core scope is the linking package recorded in `docs/PHASE-4-DIRECTION.md`**, within the existing item-level `linkedKeys[]` architecture. — §12 leaves Phase 4 to the owner's friction list and names "richer linking" as a candidate; the owner chose it and wrote the requirements. The friction list is still to be collected before planning and may add or reorder work. No brief amendment needed: nothing in the package conflicts with the contract.
- 2026-07-31 — **The friction list was deferred by the owner; Phase 4 proceeds on the linking package alone.** — §12 gates the phase on friction collected in daily use, and the direction file asks for it before planning. The owner chose to keep this phase's scope to the seven requirements and address friction later, which is their call to make; nothing in the package depends on it.
- 2026-07-31 — **The package was trimmed from eight requirements to seven before planning, and the earlier note that requirement 6 "may force the project's first schema field" no longer applies.** Grouping now uses only what the data already knows — lexical items, pages, and journal entries (a dated page, §7) — and the "sources" category is deferred until real use asks for it, because nothing distinguishes a film page from a grammar page. — Each cut removed machinery or dissolved a design question rather than trading away function: the "linking mode" on global search went (it was the package's only new UI state machinery, and raised "link this to *what*?"), replaced by a cross-cutting criterion that linking never navigates away; requirement 8's "source pages show their vocabulary" became *every* page, which is what made deferring the source concept free; tag matching left the link picker and tags left the link cards (a picker finds one specific item, where tag matches are noise); user-facing sort controls went in favour of one fixed order; and backlinks were reworded from "build" to "verify and present", since Phase 1c already derives them. The phase is now expected to need no schema change at all, and a planning session that concludes otherwise must raise it rather than proceed.

## Phase 4a — one derivation for both directions (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — **"Both directions" had two implementations; the phase ends with one.** `Detail` filtered the in-memory items array while `relatedItems`/`backlinksFor` in `src/db/items.js` did the same job against the database and were called only by tests. `relatedItems` is deleted and `src/lib/links.js` `relatedTo` is the single derivation. — Requirement 3 is "verify and present", not rebuild. Two correct implementations of one rule is one more than can be kept correct; the pure one wins because every screen already holds the whole notebook in memory (`useNotebook`), and a pure function is testable without a database — the same argument `src/lib/review.js` settled in Phase 3a.
- 2026-07-31 — `backlinksFor` **stays**, though no screen calls it. — It is the indexed reverse lookup (`*linkedKeys` multi-entry index) that `deleteItem` cleans up with, and the test that proves the Phase 1c mechanism — links stored on one side, reverse derived — needs a database to prove it against.

## Phase 4b — the link picker (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — **The picker matches term, title and translation only — never tags, notes or page bodies.** — A picker answers "find the one item I mean", where a search screen answers "show me everything about this". Tag matches are noise in the first: typing "verb" would offer every item tagged `verbs`. Pinned by a test asserting a tag match does *not* surface. The Cuaderno screen's tag-filter chips are a different feature and are untouched.
- 2026-07-31 — Picker ranking **reuses the §8 tier numbers**, so personal and dictionary matches interleave through the same `mergeResults` the search screen uses. — Two consequences worth having: an exact dictionary match can outrank a personal item that merely contains the string, and a dictionary entry already *attached* to one of the owner's items is replaced by that item (Phase 2e), so *sacar* is offered once rather than twice.
- 2026-07-31 — Already-linked items are **shown and marked, not hidden**. — "linked ✓" answers "have I already done this?"; a row that silently vanishes looks like the search failed. The old `<select>` hid them, which is why the question kept coming up.
- 2026-07-31 — Only the dictionary half of the picker is debounced (140 ms, with the out-of-order guard). — Personal matching is synchronous over items already in memory, so it can land on every keystroke; debouncing it would add lag for nothing. The reference layer reads IndexedDB, where a slow query for "sac" must never overwrite results for "sacar" typed after it (Phase 2e).

## Phase 4c — quick-create-and-link (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — Quick-create **must not navigate**, where `AddSheet` deliberately does. — `Detail` resets its body draft only when `item.id` changes, so staying on the item is the entire mechanism that keeps unsaved work alive. This is the phase's cross-cutting acceptance criterion, and it is a property of the component, not the database.
- 2026-07-31 — **The project's first component tests**, with `jsdom` + `@testing-library` as dev dependencies; the node environment stays the default and component tests opt in per file. — No database test can assert "the draft survived and nothing navigated". Per-file opt-in means the other 201 tests do not pay for a document they never touch.
- 2026-07-31 — **The first version of that test passed even with a deliberate `onOpen()` added to the handler.** It waited for the new row to appear in the database, which the first of three awaits satisfies, and raced past the rest. It now waits for the link to appear *on screen*, and was re-checked by reintroducing the navigation. — Same lesson as the Phase 2g verifier bug: a check that cannot fail in the case it exists to catch is worse than no check, because it is also reassuring.
- 2026-07-31 — A typed query containing a space becomes a `phrase`, not a `word`. — §7 makes both first-class, and "de repente" is not a word.

## Phase 4d — grouping, cards, and the link seam (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — Groups are **palabras / páginas / diario**, derived from what the data already knows: a dated page *is* a journal entry (§7), so `pageDate` is the whole test. — No field was invented and `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1. There is deliberately no "sources" group: nothing distinguishes a film page from a grammar page, so it would cost either a tag convention or the project's first schema field.
- 2026-07-31 — Linked **dictionary entries join palabras**, after the owner's own words, ordered alphabetically. — A linked dictionary word is still a word, so a fourth group would be an implementation detail leaking into the interface; they sort last within the group because they have no `updatedAt`, the owner never having edited them.
- 2026-07-31 — Link cards **show no tags**, though they show translation, date, preview and dictionary status. — Requirement 4's explicit call: on a phone the space is better spent, and tags are the least useful field for "is this the one I meant".
- 2026-07-31 — **Linked `dict:` keys now resolve through the alias map and report what they cannot find** (`src/db/linkedEntries.js`), where before they were bulk-fetched and silently dropped. A resolved alias rewrites the key on the item without logging an `edit`. — This was a real §5 hole, not a new feature: a rebuild that *renamed* an entry lost the link even though `previousIds` knew the new id. Rewriting means the link survives the next rebuild too rather than depending on a growing alias chain (the Phase 2f argument, applied to links instead of attachments). Not an `edit` because the owner changed nothing — the dataset did.
- 2026-07-31 — An unresolvable linked key is **shown as an orphan card offering "forget this link"**, not dropped. — The Repaso rail silently drops a *suggestion*, which is right for something the app proposed; this is a link the owner deliberately made, and a link that vanishes without a word is data loss they cannot see. Deleting it stays their decision. With no dictionary installed nothing is shown at all — "not installed" is not "orphaned" (Phase 2f).

## Phase 4e — per-context views (2026-07-31)

- 2026-07-31 — Each screen **leads with the group it is for**: a page leads with palabras, a word or dictionary entry with páginas. Fixed orders, derived at render, no sort controls. — Requirement 6 without a new concept: every page becomes its own vocabulary sheet, which is what made deferring the "source" page type free. Sort controls are desktop thinking for a list of five links.
- 2026-07-31 — `DictDetail`'s cards carry **no unlink ×**. — A dictionary entry is read-only (§5) and stores no link; the × belongs on the item that actually holds it, and offering it here would edit a different record than the screen the owner is looking at.
- 2026-07-31 — **Requirement 7 (exact term and title link suggestions) is deferred**, as the direction file allows. — It is the package's largest single piece of new logic — match detection, longer-phrase preference, common-term noise suppression, an approval UI — and requirements 1–6 ship without it. It is best decided alongside the deferred friction list, once the rest has had real use. If built, it inherits `linkItems` and so logs no `edit` event, unchanged.

## Phase 4f — tag suggestions (2026-08-01)

- 2026-08-01 — **The owner's friction list opened, and its items take precedence over requirement 7.** — §12 defines Phase 4 as polish driven by friction collected in daily use; requirement 7 is planned work, and the three friction items are observed work. Two of the three are not about linking at all, which is itself evidence that link suggestions are not the friction being felt.
- 2026-08-01 — **Tag suggestions match case- and accent-insensitively, through the shared `normalize`.** — This is what actually fixes the reported problem. `cleanTags` dedupes exact strings only, so `expression`, `Expression` and `expresión` are three different tags today; matching loosely is what surfaces the existing spelling and gets it reused instead of forked. ñ stays distinct, like everywhere else.
- 2026-08-01 — **Suggest, never rewrite:** what gets stored is exactly what the owner typed or tapped, even when a near-identical tag exists. — Silently folding their spelling into an existing tag is the kind of helpfulness that annoys the third time it happens; offering the alternative one tap away achieves the same convergence without overruling them.
- 2026-08-01 — One `TagInput` component serves **both** places tags are entered, retiring the add sheet's comma-separated field. — The friction is not fixed by fixing one of two tag interfaces, and comma-separated entry was the more unforgiving of them. `AddSheet` now takes `items` so it can derive the same vocabulary.
- 2026-08-01 — Adding a tag still logs an `edit` event, unlike linking. — Tags are content the owner changed; the Phase 1c distinction stands.
- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the tag editor collapses its idle “used before” suggestions into one counted disclosure; opening it keeps the choices on one horizontally scrollable line, while typing opens matching tags automatically.** — The always-visible six-chip list wrapped into a second row on a phone and made a small editor feel tall. Automatic matches preserve the original duplicate-prevention purpose, and contained horizontal overflow keeps the compact row from creating page-level overflow.

## Phase 4g — palabras and frases as separate tabs (2026-08-01)

- 2026-08-01 — **The Cuaderno type filter splits into todo / palabras / frases / páginas**, matching on `form` as well as `type` (`src/lib/filters.js`). — The word/phrase toggle has existed since Phase 1b and is already shown as *loc.*, but the filter compared `type` alone, so the choice made at creation had no visible effect. **This is not a third content type** (§7 forbids one without an amendment): it surfaces a distinction the schema has always carried. No migration, no schema change.
- 2026-08-01 — **Dictionary results are suppressed under the *frases* filter**, joining *páginas* and tag filters. — The bundled dictionary is lemma-focused (§1), so a phrase filter would fill with single-word lemmas. Same reasoning Phase 2e used for *páginas*: that filter means "look through my own things".
- 2026-08-01 — A lexical item with no `form` at all counts as a **word**, not as neither. — `newLexical` normalizes the field, but an item restored from an old or hand-edited backup must land in some tab rather than disappear from every one of them.
- 2026-08-01 — **The header count splits three ways**, and gained Spanish pluralization (only 1 takes the singular). — A single "palabras" total silently including phrases stopped being true the moment they got their own tab. The plural was already wrong before ("1 palabras"), but a phrase count will often read exactly 1, which is where it shows — and this is a notebook for learning Spanish.
- 2026-08-01 — `AddSheet`'s word/phrase toggle **follows the term until the owner touches it**: a space in the term starts it as a phrase. — This is what quick-create-and-link already infers (Phase 4c), so the two entry points agreed on the rule but not on when to apply it. Still fully overridable, so "buenos días" can be filed as a word if that is what is meant.
- 2026-08-01 — **Link grouping deliberately does NOT split the same way.** `GROUPS` in `src/lib/links.js` keeps phrases inside *palabras*. — Requirement 5 settled three groups, and a list of five links does not need four headings. The tab split earns its keep on a list that can run to hundreds of items; the link list cannot.

## Phase 4h — multi-line meanings (2026-08-01)

- 2026-08-01 — **A meaning can hold several lines, stored as newlines inside the existing `translation` string.** Both editors become textareas; nothing else about the data changes. — `translation` was always a plain string, so newlines already survived storage, backup and import; the only thing stopping them was `<input>`, which structurally cannot hold a line break. This also makes `translation` consistent with `notes` and page `body`, which have been textareas rendered with `whitespace-pre-wrap` since Phase 1b.
- 2026-08-01 — **Explicitly NOT a `translations[]` array.** — That would have been the project's first schema change, triggering §5 in full (migration plan, export-first reminder, `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, backup validation), and it edges into the sense-level annotation §7 defers. Newlines give the owner the same thing on screen for none of that, and parse into structure later if it is ever wanted.
- 2026-08-01 — Applies to **all lexical items, not just phrases**. — *saco* is "jacket, sack": words have several readings too, and restricting by `form` would have added a conditional that buys nothing.
- 2026-08-01 — Rendering splits by what the screen is for: shown **in full** on the detail screen and the review card, **clamped to two lines** on list and link cards, **flattened to one line** in the link picker. — The review card is the answer side, so clipping it would hide the thing being tested; a list row, by contrast, must stay a row.
- 2026-08-01 — Whitespace is flattened **at the translation comparison in `src/lib/search.js`**, not in `normalize()`. — Searching "jacket sack" should find a meaning whose two readings sit on consecutive lines. `normalize()` could not absorb this: the pipeline imports it and it decides what the 10,278 shipped entries match, so collapsing whitespace there would quietly change the dictionary index.
- 2026-08-01 — A backup round-trip test pins the line breaks. — Same reasoning as the Phase 3a test for review grades: if a restore flattened them, the owner would silently lose the distinction between readings, and would find out long after the export that could have saved them.

## Housekeeping — CI actions (2026-08-01)

- 2026-08-01 — Deploy workflow actions bumped to current majors (`checkout` v4→v7, `setup-node` v4→v7, `configure-pages` v5→v6, `upload-pages-artifact` v3→v5, `deploy-pages` v4→v5), and CI Node 22→24. — GitHub deprecated the Node 20 runtime those action versions targeted and had begun force-running them on Node 24; the bump makes that explicit rather than depending on a compatibility shim that will eventually be withdrawn. Node 24 also matches the local toolchain (Phase 0), so CI builds what was tested. Every input the workflow passes was checked against the new versions first — `enablement` in particular, which the Phase 0 decision records as deliberately kept.
- 2026-08-01 — A failed deploy run leaves the previously published site serving. — Worth recording as the reason this was safe to push straight to `main`: GitHub Pages keeps the last successful deployment, so the blast radius of a bad workflow edit is a red run, not a dead app.

## Phase 4 — the friction list as a standing input (2026-08-01)

- 2026-08-01 — **`docs/PHASE-4-DIRECTION.md` amended inline: the friction list arrived and shipped as 4f–4h.** The superseded "still to be collected" note is struck through rather than deleted. — Same habit as the brief's §4 amendment: the original text stays readable, so the record shows what was directed when, not a rewritten version of it. The file is the owner's authored direction, so the update is marked and dated rather than folded in silently.
- 2026-08-01 — **The friction list is recorded as a standing input, not a one-off collection.** — §12 describes it as "a running list … collected while using the app daily"; treating it as a single gate to pass would have made it a one-time requirements exercise, which is the opposite of what the phase is for. Each future batch gets planned and shipped as its own small sub-phases, and no batch waits for the list to be "complete" — three real items produced three fixes, where a long speculative list would have produced a debate.
- 2026-08-01 — Recorded that the first list pointed **away** from linking (two of three items), and that this is the evidence keeping requirement 7 parked. — The strongest argument against building the largest planned piece of work turned out to be the owner's own use, not an estimate.

## Phase 5 — organizational improvements and 5a navigation (2026-08-02)

- 2026-08-02 — **OWNER'S DECISION / BRIEF AMENDMENT: organizational improvements become Phase 5 and the AI assistant moves to Phase 6; Phase 4 remains an independent friction-driven maintenance stream.** — The owner explicitly allowed Phase 5 to begin while Phase 4 remains open. Brief §12's coordination amendment keeps that understandable: Phase 4 reacts to observed daily-use friction, Phase 5 follows its planned organizational sequence, and overlapping files or behaviours are sequenced.
- 2026-08-02 — **This supersedes the Phase 1a/3a timing notes that said Asistente would join the tab bar in Phase 5: any assistant UI now belongs to Phase 6.** — The historical entries remain intact because this log is append-only; this line makes their phase number unambiguously obsolete without deciding the future tab layout early.
- 2026-08-02 — **Phase 5 is limited to schema-v1 improvements that do not need the owner's real notebook: navigation, derived retrieval views, tag/list control, activity navigation, scan-first detail presentation and duplicate guardrails.** — Permanent source kinds, structured senses, typed links, provenance and stored taxonomies still require real-data evidence and migration planning, so they remain deferred.
- 2026-08-02 — **5a keeps a session-only detail trail in `App`; a related-item hop pushes its key, Atrás pops it, and leaving Cuaderno clears it.** — The current app already swaps list/detail from `App` state. Extending that state fixes source → word → back without adding a router, URLs, persistence or schema fields.
- 2026-08-02 — **Every tab or selected-detail change scrolls the document to the top.** — Detail screens share the window scroll container, so a long source page otherwise opens its linked target halfway down. Notebook reloads from views or edits do not trigger the reset because the route state did not change.
- 2026-08-02 — **Personal and dictionary detail components remount when the selected trail key changes.** — Example/media drafts and asynchronous link resolution belong to one destination. Reusing the component let an unsaved Spanish example from one word appear on the next and made it possible to save against the wrong item; the keyed boundary fixes that without changing stored data or remounting the same originating item during Phase 4 quick-create.
- 2026-08-02 — **A dictionary detail keeps its trail-aware back action during loading and for a missing installed entry.** — IndexedDB resolution is normally brief, but navigation cannot temporarily become a dead end, especially when the destination was reached from another detail.
- 2026-08-02 — **5b's browse orders, maintenance views and contextual tag counts live together in one pure, database-free module and are derived from items already in memory.** — These are different views of existing fields, not new notebook state. Nothing is cached or persisted, the event log remains authoritative, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1.
- 2026-08-02 — **Recently touched copies the current `allItems()` order; recently added sorts a copy by `createdAt`; A–Z sorts mixed lexical/page display headings with Spanish collation.** — `allItems()` already supplies `updatedAt` recency to both Cuaderno and the empty link picker, so re-sorting or mutating its shared array would weaken an existing contract. Untitled pages use the same fallback heading the interface shows. Search results never pass through these browse orders because brief §8 relevance stays authoritative.
- 2026-08-02 — **Maintenance-link status is derived over the complete personal notebook before any type filter.** — A page may be the only item pointing to a word, so hiding pages first would falsely call that word unlinked. Any stored `linkedKeys[]` value, including a dictionary key, counts as a link; a lexical item's optional `dictKey` remains the separate §5 reference attachment and does not.
- 2026-08-02 — **Contextual tag counts are derived after type/maintenance filtering but before the selected exact tag is applied, and count items rather than repeated occurrences.** — The chooser must not collapse to the active tag while it narrows the cards. Stored spelling remains identity, so no tag is normalized, merged or rewritten.
- 2026-08-02 — **5c fixes the Cuaderno retrieval pipeline as maintenance over the full notebook → type → contextual exact-tag counts → selected exact tag → search relevance or browse order.** — The sequence preserves 5b's cross-type backlink contract, keeps the tag chooser informative while it narrows cards, and leaves brief §8 relevance authoritative whenever a query exists.
- 2026-08-02 — **View, Order and Tag use native selects; View/Order share one row and Tag uses the full row.** — Native controls give keyboard and mobile behavior without a custom menu, while the layout and bounded labels passed the required 375 px long-tag check.
- 2026-08-02 — **A selected tag clears only when the current personal item/type/maintenance context makes that exact stored tag impossible; search text does not clear it or change its count.** — A stale invisible filter should not create an unexplained empty list, but search is a temporary narrowing layer rather than a new organizational context.
- 2026-08-02 — **Every maintenance view is personal-only and gates both pending and already-resolved dictionary results, including dictionary-oriented empty-state prompts.** — Missing meaning, missing examples and no links describe editable personal records; reference rows cannot satisfy those maintenance jobs, and an asynchronous result must not leak in after the view changes.
- 2026-08-02 — **5c control state remains local to the mounted Cuaderno component.** — The choices survive detail/back transitions and notebook reloads within the tab, but leaving Cuaderno resets them; no preference, backup, Dexie store or schema change is justified for these first-use controls.
- 2026-08-02 — **5d makes recent activity actionable only when its destination still exists: live personal items and dictionary entries resolved through the alias map reopen through `App`'s existing selection path; deleted items, search misses, installed orphans and references on a device without the dictionary stay static.** — That gives the owner a direct route back to useful history without turning an unavailable destination into a broken control or adding a second navigation mechanism.
- 2026-08-02 — **Resolving an old dictionary activity key navigates to the current canonical key but never rewrites the historical event.** — The event log is append-only and records what was opened at the time; unlike a live `dictKey` or `linkedKeys[]` relationship, historical evidence does not need rewriting to survive the next rebuild.
- 2026-08-02 — **Unknown event types are filtered before the 12-row activity limit, and mixed personal-item UI says “opened,” “opens,” “Most opened” and “Highlighted items.”** — Brief §7 requires consumers to ignore unknown types, so a future event cannot expose an internal name or crowd out known history. Pages can be opened and highlighted but are not “looked up” or words; genuine lexical-only review and dictionary wording stays specific.
- 2026-08-02 — **5e renders saved notes and page bodies as whitespace-preserving reading text; Edit or the compact empty-content action opens the existing explicit-save editor, while Cancel restores the persisted value.** — A detail is opened far more often to read than to edit. Keeping Save as the only write preserves Phase 1b's event contract, and a compact empty state makes sparse entries scannable without transforming whitespace stored by the owner.
- 2026-08-02 — **Personal-example and media-link composers are closed disclosures by default; invalid submissions stay open, while Cancel and successful Add clear and collapse them.** — Existing rows remain immediately readable, but blank optional forms no longer make every detail look unfinished. The existing required-Spanish and `http(s)` validation, trimming, array shapes and one-edit-per-add behavior remain unchanged.
- 2026-08-02 — **5e's edit and disclosure state is local to one mounted `Detail`: same-item reloads preserve an unsaved draft, and an item-key change resets it.** — This retains Phase 4 quick-create-and-link draft preservation and Phase 5a destination isolation without persistence, schema fields or a second state owner.
- 2026-08-02 — **5f duplicate comparison is personal-only, NFC-normalized, whitespace-cleaned and Spanish case-insensitive, but accent- and punctuation-sensitive.** — The warning should catch accidental casing and spacing variants without collapsing real Spanish distinctions such as `si`/`sí`, `el`/`él` and `tu`/`tú`. Word and phrase headings share a lexical group; page titles compare only with pages.
- 2026-08-02 — **Duplicate matches are advisory status messages, never validation errors, and dictionary results are independent of personal creation.** — Homographs are legitimate notebook entries, while a reference match says nothing about whether the owner needs a personal note. Add Sheet and quick-create therefore keep their creation actions enabled and visible in every warned or dictionary-only state.
- 2026-08-02 — **Personal lexical UI spells out `word` and `phrase`; dictionary part-of-speech abbreviations remain untouched.** — The owner should see one vocabulary for their own records across creation, pickers, cards, detail and review, without pretending the dictionary's grammatical labels are personal item types.
- 2026-08-02 — **5f remains a schema-v1 derivation over items already loaded in memory.** — Duplicate feedback needs no persisted index or uniqueness constraint; adding either would turn a warning into data authority and create migration work with no benefit.
- 2026-08-02 — **OWNER'S CLOSEOUT DECISION: Phase 5 is complete through 5f and enters real-use observation; it will be revisited only after the owner tests the deployed site.** — The approved organizational sequence has shipped, so extending it now would replace evidence with speculation. Regressions and ordinary live-use friction continue through Phase 4; any later Phase 5 slice needs a concrete observation, its own plan and the same independent verification.

## Tooling — CLAUDE.md (2026-08-02)

- 2026-08-02 — **Added `CLAUDE.md`.** — Claude Code auto-loads it; `AGENTS.md` (written for Codex) was never loaded into a Claude Code session, so its context had to be pasted in by hand at the start of each one. The PATH gotcha in particular existed in no repo document at all.
- 2026-08-02 — ~~**`CLAUDE.md` deliberately states no phase status and points at `README.md` and the newest phase report instead.**~~ **Superseded later the same day:** the no-duplication principle remains, but `CLAUDE.md` now delegates the shared read order to `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md`. — Phase state changes every sub-phase, so an entry file that restates it is a copy that goes stale.
- 2026-08-02 — ~~`CLAUDE.md` is standalone and does **not** cross-reference `AGENTS.md`.~~ **Superseded later the same day by the shared-guide structure below.** — This records the first shape without presenting it as the current rule; both thin entry files now point directly to one shared source rather than chaining through each other.

## Tooling — one shared agent guide (2026-08-02)

- 2026-08-02 — **Agent instructions restructured: `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md` holds everything shared; `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` are thin tool-specific entry points that point at it.** — The owner works in both Claude Code and Codex. The previous shape had four rules stated in both files (drift waiting to happen — the 4a lesson applied to docs) and five things stated in only one (the PATH gotcha, the shared ñ normalizer, the fixture-seeding recipe, the 375 px check, plan-first), so each tool was missing part of the picture. One shared copy means a fix lands once and both tools read identical rules, at the cost of one extra file read per session.
- 2026-08-02 — What stays tool-specific: Plan Mode, PATH workarounds and the preview-tool dev server for Claude Code; the no-real-browser-data warning for Codex. The old `AGENTS.md` review-expectations section moved INTO the guide — it is good practice for either tool, not a Codex quirk. Its phase-status section was dropped for the same reason `CLAUDE.md` never had one: it needed an edit every sub-phase and duplicated `README.md`.

- 2026-08-02 — **`docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md` gained a git section for the shared working directory**: check `git status -sb` before committing and pushing, treat an unexpected "Everything up-to-date" as a signal, prefer `--ff-only`, and never delete or rebase the other tool's branches. — Found the hard way: a Codex-created branch was still checked out, two commits landed on it rather than `main`, and the push then reported success while doing nothing. Nothing was lost, but the failure is silent and both tools can cause it for each other, so it belongs in the shared guide rather than in one tool's file.

## Tooling — cross-agent documentation refresh (2026-08-02)

- 2026-08-02 — **The v3 brief now addresses both Claude Code and Codex, and its completed Phase 0/0.5 bootstrap instruction is visibly superseded.** — The brief predates both the application and the use of Codex; leaving a live instruction to plan already-finished phases directly contradicted `README.md`. The original remains struck through as history, and no product requirement changed.
- 2026-08-02 — **The shared guide now uses a task-sensitive read order.** Every task starts from `README.md`, its linked current report and the relevant implementation; agents load the governing brief sections, matching decisions and the visual prototype according to the work, with the whole brief still required for a new phase or cross-cutting contract change. — The old unconditional path loaded about 148 KB before source inspection and grew every time `DECISIONS.md` did, crowding both tools' working context with unrelated history.
- 2026-08-02 — **`CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` contain only tool-specific execution notes; shared status, decision-log and commit rules live once in `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md`.** — This makes the file shape match its own stated rule and removes the remaining copies that could drift.
- 2026-08-02 — **Codex's entry point records the PowerShell spellings `npm.cmd test` / `npm.cmd run build` / `npm.cmd run dev`, plus explicit UTF-8 reading.** — Reproduced on this machine: plain `npm test` resolves to blocked `npm.ps1`; `npm.cmd test` runs all 240 tests, and default PowerShell 5.1 `Get-Content` garbles the repository's UTF-8 punctuation without `-Encoding UTF8`.
- 2026-08-02 — **The ignored Claude preview configuration has a tracked template at `docs/claude-launch.example.json`.** — `.claude/launch.json` is correctly machine-local, but a fresh clone needs a reproducible way to restore `mi-cuaderno-dev`; the template preserves that without committing machine state.

## Phase sequencing — concurrent independent work (2026-08-02)

- 2026-08-02 — **BRIEF AMENDMENT (owner's decision): independently scoped phases may proceed concurrently when their boundaries, dependencies and integration order are documented first.** Parallel work uses separate branches or worktrees, and overlapping files or behaviours are sequenced rather than edited independently at the same time. — The owner uses Claude Code and Codex together; forbidding all overlap would serialize work that has no dependency, while uncoordinated overlap would create avoidable merge and behavioural conflicts. Sequential phases remain the default, and every integrated sub-phase must still leave the app usable and verified.

## Planning — improvement ideas (2026-08-02)

- 2026-08-02 — **`docs/IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS.md` is the dated, cross-chat record for unapproved planning ideas; it records options, benefits, risks, dependencies, evidence needs and possible timing without turning them into roadmap commitments.** — The owner wants future chats to resume product discussions without reconstructing their background. Keeping captured ideas explicitly separate from the brief, phase directions and `DECISIONS.md` preserves the difference between exploration, approval and implemented behavior.

## Phase 4i — structured personal meaning blocks (2026-08-02)

- 2026-08-02 — **OWNER'S DECISION / BRIEF AMENDMENT: lexical `translation` becomes ordered personal `meanings[]` in schema v2.** — Polysemous entries need individually readable and editable meanings with their own context. This is the first personal-data migration and therefore includes a pre-open schema-v1 export gate, an atomic Dexie upgrade, and v1 backup import conversion.
- 2026-08-02 — **Personal meaning IDs use `meaning:<uuid>` and never store dictionary sense IDs or positions.** — Imported sense identity is not stable across dictionary rebuilds. Dictionary attachment remains entry-level; adding a dictionary entry only copies its first gloss into a new independent personal meaning.
- 2026-08-02 — **A meaning stores an English gloss, optional Spanish usage cue, regions, the fixed personal usage-label set, optional POS override, verb behavior, a note and personal examples.** — This is a deliberately compact personal vocabulary rather than the dictionary's full taxonomy. Entry-wide notes and general examples remain valid.
- 2026-08-02 — **Schema-v1 migration creates one meaning from every trimmed nonblank translation line, preserves bullets and numbering, does not split punctuation, and leaves existing notes/examples entry-wide.** — This is the least destructive rule without access to the owner's real browser data. Pages, events, preferences, timestamps and entry IDs do not change or acquire migration edit events.
- 2026-08-02 — **Routine reading shows all glosses and cues with context collapsed; routine editing expands one meaning; Organize meanings uses a local draft with explicit Save/Cancel.** — Reordering uses phone-safe up/down controls. Only neighboring meanings merge, keeping the upper ID; deleting populated context requires preserve-to-entry or delete-all.
- 2026-08-02 — **Repaso remains entry-level and reveals all meaning glosses/cues together; no meaning scheduling or review events exist.** — The learning-history identity stays the durable lexical item even when its meanings are reorganized.
- 2026-08-02 — **Main search keeps personal glosses at tier 4 and treats meaning cues, labels, notes and examples as tier-6 context; LinkPicker remains intentionally narrower.** — Joining glosses with spaces preserves Phase 4h's multiline query behavior, and the shared ñ-preserving normalizer remains untouched.
- 2026-08-02 — **Schema-v2 import accepts only validated v1 or v2 envelopes; v1 is upgraded and revalidated in memory before replace-and-restore.** — An already-v2 database will never run Dexie's schema migration when importing an old file, so backup upgrading must be an explicit reusable data path.

## Phase 4j–4o — persistent page profiles and Vocabulary Collections (2026-08-03)

- 2026-08-03 — **OWNER'S DECISION / BRIEF AMENDMENT: retain exactly two top-level item types and add only `general | collection` as stored page profiles in the first release.** — Collection needs durable behavior and layout; Source, Grammar, and richer Journal structures do not yet have enough evidence. Journal remains an effective kind derived from a dated General page, while Collection wins over a date.
- 2026-08-03 — **Every page stores dormant Collection metadata: `collection.groups[]`, with `page-group:<uuid>`, a trimmed unique name, and ordered `itemKeys[]`.** — Switching profiles must never destroy organization. Array order is display order; names compare with Unicode NFKC plus case folding; one lexical item can occupy at most one group within a page.
- 2026-08-03 — **`page.linkedKeys[]` remains the only relationship and Collection-membership authority; group keys are layout references only.** — This preserves the one-edge link architecture.
