Ungrouped order derives from outgoing personal lexical links, incoming lexical backlinks/page/dictionary links remain Related, incoming promotion flips the stored edge, and dictionary selections must materialize or reuse an independent personal lexical entry.
- 2026-08-03 — **Schema v3 changes page records but keeps the existing Dexie stores and indexes.** — Every old page safely defaults to General with empty groups. Both v1 and v2 owners must save and acknowledge an untouched validated export before v3 opens; direct v1→v3 runs meanings before page profiles. Backup schemas 1, 2, and 3 upgrade sequentially in memory and are deeply validated as v3 before any write; newer versions stay blocked.
- 2026-08-03 — **Collection capture and organization are coherent transactions, not sequences of visible partial writes.** — Final Add creates/reuses personal entries, writes their existing `create` events, promotes incoming edges, links, and places members together. Organizer works on a deep local draft; Cancel writes nothing, no-op Save writes nothing, and one changed Save writes one page `edit`.
- 2026-08-03 — **Collection membership add/remove, Practice, pinning, expansion, and mode changes write no events; profile changes write one page `edit`.** — Membership and view modes are bookkeeping rather than page prose, pinning is a preference, and lightweight Practice has no history. Existing link/unlink event behavior remains unchanged.
- 2026-08-03 — **Page creation uses five built-in starting points, but stores no template ID.** — Blank page preserves General capture; Blank collection and three vocabulary group starters seed editable local groups. User-authored templates, Source/Grammar starters, and a permanent template taxonomy remain deferred.
- 2026-08-03 — **Collection detail owns local Read, Organize, and Practice modes while App keeps the existing detail trail and scroll contract.** — Read supports multiple independent expansions; Organize clears them and blocks add-capture until Save/Cancel; Practice preserves group/item order, puts Not grouped yet last, excludes Related, and reveals answers independently without shuffle, grades, scores, scheduling, Repaso integration, or events.
- 2026-08-03 — **Page pins live only in the backed-up `pinnedPageIds` preference.** — A pin changes neither `updatedAt` nor the event log. Pins stable-partition Pages browsing only after the selected order, never boost search relevance, and stale/deleted IDs are filtered or removed defensively.
- 2026-08-03 — **Cuaderno page-profile filters are All pages, General, Collections, and Journal entries.** — General means undated General; Journal means dated General; Collection remains Collection even with a date. Profile filtering precedes contextual tag counts and clears when the owner leaves Pages.
- 2026-08-03 — **Dictionary attachment reuse in Collection capture follows the installed `previousIds` alias map before materializing anything new.** — A dataset rename must not make the same attached personal word appear unattached or create a duplicate; both picker presentation and the final transaction resolve the alias seam.
- 2026-08-03 — **Consecutive tricky toggles receive strictly increasing millisecond timestamps for the same item.** — Event-derived state cannot rely on random primary-key order when two owner actions land in one ISO timestamp millisecond; this preserves the event log as the sole state authority.

## Phase 4p–4s — Diario workspace (2026-08-03)

- 2026-08-03 — **OWNER'S DECISION: build Diario as a dedicated workspace over dated General pages, with no schema change, explicit Journal profile or third item type.** — The desired distinction is a writing and rereading experience; the existing date-derived identity already supplies it without migration risk or duplicate data.
- 2026-08-03 — **4p adds Diario between Cuaderno and Repaso, removes journals from ordinary Cuaderno browsing and its profile chooser, but preserves intentional global search.** — Each surface gets a clear everyday purpose while an owner who deliberately searches the whole notebook can still find anything.
- 2026-08-03 — **App owns one session-only cross-tab route trail and keeps Cuaderno's mounted retrieval state while following a journal result.** — Journal, vocabulary and page links can cross surfaces and Back returns to the actual origin, including a filtered search result list, without introducing URL or stored navigation state.
- 2026-08-03 — **Journal creation belongs only to Diario; the General-page form no longer offers a date, and header page totals exclude journals.** — Separate capture paths prevent new journal entries from falling back into Pages and make the displayed categories agree with the four-tab information architecture.

## Phase 4q — Diario home and retrieval (2026-08-03)

- 2026-08-03 — **Today is the earliest-created entry on the local calendar day; New moment always starts another same-day draft, and Continue is the most recently updated entry other than that Today anchor.** — Today stays a stable daily doorway without imposing one-entry-per-day, while Continue avoids duplicating the same call to action.
- 2026-08-03 — **The default timeline shows the current year newest-first and older years live behind an explicit, newest-year-first Archive.** — Recent writing stays close without turning a growing journal into an endless first screen; no count or stored archive status is needed.
- 2026-08-03 — **Diario search is a local derivation over title, body and tags only and never writes `search_miss`.** — An empty journal query means “not in my memories,” not “Spanish vocabulary I could not find,” so feeding it into the learning-friction log would corrupt that signal.
- 2026-08-03 — **Around this time selects the closest entry within ±7 month/day calendar days from the most recent prior year containing a candidate.** — One bounded memory creates gentle rediscovery without a feed, ranking model, stored anniversary metadata or repeated cards from many years.

## Phase 4r — focused journal editor and prompts (2026-08-03)

- 2026-08-03 — **A fresh journal draft materializes only after its body is nonblank; opening, changing a date, or typing only a title creates no page or event.** — The Today action can feel immediate without accumulating empty records from abandoned intentions.
- 2026-08-03 — **Journal writing autosaves after a short pause, flushes eligible changes before Back or unmount, and requires a date while keeping title optional.** — The main writing flow has no Save ceremony, but leaving through the editor cannot silently discard a valid draft and clearing the required identity cannot move it into Pages by accident.
- 2026-08-03 — **A new-entry editor visit emits only the existing `create` event; an existing-entry visit emits at most one `edit` even across many autosaves.** — Event history describes owner actions rather than keystrokes or debounce boundaries. Late unmount saves may reload data but cannot rewrite the tab route the owner already chose.
- 2026-08-03 — **The first prompt library contains 24 bilingual prompts in Notice, Reflect, Spanish and Grow groups; selection is visit-local guidance, never stored metadata or automatic body text.** — Prompts can lower the blank-page barrier without categorizing the owner's memories, constraining what gets written or triggering a schema change.

## Phase 4s — journal reading, connections and tools (2026-08-03)

- 2026-08-03 — **A journal opens in a reading-first view: date, optional title and body lead; vocabulary and related moments follow; Edit and Más are explicit actions.** — Most revisits are for remembering, so forms, activity and destructive controls should not visually compete with the entry itself.
- 2026-08-03 — **Journal vocabulary capture searches existing personal words and phrases only; valid legacy dictionary links still resolve through aliases and unresolved keys remain visible and removable.** — The journal connects to vocabulary the owner can edit without recreating the broad dictionary/page picker, while the §5 seam continues to protect earlier links from silent loss.
- 2026-08-03 — **Reflect creates a new current-local-day draft carrying one ordinary link to the source entry and a visit-local reflection prompt.** — A later perspective remains its own dated memory rather than overwriting history or becoming a stored subrecord; abandoning the draft creates neither page nor link.
- 2026-08-03 — **Más manages tags, media, nonjournal page relations, activity, tricky state, two-step deletion and explicit Move to Pages; it offers neither a journal pin nor direct Collection conversion.** — Existing page fields and event rules already support the useful tools. Clearing `pageDate` preserves the same ID, body and links, then App's canonical route moves the entry to Cuaderno with Diario still on the Back trail.
- 2026-08-03 — **The editor's mounted guard is restored in every effect setup, not only initialized once in a ref.** — The 375×812 browser pass exposed React StrictMode's setup→cleanup→setup replay leaving the guard false: persistence completed, but Saved and route materialization were suppressed. Re-establishing the live flag in setup preserves real unmount protection and now has explicit StrictMode coverage.
- 2026-08-03 — **If a tab switch unmounts an editor while its required date is temporarily blank, the quiet flush restores the most recently selected nonblank date for that visit; explicit Back still blocks until a date is visible.** — Primary navigation bypasses the editor's leave guard, so refusing the unmount write would silently discard valid title/body changes. Reusing the last valid date preserves writing without clearing `pageDate`, moving the entry into Pages, or weakening the visible validation rule.

## Phase 4t–4x — typed and explained link relationships (2026-08-04; implemented locally, browser closeout pending)

- 2026-08-04 — **OWNER'S DECISION / BRIEF AMENDMENT: proceed with Phase 4t–4x without the previously proposed real-link audit and ship all seven approved relationship types together.** — The owner reviewed the hypothetical examples, found them representative enough to make the product choice, and explicitly waived the evidence gate. This records approval to implement, not a claim that the phase has shipped.
- 2026-08-04 — **Schema v4 adds mandatory sparse `linkAnnotations[]` to each `UserItem`, while `linkedKeys[]` remains the sole connection-existence and Collection-membership authority.** — One annotation can describe an existing ordinary edge but never create one. An absent annotation derives Related with a blank note, and Related with a blank note is normalized away rather than backfilled densely.
- 2026-08-04 — **The fixed display order is Similar meaning, Contrast, Often confused, Variant, Explained by/Explains, Found in/Contains, then Related.** — The two directional types use `subject: owner | target` so the endpoint receiving the forward label is independent of physical edge storage; symmetric types normalize to owner. There are no custom or multiple types per pair in this release.
- 2026-08-04 — **Schema v3→v4 adds only `linkAnnotations: []`; it does not repair or discard self, duplicate, or reciprocal legacy edges.** — The application has no evidence that the owner's data contains those anomalies, and an export-first migration must not silently alter topology. Runtime derivation tolerates old redundancy, mutation APIs prevent new redundancy, and explicit unlink/delete cleanup removes every stored copy of the conceptual connection.
- 2026-08-04 — **Schema-v1, v2, and v3 databases require an untouched validated export before v4 opens; backup schemas 1–4 upgrade sequentially and validate completely before any write.** — V4 validation covers mandatory annotations, fixed values, physical-edge membership, personal targets, conceptual-pair uniqueness, Collection metadata, dictionary orphans, and unresolved alias conflicts while preserving exact v4 round-trip behavior.
- 2026-08-04 — **Link creation, unlinking, relationship type changes, and relationship note changes write no activity events.** — The owner chose to treat all of them as connection bookkeeping: a relationship note explains an edge rather than becoming either endpoint's prose. This is a deliberate exception to the item-content rule under which tags and item notes log `edit`.
- 2026-08-04 — **Metadata-only relationship saves and alias-conflict resolution preserve both endpoints' `updatedAt`; link creation/removal retain their existing timestamp behavior.** — Editing from a backlink must not reorder either the screen being viewed or the item that happens to own the physical edge.
- 2026-08-04 — **A dictionary alias rewrite carries its relationship annotation only when the result is unambiguous; conflicting explicit old/canonical annotations pause all rewrite and deduplication until owner resolution.** — Keeping both raw keys and values exportable avoids silent loss. Resolution chooses or edits one surviving type/note, then canonicalizes without an event or timestamp change; installed orphans keep annotations, while an uninstalled dictionary stays hidden.
- 2026-08-04 — **Collection membership and `dictKey` attachment remain outside ordinary relationship annotations; Diario's earlier “no new relationship type” boundary applied only to completed 4p–4s and is superseded for this phase.** — Active Collection membership hides but preserves dormant annotation metadata, promotion flips a directional subject when edge ownership reverses, and specialized screens can group ordinary connections without inventing a new content/profile identity.
- 2026-08-04 — **Relationship notes remain outside search, filters, Repaso, and activity; Phase 4t–4x adds no hub, provenance model, custom/multiple types, or Example of/Part of types.** — The first release makes individual connection lists understandable without turning the notebook into a general graph system.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 4t–4x is implemented locally after 497/497 serial tests, the production build, and all planned deliberate failure proofs passed; the disposable 375×812 browser closeout remains pending.** — Codex's in-app browser-control kernel failed before fixture setup with a missing local asset path. This is a verification-tool limitation, not a claimed browser result; no owner data was inspected and no push or deployment was performed.
- 2026-08-04 — **Standard Detail and Collection ordinary Connections group mixed personal and dictionary targets by relationship, superseding Phase 4d–4e's palabras/páginas/diario presentation on those surfaces; Diario retains its specialized sections and groups by relationship inside each.** — This is the intentional phone-first design approved for 4t–4x: the semantic reason for a connection is primary, nested kind headings are avoided, and existing unannotated connections initially share one Related group. Collection membership remains separate from ordinary Connections.
- 2026-08-04 — **Post-implementation review follow-up passes 493/493 serial tests and the production build after retiring seven unreachable legacy-grouping tests and adding three regressions; the disposable 375×812 browser closeout remains pending.** — The follow-up records relationship-first grouping as intentional, fixes preserved self-link editing, hardens alias-resolution failure handling and annotation-driven refreshes, and leaves the earlier closeout snapshot intact. The browser-control connection still fails at the same missing local asset path before fixture setup; no owner data was inspected and no push or deployment was performed.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 4t–4x is deployed from `main` at `eb93c90`.** — GitHub Pages workflow run 30949552774 completed both build and deploy jobs successfully; the live site returned HTTP 200 and referenced the verified build's `index-BhBJ0bH7.js` asset. The earlier no-deployment lines remain as append-only snapshots of their respective verification moments, while the disposable 375×812 browser closeout remains open.
- 2026-08-04 — **OWNER-OBSERVED FRICTION / PHASE 4y: a personal word or phrase can add itself to one active Vocabulary Collection and choose its group; active Collections no longer appear as ordinary lexical Connection targets.** — Reverse capture reuses the same atomic `commitCollectionAdd` writer, defaults to Not grouped yet, stays on the lexical entry, and leaves move/remove in Collection Organize. This closes the endpoint asymmetry without changing schema v4, page-owned membership authority, dormant relationship metadata, timestamps, or event rules.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 4y is deployed from `main` at `c716e9d`.** — GitHub Pages workflow run 30955868049 completed both build and deploy jobs successfully; the live site returned HTTP 200 and referenced the verified build's `index-BpNcWMY7.js` asset. The disposable 375×812 browser closeout remains pending because the local browser-control kernel still cannot initialize.

## Phase 7 — composable pages, Source notebooks and Grammar guides (2026-08-04; approved for implementation)

- 2026-08-04 — **OWNER'S DECISION / BRIEF AMENDMENT: replace exclusive General/Collection profiles with composable pages in schema v5 while retaining exactly two top-level item types.** — Pages need to serve simultaneously as notes, vocabulary hubs, sources and grammar guides without multiplying content types or forcing one permanent subtype.
- 2026-08-04 — **Every page stores one leading `pageFocus: notes | vocabulary | source | grammar`; Notes remain the permanent body-based foundation, while Vocabulary, Source and Grammar enable independently.** — Focus controls section order and presentation rather than identity. A structured focus is valid only while its matching capability is enabled.
- 2026-08-04 — **Disabling a populated page structure hides and preserves it; existing pages acquire Source or Grammar only through explicit owner customization.** — Reversible experimentation must not destroy content, and titles, dates, tags, bodies or links are not reliable enough to classify old pages automatically. Hidden structures stay outside role filters, search and contextual summaries until restored.
- 2026-08-04 — **The schema-v5 Source structure stores a small identity (`format`, creator, scope, URL and context) plus one flat ordered stream of Passage, Reflection, Language note and Question captures.** — This supplies durable source-specific capture and retrieval without attachments, a reading tracker, deep provenance or rigid per-medium schemas. Captures use stable `source-capture:<uuid>` IDs and require nonblank text when saved.
- 2026-08-04 — **The schema-v5 Grammar structure stores a key idea plus ordered named sections containing explanation, pattern and ordered example pairs.** — Rule/construction, comparison and example-bank workflows need repeatable structure without becoming separate page types. Section and example IDs remain stable; section names use the existing normalized uniqueness rule, and each saved example requires Spanish text.
- 2026-08-04 — **A Grammar example may attach vocabulary and at most one exact Source capture, including a capture on the same composable page.** — Exact context is useful without introducing a general provenance graph. An external reference requires or promotes one ordinary page connection and preserves existing relationship metadata; a same-page reference does not create an illegal self-link.
- 2026-08-04 — **`linkedKeys[]` remains the only page-vocabulary-membership and ordinary-connection authority; Collection groups, Source captures and Grammar examples store contextual/layout references only.** — The established one-edge architecture and dictionary seam remain intact. Dictionary selections materialize or reuse independent personal lexical items before attachment.
- 2026-08-04 — **Detaching vocabulary from one capture or example keeps its page membership, while removing it from the page clears every active or hidden contextual reference after an impact confirmation.** — Local context edits should not unexpectedly eject useful vocabulary from the page, but no nested reference may outlive authoritative membership. Deleting a Source capture clears exact Grammar references without removing ordinary page connections.
- 2026-08-04 — **Diario remains a separate workspace over dated pages with no enabled structured capability; dated enhanced pages remain in Pages.** — Journal identity stays derived and no Journal-only schema is added. If configuration removes the last capability from a dated page, it moves to Diario; Diario itself does not expose page customization.
- 2026-08-04 — **Schema v5 removes `pageProfile` without changing Dexie stores or indexes.** — Existing Collection pages migrate to Vocabulary focus with Vocabulary enabled; every other page migrates to Notes focus with Vocabulary disabled, retaining dormant groups and links. Empty disabled Source and Grammar structures are added while IDs, content, events, preferences, annotations, timestamps and order remain unchanged.
- 2026-08-04 — **Schema-v1 through v4 databases require an untouched validated export before v5 opens; backup schemas 1–5 upgrade sequentially and validate completely before any write.** — Nested IDs, enums, URLs, focus/capability consistency, hidden populated structures, membership authority and exact Source references are now personal-data durability invariants. Versions newer than 5 remain blocked and v5 exports round-trip exactly.
- 2026-08-04 — **Changed configuration, persisted focus, Source-capture saves, Grammar-guide saves and organizer saves each write one page `edit`; membership-only bookkeeping and automatic dependent cleanup remain event-free.** — Activity records explicit content/structure actions rather than navigation or referential maintenance. Explicit saves update their owner normally, while cleanup caused only by another record is timestamp-neutral on the dependent page.
- 2026-08-04 — **Pages retrieval uses overlapping single-choice roles: All, Sources, Grammar, Collections and Notes, with Notes defined by the saved leading focus.** — A composable page may appear in several capability filters while retaining one clear reading order. Empty-query pin behavior remains unchanged and cards expose focus-first badges plus role-aware counts.
- 2026-08-04 — **Creation becomes family-first and stores no starter identity: Notes; Vocabulary; Source; Grammar; or Copy page structure.** — Built-in recipes seed editable empty structure only. Copying carries focus, enabled capabilities, Collection group names and Grammar section names with fresh IDs; it copies no prose, dates, tags, media, links, Source identity/captures, Grammar explanations/patterns/examples or template identity.
- 2026-08-04 — **Source and Grammar starters enable Vocabulary by default.** — Both workflows are intended to become vocabulary hubs, while the owner can hide that capability later without losing memberships or placements. Source recipes cover book/written work, podcast/audio, film/video and article/lesson; Grammar recipes cover rule/construction, compare forms and example bank.
- 2026-08-04 — **Pages-only search may match contained vocabulary and active Source/Grammar content, while global search keeps the lexical result primary and summarizes its active page contexts.** — This makes Pages useful as contextual hubs without flooding global results with one page hit per placement. Hidden structures and relationship notes remain outside retrieval, and all matching continues through the ñ-preserving normalizer.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 7 will be implemented as staged 7a–7g commits, but schema v5 and the complete composable interface deploy together only after explicit approval.** — Contract/durability, shared page foundations, creation/library, Source, Grammar, contextual retrieval and integration have ordered dependencies. Phase 6 AI may remain unstarted; no partially exposed Source or Grammar toggle ships.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 7 does not add a third content type, folders, a free-form block editor, custom page kinds, a template manager, attachments, rich-media cataloging, deep provenance, source hierarchies, a reading tracker, Journal schema expansion or AI behavior.** — The approved release addresses the validated page workflows without turning the notebook into a general content-management system.
- 2026-08-04 — **Schema-v5 page identity is derived only from `pageFocus` and enabled structures; the old profile module, optional constructor input and `setPageProfile` entry point remain bounded compatibility adapters.** — Backup validation and direct v1–v4 upgrades must still understand historical General/Collection records, and existing call sites can delegate during the transition, but `pageProfile` is removed before any v5 page is persisted or exported.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 7 is assembled locally on its feature branch, with release status deliberately left open until the complete serial suite, production build, disposable seeded browser flow and final approval are recorded.** — Focused implementation tests are green, but partial evidence is not a production claim; no owner browser data was inspected and no push or deployment was performed.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 7 post-audit automated closeout passes: the complete serial suite reports 58 files / 593 tests in 229.90s, the Vite production build processes 1,863 modules in 4.49s, and `git diff --check` passes.** — The in-app browser bootstrap failed before disposable fixture setup with `failed to write kernel assets: The system cannot find the path specified. (os error 3)`, so the 375×812 visual flow remains explicitly unverified. No owner data was inspected, and no push or deployment is claimed.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 7 is deployed from `main` at `982bae5`.** — GitHub Pages workflow run [30966093217](https://github.com/bpanozzo23-prog/mi-cuaderno/actions/runs/30966093217) completed successfully after the approved fast-forward from `phase-7-composable-pages`. The disposable 375×812 browser closeout remains explicitly unverified because the in-app browser could not initialize; no owner browser data was inspected.

## Phase 4z — dedicated Pages hub (2026-08-04)

- 2026-08-04 — **OWNER'S DECISION / PHASE 4z: the existing `páginas` control opens a dedicated Pages hub with a focused `‹ Cuaderno / Pages / +` header rather than adding a fifth bottom tab or replacing Cuaderno.** — The schema-v5 page library now has enough distinct retrieval and creation behavior to merit a calm home, while the session route trail and still-mounted Cuaderno preserve the owner's actual origin and visit-local state.
- 2026-08-04 — **The hub keeps Diario outside its browse and contextual-search candidates, uses overlapping All/Sources/Grammar/Collections/Notes roles, separates matching pins only for empty-query browsing, and reveals page-relevant View/Order/Tag controls progressively.** — Global Cuaderno search remains the intentional cross-surface path to journals; pinning must never override §8 search relevance, and a hidden refinement always reports its active count.
- 2026-08-04 — **Hub cards derive focus-first badges and summaries only from current page data; creation recipes remain transient and no recipe label, hub filter, counter or page identity is stored.** — Source identity/captures, Grammar sections/examples, Vocabulary membership/groups, Notes prose and tags already provide honest scan cues. Hoisting the existing backed-up pin preference into App keeps hub cards, global results and detail synchronized without a schema, event, timestamp or backup change.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 4z is implemented locally after 596/596 serial tests across 59 files, the production build and a deliberate Pages-route failure proof passed.** — The focused hub reuses schema-v5 roles, contextual search and family-first creation; no owner data was inspected, no deployment was performed, and the disposable 375×812 browser closeout remains pending.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 4z's disposable 375×812 browser closeout passes on an isolated local origin.** — A fixture created through the visible UI verified focused header replacement, overlapping roles, 44px role-filter targets, pinned/all separation, Grammar search in relevance mode, progressive Refine controls, detail return and no horizontal overflow, console warnings or console errors. No owner browser data was inspected or changed, and no deployment was performed.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 4z is deployed from `main` at `67a646d`.** — GitHub Pages workflow run [30971214618](https://github.com/bpanozzo23-prog/mi-cuaderno/actions/runs/30971214618) completed successfully after the confirmed push. The deploy contains only the verified Pages hub source, tests and project documentation; no owner browser data was inspected or changed.

## Phase 8 — dedicated Words & phrases hub (2026-08-04)

- 2026-08-04 — **OWNER'S DECISION / PHASE 8: words and phrases share ONE hub with All/Words/Phrases chips rather than getting separate hubs.** — They are one content type told apart by `form` (§7) and share every field, the same detail screen and the same review model; two hubs would duplicate the surface and force the owner to guess whether *dar con* lives under words or phrases. The header already prints the two counts, so a chip row satisfies the distinction without splitting the code.
- 2026-08-04 — **OWNER'S DECISION: `palabras` and `frases` become doors to the hub, exactly as `páginas` has been since Phase 4z; the Cuaderno root keeps `todo` plus global dictionary-merged search.** — After 4z the root list was already mostly the lexical list while doubling as the global search surface, and it could only organize by what is true of any item. Making the remaining chips doors leaves the root honestly one thing: the single list that spans both layers (§8).
- 2026-08-04 — **The hub ships all four organizing abilities in its first release: where a word lives, a read-only learning signal, the completeness views, and the A–Z index with pinned words.** — Each is already derived and shipped; the hub browses what previously could only be seen one item at a time on a detail screen. "Where it lives" reuses `activePageContextsForLexical` and is the true lexical mirror of the Pages hub's roles.
- 2026-08-04 — **Review state is displayed and filtered but never graded in the hub; Repaso remains the only review flow (§12).** — The hub answers "what have I collected and how is it going", not "test me now". Retired is checked before enrolment when badging, because a graduated word that is not highlighted is no longer enrolled and would otherwise show nothing.
- 2026-08-04 — **Hub search covers personal vocabulary only and offers a hand-off that carries the query to Cuaderno's mixed list; it logs no `search_miss`.** — §7 says a query the dictionary answers must not become a miss, and a hub that never consults the dictionary cannot tell the difference. `SearchBar` gained `logMisses` (default true, so every existing caller is unchanged) and the root list records the genuine miss after the hand-off.
- 2026-08-04 — **Filtering by relationship type stays out, upholding the Phase 4t decision that relationship metadata is not filterable and there is no relationship hub.** — Revisiting that is a separate owner decision, not a side effect of building this surface.
- 2026-08-04 — **Pinned vocabulary adds the `pinnedLexicalIds` preference WITHOUT a `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump; it stays 5.** — Backups already dump and restore preferences generically (`allPrefs`), the pin validator is guarded by `hasOwnProperty` so backups written before this release still validate, and no item shape or migration is involved. `deleteItem` now cleans both pin lists in the same transaction, because a stale id would make the backup validator reject the export rather than merely showing a phantom card.
- 2026-08-04 — **`wantsDictionary` simplifies to "todo, and no tag".** — With three of the four chips now doors, the `palabras` branch was unreachable from the root list; a dead branch that no test can exercise is worse than a smaller function. The tag rule is what genuinely remains.
- 2026-08-04 — **The hub's two whole-notebook derivations are gated behind an `active` prop.** — It stays mounted so its visit-local controls survive a detail hop, but the page-context index and the Leitner replay would otherwise run on every unrelated notebook change. Repaso avoids this by being conditionally rendered; the hub cannot be, so it skips the work instead.
- 2026-08-04 — **A–Z headings group but do not stick.** — Sticky letters under an already-sticky focused header need a hard-coded offset that breaks if the header wraps, and phone-width safety outranks the polish. Grouping order comes from `orderItems`' Spanish collator, which places Ñ after N; `normalize` decides each letter, so accents fold and ñ does not.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 8 is implemented locally: 648/648 serial tests across 61 files, the production build, `git diff --check`, and three deliberate red/green failure proofs all pass.** — The proofs broke `matchesContextFilter`'s `none` case, the lexical pin type check, and pinned/relevance separation in hub search; each turned the intended test red and passed again on restoration. No owner data was inspected and no deployment was performed.
- 2026-08-04 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the Pages hub stops logging `search_miss` too; only a search whose scope is everything may log one.** — Phase 4z never considered this (its entries cover roles, pins, Refine and cards; nothing about misses) and no test covered it, so `onMissLogged` was inherited from SearchBar's default rather than chosen. The decisive problem is that a `search_miss` stores only `{query}` with no scope, and Repaso pools every miss into one "Searched for, not found" list — so a page-title miss and a vocabulary miss are indistinguishable to the owner. The harm is permanent and one-directional: searching *casa* in the Pages hub logged "Couldn't find casa" into the append-only log even though the dictionary holds it, and nothing retracts it. After Phase 8 the Cuaderno root is the only full-scope search left, and it remains the sole miss-logging surface. "I looked for a *subjuntivo* page and there is none" is genuinely useful but is a different signal with nowhere to land today; it needs its own design, not this one.
- 2026-08-04 — **Phase 8's 375×812 browser closeout passes on a disposable local origin, except that no visual screenshot was captured.** — The in-app browser pane did not composite frames, so `computer` clicks and screenshots time out — the same limitation recorded for Phases 4t–4x, 4y and 7. Verification instead drove real DOM events and measured layout numerically: focused header replacement, 44px chip targets, all four context filters, the learning lens and card badges, completeness views, the A–Z index including Ñ after N and `#` last, pinned/all separation with the preference confirmed in IndexedDB, meaning search with its reason, the dictionary hand-off landing in Cuaderno with the query applied, detail return with chip and pin intact, `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375` at every step, and no console warnings or errors. The fixture was cleared afterwards; no owner browser data was inspected or changed.
- 2026-08-05 — **Phase 8 re-verified after the Pages-hub miss change: 649/649 serial tests across 61 files, the production build and `git diff --check` pass, and a fourth deliberate red/green proof passed.** — Restoring `onMissLogged` on the Pages hub turned the new test red with exactly one false `search_miss`, and it passed again on restoration. That test carries its own positive control — a bare `SearchBar` through the same mock and settle must still log — so the absence assertion cannot quietly pass if the logging path itself breaks. The 648/648 line above remains as the append-only snapshot of the earlier verification moment. No owner data was inspected and no deployment was performed.
- 2026-08-05 — **Phase 8 is deployed from `main` at `470c9af`.** — Local `main` was confirmed level with `origin/main` (both `ef948ce`) before fast-forwarding to the Phase 8 commit and pushing; the push reported a real ref move, not "Everything up-to-date". GitHub Pages workflow run [31011294641](https://github.com/bpanozzo23-prog/mi-cuaderno/actions/runs/31011294641) completed both the build and deploy jobs successfully, and the live site returned HTTP 200 with `Last-Modified` matching the deploy and the verified `index-C440RsSH.js` asset. The disposable 375×812 browser closeout recorded above remains without a visual screenshot; no owner browser data was inspected or changed.

## Phase 9 — filtered free-practice flashcards (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION / BRIEF AMENDMENT: the Words & phrases hub can practice any currently filtered personal-vocabulary subset, while Repaso remains the only scheduled review flow.** — The hub already composes form, page context, learning, completeness, tag and search narrowing. Reusing that visible result set avoids a second configuration taxonomy and preserves the meaning of the existing Leitner queue.
- 2026-08-05 — **Free-practice Again/Got it feedback is session-only and never writes `review_pass`, `review_fail`, a practice event, a stored score or a schedule change.** — Self-check feedback is useful for a missed-only follow-up round, but a hand-picked study session must not unexpectedly enroll, advance or retire words in Repaso. This supersedes Phase 8's no-session boundary only for transient practice; event-backed grading remains exclusive to Repaso.
- 2026-08-05 — **The preflight offers 10, 20 (default) or All cards and Shuffled (default) or current hub order; it stores nothing.** — A small explicit size avoids an overwhelming full notebook, shuffle removes alphabetical/recency cues, and current order remains available for intentional sequences. The chosen deck is snapshotted so navigation or later derivation cannot reorder it mid-session.
- 2026-08-05 — **Only personal lexical entries with at least one saved meaning are eligible; Spanish is the front and every personal meaning and cue is revealed together.** — An incomplete entry has no honest answer side, raw dictionary keys cannot own durable learning, and entry-level cards preserve the existing structured-meaning and Repaso boundary. Matching incomplete entries are counted and explained rather than silently disappearing.
- 2026-08-05 — **The answer side is shared with scheduled ReviewSession, but the session controllers and event behavior remain separate.** — Meanings, labels, optional notes/examples and full-entry navigation should not drift visually; isolating the scheduled event writer prevents presentation reuse from turning free practice into review history.
- 2026-08-05 — **Phase 9 adds no schema, database write, preference, backup field, timestamp change, saved deck, exact-page/POS filter, reverse direction, history, score, reminder or scheduler. `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 5.** — Every new setting and result exists only for the mounted hub visit, while Vocabulary-page Practice and scheduled Repaso retain their existing behavior.
- 2026-08-05 — **Phase 9 is implemented locally after 662/662 serial tests across 64 files, the production build, `git diff --check`, a deliberate missing-meaning failure proof and a disposable 375×812 visual browser flow all passed.** — The phone flow verified active-filter candidate counts, capped default preflight, current-order cards, reveal content, 44px controls, full-entry return, missed-only round 2, unchanged Repaso and zero horizontal overflow, warnings or errors. A pre-existing schema-4 gate on the first local origin was left untouched; the check used a fresh isolated origin, inspected no owner data, and performed no push or deployment.

## Hub visual-density polish (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the Pages and Words & phrases hubs remove their repeated introductory copy and lead with filters plus one compact utility row; Words & phrases reduces its practice callout to a single Practice button.** — The sticky headers already identify each destination, so repeating an eyebrow, large title and description consumes the phone's most valuable space without adding orientation. Search, Refine and Practice retain 44px targets and all existing behavior; eligibility guidance moves to assistive text and the practice setup sheet, with no schema, event or stored-state change.

## Cuaderno adopts the Refine disclosure (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the Cuaderno list hides its View, Order and Tag selects behind the hubs' Refine button, and the result count moves out of the chip row onto that utility row.** — The always-open grid spent two rows of the phone's most valuable space on controls that sit at their defaults most of the time, while both hubs had already proved the disclosure. The chip row now carries only the four doors, and the count keeps its place at the right of the row that replaced the grid.
- 2026-08-05 — **The Refine button, panel and labelled select are extracted to `src/components/Refine.jsx`, which also owns the single copy of `controlStyle`.** — Cuaderno would have been the third hand-maintained copy of the same markup, and the two hubs had already drifted apart in whitespace alone; the hubs' unmodified tests are what proves the extraction changed no behavior. It stays a disclosure rather than a dialog — no focus trap, no Escape handler — because nothing in this app is modal in that sense.
- 2026-08-05 — **Cuaderno's `refineCount` counts View, Order and Tag only, not the type chips or the query.** — A hidden refinement must report itself, but the chips and the search box stay visible, so counting them would inflate a badge for state the owner can already see. This matches both hubs.
- 2026-08-05 — **Implemented locally after 663/663 serial tests across 64 files, the production build, a deliberate `refineCount` failure proof and a disposable 375×812 browser flow.** — The proof forced the count to a constant and turned the new badge test red before restoring it. The phone flow used a fixture seeded into and then removed from a local origin, and verified hidden-on-load controls, the badge tracking View and Tag, retained refinements while the panel is closed, the Order control greying out to "Search relevance" under a query, both hubs still refining through the shared component, 44px targets and zero horizontal overflow, console warnings or errors. No owner data was inspected and no deployment was performed.

## Words & phrases hub card density (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the page-context summary loses its box and its "Used in N page contexts" header, becoming a compact icon-led strip whose rows open the page they name.** — The box cost about 40px on every card in the app's longest list while its header only restated what the rows already showed, and a line that names a page but does nothing when tapped is a dead end. `PageContextSummary` gains an optional `onOpenPage`, so the Cuaderno search card — which renders the summary inside its own card button, where a nested button would be invalid HTML — keeps inert rows and no caller has to duplicate the component.
- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the card shows one page title and "+N more", nothing else — not the placement kind, not the `detail` (group name, grammar section, capture location).** — On a 375px phone the detail only ever arrived truncated mid-word ("Thinking, Opinions, Understanding · Vocabular…"), and the kind repeated on nearly every row without changing what the owner was looking for: which page. One example plus a count answers "where does this live" in a single line. Both the kind and the detail keep their home on the entry's own "Used in pages" list, which has room for them.
- 2026-08-05 — **The summary de-duplicates by page id, so "+N more" counts distinct pages rather than placements.** — With only the title left, a word captured twice on one page would otherwise render the same line twice, which reads as a rendering bug. Counting from the de-duplicated list is what stops the row and the count from describing different things.
- 2026-08-05 — **`learningBadge` no longer returns `Box N`; an enrolled word that is not due gets no badge at all.** — The Leitner box is derived scheduler bookkeeping the owner cannot act on while browsing, and it appeared nowhere else in the app — not Repaso, not the entry, not this hub's own Learning filter, which offers Highlighted / In review / Due today / Retired. `Retired` and `Due today` are unchanged, including the deliberate ordering that checks graduation before enrolment.
- 2026-08-05 — **Hub titles are sized by length through one shared `hubTitleSize` helper in `src/theme.jsx`: 20px normally, 17px past 18 characters.** — A 22px headword made a phrase like "no te entendí muy bien" wrap to two lines and dominate its card, while single words deserve to stay the focal point. Both hubs call the same helper so the Pages and Words & phrases cards cannot drift, and both class names stay complete literals because Tailwind scans source text and would never emit a concatenated one.
- 2026-08-05 — **The state badge and the tag chips share one wrapping row, with filled chips reserved for review state and outlined chips for the owner's own tags; the card drops the `×N` lookup count and tightens to `py-3`.** — Two chip rows cost a row of height whenever both applied, and rendering a queue verdict and a hand-written label identically made them read as the same kind of thing. The lookup count was the least actionable thing on a scanning surface and shares its row with nothing else now; it remains on the entry and on the Cuaderno card. Removing it also removed the card's only use of `itemState`, so the prop and its plumbing went with it.
- 2026-08-05 — **Context rows are 32px tall, not the project's usual 44px.** — The change exists to reclaim vertical space in the app's longest list, and two 44px rows would give back more than the box cost. They are secondary links inside a card whose own body remains the large target, and each row spans the full card width. Raising them remains a one-line change if daily use shows mis-taps.
- 2026-08-05 — **Implemented locally after 665/665 serial tests across 64 files, the production build, a deliberate `Box N` failure proof and a disposable 375×812 browser flow.** — The proof restored the removed branch and turned both new badge checks red before reverting. The phone flow seeded a fixture into a disposable local origin and cleared it afterwards, and verified the compact strip, a context row opening its own page, "+1 more" and the card body opening the entry, no `Box N` or `×N` anywhere on the hub, "Due today" and outlined tags sharing one row, the 17px/20px title split on both hubs, inert rows on the Cuaderno search card, and zero horizontal overflow, console warnings or errors. No owner data was inspected and no deployment was performed.

## Card meanings, badge removal and tag colours (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: a browsing card shows one meaning — the first — behind a dash, on both the Cuaderno card and the Words & phrases hub card.** — A word with five senses turned its row into a paragraph that `line-clamp-2` then cut mid-thought, and the hub card had been rendering its gloss without the dash the Cuaderno card has always had, so the same entry read differently on two screens. `firstMeaningGloss` is a new sibling of `meaningGlosses`; search, links, the entry, the pickers and both answer sides still read every meaning, because a picker exists to tell similar words apart and an answer side is the answer.
- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: `learningBadge` is deleted. No browsing card carries review state — not Due today, not Retired, not the box.** — Removing the box number last pass had already left the function thin. A verdict the owner cannot act on from the surface they are reading is decoration; grading belongs to Repaso (§12) and the state is on the entry. The tricky highlighter stays, because that mark is the owner's own rather than the queue's. The hub's Learning filter keeps its Due today and Retired options — a filter is actionable in a way a badge is not.
- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: tags carry an owner-chosen colour from a fixed palette of eight presets, edited in Ajustes → Tag colors.** — Presets rather than a colour wheel: each swatch pairs a pale background with a text tone dark enough to read on paper, so no choice the owner can make produces an unreadable chip. The map is stored as one `tagColors` preference, so it needs no schema change (`SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 5), rides along in backups automatically, and prunes tags the notebook no longer has when it loads — in memory only, because a write on every launch is hard to explain later.
- 2026-08-05 — **One `TagChip` replaces five hand-rolled copies, and `theme.jsx`'s `Chip` becomes a filter control only.** — The hub card, Cuaderno card, collection page, journal reader and tag editor each styled tags with the same two tokens copy-pasted; a colour that applies everywhere cannot be maintained five times. The colour map reaches them through context rather than props, because threading a preference through every card and sheet in between would be noise, and a chip rendered without the provider falls back to Plain — exactly how tags looked before. `Chip` was doing double duty as a plain display chip for the tag editor; with that gone its `onRemove` branch was dead and its `aria-pressed` is now always meaningful.
- 2026-08-05 — **`tagColors` is validated on import like the pin lists: absent is valid, present must name swatches this build knows.** — Same contract as `validatePinnedPreference`, and the reason no schema bump is needed. It is deliberately not version-gated, because tag colours reference nothing in the notebook, so the check is identical whatever schema the file was written at. Colours for tags the restore does not contain are left alone — the owner may be restoring the entries that carry them.
- 2026-08-05 — **Tag rename and delete are explicitly deferred.** — Ajustes now lists every tag in use, which makes it the obvious future home, but renaming rewrites content across many items and must log an `edit` event for each (tags are content, not bookkeeping). That deserves its own pass with its own tests rather than riding along with a display change.
- 2026-08-05 — **Implemented locally after 678/678 serial tests across 65 files, the production build, a deliberate first-meaning failure proof and a disposable 375×812 browser flow.** — The proof made `firstMeaningGloss` join every gloss again and turned three tests red across the helper and both cards before reverting. The phone flow seeded a fixture into a disposable local origin and cleared it afterwards, and verified one dashed meaning on both cards, no Due today on a word with a `review_fail`, the tricky highlight intact, 44px swatches, colours reaching the hub card, Cuaderno card, collection page, entry and tag editor, survival across a reload, and zero horizontal overflow or console errors. No owner data was inspected and no deployment was performed.

## Design tokens moved to CSS variables (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **`src/theme.jsx`'s `C` palette now holds `var(--color-*)` references instead of hex literals; the hex values live once, in a `@theme static` block in `src/index.css`.** — `C` was a plain JS object, so every one of its ~900 call sites baked a colour into a React render; changing the palette meant a rebuild, not an edit. Pointing `C`'s existing keys at CSS variables makes every call site resolve through one file without touching any of them. `static` is required so Tailwind 4 emits every token unconditionally rather than only the ones its scanner can see referenced in a JS string.
- 2026-08-05 — **The highlighter gradient's 90%-alpha stop becomes its own token, `--color-hi-soft`, instead of string-appending `E6` to `C.hi` at the call site.** — `${C.hi}E6` relied on `C.hi` being a raw hex literal; once it is `var(--color-hi)`, appending `E6` produces an invalid `backgroundImage` and the highlighter silently disappears (confirmed: the construct resolves to `none`). A second named token reproduces the exact original colour without string surgery on a `var()`.
- 2026-08-05 — **Three colours already in real use — `#D9E1F2`, `#E5C4BC`, `#B9C2D8` — get token names (`chipBorder`, `dangerBorder`, `disabled`) instead of staying as literals repeated across ~12 components.** — They were real theme colours that had simply never been added to `C`; leaving them as bare hex would have left the token layer bypassable everywhere they appear.
- 2026-08-05 — **`index.html`'s `theme-color` meta and `vite.config.js`'s manifest `theme_color`/`background_color` stay hex literals, with a comment noting they mirror `--color-paper`.** — Both are read outside the CSS cascade (browser chrome, PWA manifest) and cannot reference a `var()`; the comment exists so a future palette change doesn't miss them.
- 2026-08-05 — **Verified after the full 678/678 serial suite, the production build, and a browser check at 375×812:** the built CSS carries all 15 tokens at their original hex values; a live DOM element's inline `var()` resolved to the exact original paint colour; overriding one variable in devtools repainted 11 elements across multiple components from a single edit, proving the palette is now swappable from one place; and a deliberate red/green proof restored the pre-fix `${C.hi}E6` construct, confirming it resolves to no gradient at all before reverting to the fix. No component markup, test, or `SCHEMA_VERSION` changed. The owner has said the current palette is not locked in; this pass deliberately preserved every value byte-for-byte so a later palette change is a `src/index.css` edit.

- 2026-08-05 — **`docs/mi-cuaderno.jsx` is demoted to explicitly historical in `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md` and `README.md`; the real app supersedes it for visual and interaction decisions, not just storage/state.** — It was the pre-build look-and-feel reference and had drifted: recent styling passes (card density, meanings, tag colours) were made only in `src/`, so the prototype no longer reflects current choices. A stale reference read as current risks an agent restoring old styling on the mistaken belief it is the source of truth.

## Visual-changes working process (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: purely-visual changes (colour, spacing, type, borders — no behaviour, data, navigation, or structure) skip the upfront plan and go straight to a shown, verified result; plan-first stays for anything structural. Recorded as a new "Visual changes" section in `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md`.** — The brief (§2) requires a plan per phase or sub-phase; the guide had broadened that to "before writing code", which made a padding tweak as ceremonial as a schema change. This clarifies the guide back toward the brief rather than amending the brief. The unsure case defaults to structural.
- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: subjective visual choices are presented as 2–3 side-by-side variants on a disposable page outside the repo, with real-length content, before anything enters `src/`.** — Chosen over in-app one-at-a-time application and over per-variant branches: fastest comparison, and taste iterations stop polluting git history.
- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: no design-direction statement in the guide yet; the section is process-only.** — The palette is explicitly not locked in, and the owner prefers to settle aesthetic direction through iteration rather than declare it up front. A direction statement can be added once taste has stabilised.
- 2026-08-05 — **The guide now states plainly that the in-app browser pane cannot composite frames and that visual verification is numeric (computed styles, resolved colours, layout measurements).** — Three phase reports had already worked around this individually; writing it down once stops each new session from rediscovering it or reading it as a broken environment.
- 2026-08-05 — **The Cuaderno floating Add button uses an 82%-opaque version of the primary blue while its white plus remains fully opaque.** — The solid button visually overpowered the card beneath it; applying transparency only to the background softens the overlap without weakening the icon or changing the button's size, position, or behaviour.

## Compact empty actions on lexical entries (2026-08-05)

- 2026-08-05 — **OWNER'S DECISION: empty General examples, Media links and available Collection placement controls collapse into one row of short Example, Media and Collection actions; populated categories keep their titled sections, and remaining empty categories stay compact.** — Three headings plus three buttons made sparse phone entries look unfinished and consumed most of a screen without showing saved content. The compact controls retain 44px targets and their full accessible names, while the underlying forms, validation, writes and event rules remain unchanged.
- 2026-08-05 — **Implemented locally after 684/684 serial tests across 66 files, the production build, a deliberate missing-Example failure proof and a disposable 375×812 browser flow.** — The phone check measured three 44px actions on one 328px row, confirmed the empty headings were absent, and found zero horizontal overflow or console warnings. The temporary Collection fixture was deleted afterward; no owner data was inspected and nothing was deployed.

- 2026-08-05 — **Three habits added from a `/insights` review: read the commit message back with `git log -1 --pretty=%B` (`CLAUDE.md`), confirm `git rev-parse HEAD origin/main` matches before calling a push successful, and never use fake timers in this suite (both `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md`).** — Each closes a failure that looked like success: a corrupted commit subject, a push that reported "Everything up-to-date" from the wrong branch, and a fake-timer test that hangs instead of failing. The report's other three suggestions were rejected — two restated rules already in `CLAUDE.md`, and its commit-message rule ("never use heredocs") contradicted the correct one, having conflated the bash heredoc that works with the PowerShell here-string that broke the message.

- 2026-08-05 — **The "All matching vocabulary" / "All matching pages" section headings and their "N total" counts are gone; the count now rides inside the selected form or role chip (`All 3`, `Words 2`), shown only on the active chip.** — The heading restated the filter the chip already names, and its count sat far from the control that changes it. Putting the number in the pressed chip removes a full heading row above the first card on a phone and keeps count and control in one place. The section keeps its accessible name via `aria-label`, and the Pinned heading and the search results heading are unchanged.

## Phase 10 — learning depth (2026-08-06)

- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: Phase 10 adds four Tier-1 learning features — cloze cards, reverse cards, a conjugation drill and browser pronunciation — chosen from a brainstorm that also raised a confusion-pair drill and frequency weighting, both dropped.** — All four derive from data the notebook already holds, so none needs a schema change, a new event type or a stored counter. Phase 6 stays reserved for the AI assistant; this is numbered 10 rather than folded into Phase 4, whose remit is friction observed in daily use.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: cloze and reverse vary the existing graded due card; the conjugation drill is a separate ungraded pass.** — Keeps a Leitner box meaning exactly one thing, "do I know this word", rather than conflating meaning recall with conjugation accuracy. It is also what keeps the drill clear of §14's deferred practice history, grading and scheduling.
- 2026-08-06 — **Session direction is chosen per session (es→en / en→es / mixed) and assigned per card at snapshot time; mixed flips a coin per card.** — Deciding at snapshot means a reload after a grade cannot flip a card underneath the owner. Not persisted: a remembered direction would be a stored preference nobody asked for, and the useful default is the one you get by tapping Start.
- 2026-08-06 — **One Leitner ladder covers both directions, but `logReview` now records `direction` and `face` beside the grade.** — §14 defers scheduling beyond Leitner, so nothing schedules on direction; but which way a historical card faced cannot be reconstructed afterwards, and §7 explicitly allows additive event metadata. `backup.js` validates `event.type` as any non-empty string, so no backup change was needed.
- 2026-08-06 — **`logReview` gained `details` as its third parameter, ahead of `when`, and detects a `Date` in that position.** — A `Date` spreads to no keys, so the old three-argument call would have stamped "now" while the metadata still looked correct. The pre-existing timestamp test caught it; the guard turns a silent wrong timestamp into correct behaviour.
- 2026-08-06 — **A card with no written gloss always faces forward, whatever the session chose.** — Reverse asks the glosses; with none there is no question side to show at all.
- 2026-08-06 — **Reverse hides the term, its heading suffix and every `usageCue` until reveal.** — The cue is Spanish and routinely contains the word being asked for. Proved by a deliberate red/green: showing the cue on the question side fails the test.
- 2026-08-06 — **Cloze matches on whole token runs, not string offsets, and only ever through `normalize.js`.** — Normalization can change a string's length, so an index found in normalized text cannot be trusted against the original; token runs also stop a short term being cut out of a longer word. A deliberate ñ-collapsing break was confirmed to fail the "never blanks ano for año" test before being reverted.
- 2026-08-06 — **Verbs match their conjugation table's simple forms, gerund and participle; clitic pronouns and the perfect tenses are excluded.** — The perfects are haber + participle, so including them would add haber's own forms and let "he" or "han" blank the wrong word; "me" would match the pronoun in any sentence. This is what lets a personal example written in the preterite still become a cloze for the lemma.
- 2026-08-06 — **The owner's examples are exhausted before a dictionary stock example is considered; the random choice happens inside whichever pool is used.** — Found in the browser check, not by the tests: the candidates were ordered by preference and then chosen from uniformly, so an entry shipping more stock examples than the owner had written would usually ask the dictionary's sentence. Every existing test had a single candidate and could not have failed on it.
- 2026-08-06 — **The cloze gap is an empty fixed-width span, not the answer painted transparent, and the English side is withheld until reveal.** — A colour-hidden word is still in the DOM for a screen reader and for anyone selecting the text; a fixed width also gives no hint of the answer's length. Reverse cards get no cloze at all, since a sentence built around the term hands over what is being asked.
- 2026-08-06 — **The drill covers six tenses — present, preterite, imperfect, future, conditional, present subjunctive — excludes vosotros, and reads cells rather than assuming them.** — The rest of the tables remain available in the dictionary but would make a ten-card deck mostly rarities; vosotros is collapsed everywhere else (§3); and an empty cell is not a gap in the data, since Spanish has no first-person imperative.
- 2026-08-06 — **The drill writes no events at all, not even a `view`.** — Drilling a verb is not opening its detail screen and must not inflate the lookup counts that decide what Repaso enrolls. Proved by a deliberate red/green: adding one `logEvent` call fails the test.
- 2026-08-06 — **Drill eligibility resolves through `resolveEntry`, and nothing is written back.** — §5: a verb whose `dict:` key moved in a dataset rebuild keeps its drill. Rewriting the key belongs to the screens that own the attachment, not to a derived list.
- 2026-08-06 — **`tenseHeading` moved from `DictDetail.jsx` into `lib/conjugation.js`.** — The drill and the dictionary must not name the same tense differently. Additive export, safe for the pipeline that shares this file.
- 2026-08-06 — **Pronunciation uses the browser's own `speechSynthesis`, preferring es-MX → es-419 → es-US → any Spanish, and renders nothing when the device has no Spanish voice.** — Zero storage and nothing sent anywhere, which is the only shape that fits §13's no-server rule and the reserved Attachment model. It speaks and never records. A disabled control would promise what the browser cannot deliver; voices also load asynchronously, so an empty list means "not yet" and a loaded list without Spanish means "never".
- 2026-08-06 — **`speakSpanish` tolerates a voice the browser rejects, falling back to es-MX by language alone.** — A voice handed out earlier goes stale when a TTS engine is installed or removed, and the failed assignment throws out of the click handler, leaving a button that silently does nothing.
- 2026-08-06 — **No speaker button appears on a face that is hiding its answer.** — Speaking the term on a reverse card, or the sentence on an unrevealed cloze, would read out exactly what the card is asking for.
- 2026-08-06 — **Verified across four commits with the complete serial suite at each step (762/762 across 71 files at close), the production build, four deliberate red/green proofs, and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout.** — The phone check ran forward, reverse and mixed sessions and the drill against seeded fixtures: cloze blanked the preterite `saqué` from the owner's own sentence, the reverse face withheld the cue, `año` fell back to forward with no gloss, the drill produced correct tense/slot/answer triples and left the event log untouched, both speaker buttons measured 44×44, and no screen overflowed 375px. Fixtures were deleted afterward; no owner data was inspected and nothing was deployed. One unrelated Phase 5a navigation test (`App.test.jsx`) timed out intermittently under full-suite load and passes in isolation and on the untouched baseline; it is pre-existing timing sensitivity, not a regression from this phase.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: this batch is renumbered from Phase 7 to Phase 10, including a rewrite of its five local unpushed commit messages.** — Codex shipped Phases 7 (composable pages, schema v5), 8 (Words & phrases hub) and 9 (hub flashcard deck) in this same checkout while the batch was being implemented, so the planned "Phase 7" label collided with shipped work. The rewrite used `git filter-branch --msg-filter` over `dae9b8f..HEAD`, which changes messages only; commit contents are byte-identical and nothing had been pushed, so no shared history was touched. The guide's warning about two tools sharing one checkout applies to phase numbers too, not just branches: confirm the highest number in `README.md` at commit time, not at planning time.
- 2026-08-06 — **Phase 10 is distinct from Phase 9's hub flashcard deck and does not touch it.** — Phase 9 turns hub filters into an anytime in-memory deck that deliberately never affects scheduling; Phase 10 changes the scheduled Leitner session itself. Both can coexist, but a future merge of the two should be a deliberate decision rather than a drift.

## Phase 11 — owner-centric stats (2026-08-06)

- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the study streak and activity heatmap proceed, amending the deferral row in `docs/IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS.md`.** — That row deferred streaks over stored counters, analytics pressure and overlap with Repaso. Nothing here is stored, and these stats live inside Repaso rather than beside it, so two of the three concerns do not apply. The third — a breakable chain that nags — is accepted deliberately: the streak is a number to look at, never a goal, target, reminder or notification. Journal-specific streaks, completion and trends in Diario stay deferred on the row's original terms.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: day-level activity counts every event, including events whose item has since been deleted.** — §7 excludes deleted items' events from active queues and statistics, which protects *item-centric* results: a deleted word must not sit in a queue or a most-opened list. The activity calendar is *owner-centric*. Studying a word later deleted was still studying that day, and thinning past days on delete would make the history lie. A test encodes this so the exclusion cannot creep back in.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the streak tile and box distribution are inline on Repaso; the heatmap and growth chart sit behind an Estadísticas sub-view.** — Split by how often each is worth seeing. The sub-view reuses Repaso's existing local-boolean swap (`inSession`, `inDrill`), so it needs no route entry, tab, or back-label plumbing, and Repaso's daily screen stays scannable on a phone.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: growth is a cumulative weekly line over lexical items' `createdAt`; pages excluded.** — Cumulative reads as growth where per-week bars would read a quiet week as a dip. Deleted items are absent from the items table by construction, so §7's exclusion holds without a filter.
- 2026-08-06 — **The heatmap is Monday-start, 16 trailing weeks, with fixed intensity buckets at 1/3/6/10 events.** — Monday is the Spanish convention. 16 weeks fits 375px at a legible 14px cell without horizontal scrolling. Fixed buckets keep a quiet week from being recoloured by a busy one, which a relative scale would do.
- 2026-08-06 — **Phase 11 is not §13's "no analytics" non-goal.** — That rules out analytics in the telemetry sense: measuring the owner for someone else's benefit. These are on-device derived views of the owner's own log — nothing measured, stored, sent, or retained beyond events that already exist.
- 2026-08-06 — **`boxDistribution` counts a retired word even though retiring removes its enrollment.** — `deriveReviewState` clears `enrolled` on graduation, which is exactly what taking a word out of the queue means. Reading enrollment alone therefore left the Retired rung permanently empty and quietly lost the ladder's finish line. The returned total is named `tracked` rather than `enrolled` because it no longer means "in the queue". Found by a component test walking a word up all five boxes, not by reading the code.
- 2026-08-06 — **`lib/stats.js` re-declares the event-type strings instead of importing `EVENT_TYPES`.** — `db/events.js` already imports from `lib/`, and `lib/` staying free of database imports is what lets these functions be tested without a database. Same reason `review.js` does it.
- 2026-08-06 — **`cumulativeWordsByWeek` derives each item's calendar day from `createdAt` in the device's current zone.** — Items carry no stored `localDate` the way events do. At week granularity the drift cannot move a point, and adding a stored field would be a schema change for a chart.
- 2026-08-06 — **The activity calendar is a CSS grid of divs; only the growth line is SVG.** — The grid needs no viewBox arithmetic, takes `aria-label` and `title` per cell natively, and colours through ordinary `background` styles, which is what keeps the no-hex-in-components rule workable. The growth line is genuinely one path, and a charting dependency would be larger than the chart. This is the first hand-authored SVG in `src/`.
- 2026-08-06 — **Five `--color-heat-*` tokens rather than an alpha ramp computed in JS.** — The no-hardcoded-hex rule applies to computed colour too, and there was no existing ramp to reuse. Ink rather than green: heat-4 is `--color-pen` by hand, so the calendar ends on the app's own blue instead of near it.
- 2026-08-06 — **The heatmap grid is 254px wide by construction (16 columns × 14px + 15 gaps × 2px).** — Sized to fit inside a 375px phone's card without an inner scroll region, and measured at 254px in the browser rather than assumed.
- 2026-08-06 — **Cuaderno's review derivation is gated on a lexical entry being open, not merely memoized.** — App keeps Cuaderno mounted behind `hidden` so its filters survive a trail hop, so an ungated `useMemo` would replay the whole event log on every notebook change while the owner is on another tab. The `LexicalHub` precedent, applied for the same reason.
- 2026-08-06 — **The Estadísticas sub-view scrolls itself to the top on mount.** — App's scroll reset keys on route changes, and a local boolean swap is not a route change; without it the screen opens wherever the Repaso list happened to be scrolled to.
- 2026-08-06 — **Verified across five commits with the complete serial suite at each step (827/827 across 73 files at close), the production build, three deliberate red/green proofs, and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout on a throwaway origin.** — The proofs: dropping the streak's grace day, filtering deleted items out of the calendar, and letting pages into the growth line each reddened exactly one test and no others. The phone check ran against 24 seeded words and 107 seeded events: the streak tile read 24 and matched an independent recount of the seeded log whose first quiet day was 24 days back; the ladder read 4/0/1/1/1 with one retired; 112 calendar cells rendered with 109 labelled and three future days blank; computed cell colours matched the 1/3/6/10 thresholds exactly; the growth SVG measured 317px inside a 341px card and reported 24 words; and the detail strip wrapped to two lines reading "box 1 · reviewed 1d ago · due today" for a missed word and "retired" with no due segment for a word that finished the ladder. No screen overflowed 375px. Fixtures were deleted afterward; no owner data was inspected and nothing was deployed. One full-suite run reported a single uncaptured failure between two clean runs; `App.test.jsx`, which this log has recorded as intermittently timing out under full-suite load since Phase 10, passes 15/15 in isolation.
- 2026-08-06 — **The suite runs one file at a time, set as `fileParallelism: false` in `vitest.config.js` rather than as a flag on the `test` script.** — Every verification line above reports a "complete serial suite", from 493/493 through 827/827, but `npm test` was plain `vitest run` and therefore parallel: the command the docs prescribed was never the command the reports certified. Putting it in the config closes that gap for every invocation, including a bare `npx vitest run`, which a script flag would bypass. The cost is roughly 225s against 68s. Raising `testTimeout` would have kept the speed and silenced the same false alarm — `App.test.jsx`'s Phase 5a navigation test, logged as intermittently timing out under full-suite load since Phase 10 and passing 15/15 alone — but the 5s bound is a live hang-detector in the one file where a Phase 4c test once passed against a deliberate break by racing an async handler, so the contention was removed instead of the alarm loosened. CI is unaffected: the deploy workflow runs `npm ci` and `npm run build` only.

## Phase 6 — AI assistant, first slice: Diario feedback (2026-08-06)

- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: Phase 6 opens with one narrow feature — a user-initiated review of a single Diario entry — rather than an app-wide assistant.** — §9's "done when" also names assistant Q&A over the notebook and approved proposed entries; both stay unbuilt. A judge for one entry is the part with a felt need (there is no reviewer for diario writing today), and it needs no notebook-wide data disclosure to work.
- 2026-08-06 — **`buildBackup` destructures `aiApiKey` out of the exported preferences, and `validateBackup` refuses any envelope that carries one.** — §10 says the key is never exported; doing it inside `buildBackup` rather than at the call sites means the pre-import safety export gets the same treatment for free. Refusing on import rather than silently stripping follows the same contract as every other validator here — explain exactly what is wrong and write nothing — and a file containing a key did not come from this app's exporter. The practical effect either way: a restore lands with AI off until the key is entered again on that device.
- 2026-08-06 — **`aiEnabled` is exported like any other preference, and absent stays valid.** — It is configuration, not a secret, and every backup written before this release lacks it; validating "boolean if present" is what let this ship without a schema-version change, the same contract the pin lists and tag colours keep.
- 2026-08-06 — **Added `delPref` beside `getPref`/`setPref`.** — Removing the key has to leave no row behind: a preference stored as `null` would still be a `prefs` row, and the export's guard is the key's absence rather than its value.
- 2026-08-06 — **The Anthropic call is a raw `fetch` carrying `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access`, not `@anthropic-ai/sdk`.** — One call site, not streaming, no tools: none of what the SDK adds would be exercised, and its weight would be paid by every install of a PWA that ships with four runtime dependencies. That header is what the SDK's `dangerouslyAllowBrowser` sets underneath, and `db/ref/install.js` already establishes the hand-rolled-fetch-with-status-messages shape. A serverless proxy stays the named §9 upgrade path if the browser-stored key ever stops being acceptable.
- 2026-08-06 — **Model `claude-opus-5` in one constant, `effort: "low"`, structured output via `output_config.format`, and no `thinking`, `temperature` or `top_p` at all.** — Thinking is on by default on this model and any explicit configuration is rejected; the sampling parameters were removed from the family and would 400 every review. A json_schema output means the panel renders fields rather than parsing prose. Tests assert the absence of all three, because a stale example copied in later would break the feature silently at request time, not at build time.
- 2026-08-06 — **`stop_reason === "refusal"` is checked before `content` is read.** — A refusal arrives as a normal HTTP 200 whose content array can be empty, so reading it first turns "Claude declined" into "the review came back empty". Proved by a deliberate red/green: swapping the two blocks reddens exactly that test.
- 2026-08-06 — **`testApiKey` treats anything that is not an auth rejection as a working key.** — A rate limit or an outage means the key reached Anthropic and was accepted; calling that a bad key would send the owner to re-enter something that is already correct. A network failure is reported as itself rather than as a verdict on the key.
- 2026-08-06 — **§9 RISK ACCEPTED: the Anthropic API key is stored in the `prefs` table on this device, readable by any code running on the page, on the condition that a hard monthly spend cap is set in the Anthropic console before the feature is enabled.** — §3 makes the cap the condition of the whole arrangement, so the card gates saving a key behind an explicit acknowledgement with a link to the console, and the enable form states the risk in the same breath. A serverless proxy remains the named upgrade path if this stops being acceptable.
- 2026-08-06 — **The acknowledgement is a gate, not a stored consent record: turning the feature off and on again asks for it afresh.** — A remembered tick would outlive the cap it refers to, and caps get changed or reset. `aiEnabled` records only that the feature is on.
