- 2026-08-06 — **Turning off keeps the key; re-enabling needs the cap acknowledged but not the key retyped.** — Off is a pause, not a revocation; that is what the separate two-tap Remove key is for. The key's value is never rendered back to the screen, only the fact that one is held.
- 2026-08-06 — **A restored backup lands on the enable form, because the flag comes back and the key never does.** — The state the card actually keys on is "enabled AND a key present", so the half-restored state cannot present an on-looking card whose every button would fail.
- 2026-08-06 — **`--color-red-pale` named in `index.css`, first used by this card's error row.** — The value was already loose in `DictionaryCard.jsx` as a bare hex, which is exactly the drift the palette file exists to stop. Migrating that existing hardcode is a separate visual change, not part of this feature.
- 2026-08-06 — **The review is session-only: held in component state, never stored, and no event is logged.** — Storing it would need either a schema change (§5 in full) or a third content type (§7 forbids one without a brief amendment), and neither is worth a paragraph the owner can ask for again in seconds. No event, for the Phase 10 drill's reason: asking for feedback is not opening the entry and must not move the lookup counts that decide what Repaso enrolls. A test asserts the items and events tables are still empty after a successful review.
- 2026-08-06 — **Nothing is sent until the owner confirms a disclosure naming the entry's title and text.** — §9 requires the interface to state which categories of notebook data are included before anything is transmitted. Proved by a deliberate red/green: sending on mount reddens the whole file, starting with the test that asserts `fetch` has not been called while the disclosure is on screen.
- 2026-08-06 — **The Feedback button renders only when the feature is enabled *and* a key is present.** — Either half alone — a restored backup, a key removed while the flag stayed — would otherwise put a button on the entry whose only possible outcome is an error.
- 2026-08-06 — **The panel closes when the entry changes, and unmounting aborts a request in flight.** — A review belongs to the text it was asked about; carrying one entry's feedback over the next one's would be worse than having none. Aborting on unmount is the `DictionaryCard` pattern: a cancel returns to the disclosure with no error, since stopping is the owner's own doing.
- 2026-08-06 — **The panel lives below the entry body and tags, not in the header icon row.** — The header is entry management (edit, more); this reads the text, so it belongs after it and before the vocabulary sections.
- 2026-08-06 — **Verified across four commits with the complete serial suite at each step (868/868 across 76 files at close), the production build, four deliberate red/green proofs, and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout.** — The proofs: removing the backup destructure reddens two tests, disabling the AI preference validator reddens one, dropping the spend-cap gate from the save button reddens one, and sending on mount instead of on confirmation reddens the whole panel file. The phone check ran against one seeded worst-case entry: the request carried the entry's title and body and *not* its tags, with the key in the header and absent from the body, at `claude-opus-5` / effort low / json_schema and with no `thinking`, `temperature` or `top_p`; the disclosure held with `fetch` uncalled until confirmation; the rendered review showed the verdict chip resolving to `--color-pen-pale` on `--color-pen-dark`, six examples, and a deliberately unbroken 60-character quote wrapping to a right edge of 335px; the error surface resolved to `--color-red-pale` on `--color-red`; the items and events tables were unchanged after two reviews (the single `view` event predates the panel); leaving and re-entering the entry destroyed the review; and no screen exceeded 375px. A backup built from a database genuinely holding the key contained neither `aiApiKey` nor its value while `aiEnabled` rode along. Fixtures were deleted afterward; no owner data was inspected and nothing was deployed.
- 2026-08-06 — **One real request reached `api.anthropic.com` during the browser check, when a page reload silently dropped the in-page `fetch` stub, and returned HTTP 401 for the fixture key.** — Unplanned but the most useful evidence of the run: a 401 rather than a CORS failure proves the `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access` header actually works from a browser origin, and that the 401 mapping reads as intended. The entry sent was a seeded fixture, not owner content, and the key was never valid.
- 2026-08-06 — **The panel's buttons are the shared `Button` primitive's 38px and the close X is 32px, below the 44px used elsewhere.** — Measured, not assumed: this is the primitive's own height everywhere in the app, and the close X matches `PromptLibrary`'s exactly. Raising them is an app-wide visual change to shared components and belongs to a deliberate visual pass, not smuggled in with a feature. Raised here so it is a known state rather than an oversight.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the tutor prompt was workshopped across three rounds — the owner's own draft, a synthesis, and an external review — and the shipped version replaces the original three-section review with one positional list of categorized margin notes.** — The owner's draft contributed the level-and-dialect header ("intermediate learner of Latin American Spanish, Mexican Spanish preferred"), the named CORRECTNESS/NATURALNESS/COMPREHENSIBILITY areas, the minimal-change rule, the praise category with the anti-fluff bar ("genuine praise only, never filler"), and the 8-item cap with no floor, which fixed the original's contradiction between "three to six examples" and "return an empty list rather than inventing problems". The synthesis kept the original's code-switching guard (entries deliberately mix English; without the rule every English word gets flagged), the graded three-level verdict over a boolean, and character-exact quoting. The review round added the content boundary ("comment only on the language, never on the events or feelings described" — models default to sympathy before grammar on a diary), the unclear-item convention (interpretation in the explanation, best-guess Spanish in corrected), one-item-per-distinct-problem with minimal spans, the delimiter rule naming the <entry> tags the request already sent, and an operational tone line.
- 2026-08-06 — **Accent policy is aggregation, not suppression: meaning-changing accents are flagged individually, purely orthographic ones as a single pattern item at most.** — The review proposed flagging only meaning-changing accents, but for a learner the "harmless" ones are still part of writing Spanish properly, and a tutor who never mentions them teaches that they are optional. One pattern item ("accents dropped on preterite endings") teaches the reusable habit without letting phone-keyboard accent drops flood the 8 slots.
- 2026-08-06 — **The prompt spends no words on output format; the schema is enforced by the API's structured output, and `corrected` is nullable for praise.** — "Return only valid JSON" instructions guard against a failure the API has already made impossible. A test pins the prompt's three load-bearing rules — code-switching, the content boundary, and the <entry> tag promise — so a future rewording cannot silently drop the lines that would visibly misbehave on day one.
- 2026-08-06 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (872/872 across 76 files), the production build, one deliberate red/green (loosening the category check to any string reddens exactly the unknown-category test), and a 375×812 browser closeout.** — The phone check drove a stubbed review carrying all four categories: five margin notes rendered in entry order with labels resolving exactly to `--color-red`/`--color-pen-dark`/`--color-mut`/`--color-green`, the praise item rendered without a correction arrow (corrected: null), a deliberately unbroken 57-character quote wrapped at a right edge of 335px, no screen exceeded 375px, and the outgoing request carried every workshopped rule (dialect, code-switching, content boundary, tag rule, accent policy, praise bar, 8-item cap) with the new three-field schema. Fixtures were deleted afterward; nothing was deployed.

## Phase 12 — importing meanings from the attached dictionary entry (2026-08-06)

- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: an optional, owner-initiated import copies senses from the attached dictionary entry into the item's own `meanings[]`, and reads only the `dictKey` attachment.** — Ordinary Connections can also point at `dict:` keys, but a "contrast" or "often confused" entry's glosses are not this word's meanings; the attachment is the one edge that already means "this is my copy of that entry". Retyping a five-sense word by hand was the friction; `newLexicalFromEntry` only ever seeded gloss 1, and only at creation.
- 2026-08-06 — **A one-time copy, not an attachment: imported rows get fresh `meaning:<uuid>` ids and carry no sense id, no sense ordering and no provenance field.** — This is what keeps the feature clear of §14's deferred *dictionary-sense attachment or synchronization* and of the deferred content-history model, and what makes the identity rule hold: a dataset rebuild cannot reach a copy, so the item stays meaningful alone. A test asserts the saved record's key set contains nothing but the nine personal meaning fields.
- 2026-08-06 — **Labels cross only where the personal vocabulary already has the word, and anything else is named in the sheet as "Not carried across".** — `regionLabels` become `regions` (free text on both sides); the twelve-item closed `usageLabels` list takes exact matches plus `figuratively → figurative` and `literally → literal`; the five verb behaviors map exactly. `obsolete` is *not* `archaic` and `derogatory` is not `offensive`: bending them would put a word in the notebook the owner never wrote. Showing the dropped ones costs one line and makes the loss the owner's decision rather than the mapper's.
- 2026-08-06 — **Examples are not imported.** — The entry's examples are Tatoeba's and carry per-sentence attribution (§4) that a personal copy would strip, and they belong to the entry rather than to any one sense. Personal examples were always meant to be the owner's own sentences.
- 2026-08-06 — **The import appends; it never overwrites, reorders or edits an existing meaning, and a sense whose gloss the owner already has is shown disabled rather than hidden.** — Gloss matching is plain trim-and-case-fold on English, deliberately not `normalize.js`, which is the Spanish matching seam and must keep deciding only what the shipped entries match. Marking rather than hiding answers the question the sheet would otherwise raise — "why is that sense missing?" — and after an import the new rows mark themselves.
- 2026-08-06 — **Nothing is selected when the sheet opens.** — Importing all senses of a common verb would bury the owner's own rows under Wiktionary's taxonomy, which is exactly what the meaning-block amendment says `meanings[]` is not.
- 2026-08-06 — **The control appears only when the attachment resolves, and the component reads the entry without ever rewriting an aliased `dictKey`.** — "Not installed" is not "orphaned" (§5) and neither state can act, so neither shows a control; the orphan story stays `DictAttachment`'s to tell. That component owns the alias rewrite, and two components racing the same write is a bug waiting for the next dataset rebuild to trigger it.
- 2026-08-06 — **No schema change: `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 5, `meanings[]` already existed, and the import saves through the ordinary `patch` path so it logs exactly one `edit` event.** — Imported meanings are content, like tags and notes, and are edited afterwards by the existing Organize/Edit controls with no new code.
- 2026-08-06 — **Two of the five red/green proofs failed to redden, and fixing that changed the tests rather than the code.** — The "offers nothing when orphaned / when nothing is installed" tests asserted an absence immediately after render, so they passed against a component deliberately rigged to offer a stub import: they were racing the lookup, the same shape as the Phase 4c navigation test this log already records. They now mirror the component's own reads on the same reference connection before asserting, and both redden against the rigged build. Worth the entry because the first version looked green and proved nothing.
- 2026-08-06 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (889/889 across 77 files), the production build, `git diff --check`, five deliberate red/green proofs, and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout.** — The proofs: overwriting instead of appending, mapping `obsolete → archaic`, emptying the duplicate set, and rigging each of the two unresolved-attachment paths to offer a stub each reddened exactly the intended tests. The phone check ran a seeded four-sense `casa` against a personal entry that already held one meaning: the sheet opened with all four boxes unchecked and the confirm button disabled and reading "Import 0 meanings", `house` disabled as already held, chips rendering Mexico/colloquial/Spain/figurative/formal, and two "Not carried across" lines for `obsolete` and `derogatory`; importing two senses appended them after the owner's untouched meaning with fresh uuid ids, left `dictKey` unchanged, and logged one `edit`; editing an imported gloss afterwards kept its id, logged a second `edit`, and left the four dictionary senses byte-identical; no screen exceeded 375px and the console stayed clean. Fixtures were deleted afterward; no owner data was inspected and nothing was deployed.

## Phase 13 — drill modes and tracked performance (2026-08-06)

- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: reverses Phase 10c's "the drill is a separate ungraded pass" (line 505) and "the drill writes no events at all" (line 516). Drill answers are now graded and recorded.** — Both originals stand above as the reasoning that was true when the drill had not yet been used; having used it, the owner wanted the weak tenses findable rather than merely felt. The append-only log keeps them rather than rewriting them, so the reversal reads as a change of mind with a date on it.
- 2026-08-06 — **§14's deferral of "Collection practice history, grading, scoring, or scheduling" is read narrowly, as naming Collection Practice.** — The conjugation drill is a different feature with a different surface; the deferral's words are specific and this is not the thing they name. Recorded explicitly because Phase 10c's line 505 leaned on §14's *spirit* to justify staying ungraded, so the narrow reading is a decision rather than an oversight. Scheduling stays deferred under either reading: nothing here orders a deck by history or moves a review date.
- 2026-08-06 — **New `drill_pass` / `drill_fail` event types rather than reusing `review_pass` / `review_fail`.** — A Leitner box answers "do I know what this word means"; missing *pusieron* is a different fact about the same word, and sharing the types would make one box number mean two things. §7 allows new types freely and requires consumers to ignore what they do not know, so this needs no schema change: `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched at 5 and `backup.js` validates `type` as any non-empty string. (Written as "stays 4" when first committed, from a plan drafted before Phase 12 bumped it; corrected here rather than left as a wrong number in the record.) `review.test.js` now asserts the ignoring rather than assuming it — a drilled word gains no box, no enrolment and no lookup day.
- 2026-08-06 — **`itemKey` is the personal item id; the drill still logs no `view`.** — The form drilled comes from the replaceable reference layer, but the history is the owner's, so a dataset rebuild cannot delete it (the Phase 3a rule). No `view` because drilling a verb is not opening its detail screen and must not inflate the lookup counts that decide what Repaso enrols — the one part of line 516 that survives, and now asserted as an exact event set rather than an empty one.
- 2026-08-06 — **Every event records `mode` and `verdict` from the first one written.** — A self-reported "got it" and an exact string match are different measurements; blended into one accuracy figure they would mean neither. Same reasoning as Phase 10a's `direction` on review events: it cannot be reconstructed afterwards, so it is recorded before there is anything to reconstruct.
- 2026-08-06 — **The Phase 13a red/green proof found a real racing test, and the fix was in the test.** — Routing `logDrill` through the `view` type reddened the exact-event-set assertion as intended. Re-running then exposed that the grading helper asserted straight after `user.click`, which resolves when the handler is invoked rather than when its write settles: the suite had passed by winning a race. Waiting on the grade button's absence fixed it. `waitForElementToBeRemoved` was the wrong instrument — it throws when the element is already gone, which is the outcome being waited for — so the wait is a `waitFor` on the absence. The Phase 4c lesson, met again.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the drill asks one of two ways, chosen at the launch card and not remembered — reveal-and-self-grade, or type the form and be marked.** — Both are wanted for different moments: typing is the better practice and the objective signal, reveal is the faster one on a couch. The control is the Phase 10a direction control's pattern and its non-persistence argument: the useful default is the one you get by just tapping Drill. Shipped in the same sub-phase as typed mode, so the choice never offers an option that does nothing.
- 2026-08-06 — **`checkTypedAnswer` compares exactly first and only then through `normalize.js`, returning `exact | accents | wrong`.** — This deliberately inverts §8's "all matching goes through normalize" for one function, because normalize strips acute accents and in a conjugation the accent *is* the answer: `hablo` is present where `habló` is preterite, `hable` subjunctive where `hablé` is preterite. Marking one correct for the other would fail the drill at its own subject. Normalize survives as the *second* pass, telling a near miss from a wrong one — ~~worth passing where every accent is a phone long-press~~ **worth passing only when the tidied input is not exactly a different cell in that verb's paradigm**, and worth naming so it still teaches. ñ is a letter, not an accent, so `ano` for `año` stays wrong; a test pins that. The deliberate red/green: normalizing first reddens both accent tests with `expected 'exact' to be 'accents'`.
- 2026-08-06 — **A typed card is graded by the check, not by the owner: ~~passing covers `exact` and `accents`~~ passing covers `exact` and non-colliding `accents`, while an accent collision is `wrong`; no Got it / Missed it appears over a verdict.** — Offering self-grading would invite overruling the marker, which is the self-assessment bias typed mode exists to remove. The verdict and the typed attempt are both shown beside the right answer, because seeing the two together is what makes an accent slip legible as a slip rather than as a mystery.
- 2026-08-06 — **OWNER'S DECISION: `drill_pass` and `drill_fail` join `KNOWN_TYPES`, so a drill-only day holds the streak and colours the calendar.** — The calendar is owner-centric by the Phase 11 decision above: it records days the owner studied, not items that survived. Drilling is studying, so a day of it is not a gap. The deliberate red/green: dropping the two types reddens the drill-day test with `expected undefined to be 2`.
- 2026-08-06 — **Drill history is shown by tense, weakest first, and only once there is any.** — "Preterite is the shaky one" is actionable where a single overall percentage is not; sorting weakest-first puts the answer at the top rather than making the owner scan for it. Ties fall back to the busier tense, then the name, so the order does not shuffle between renders. The section is absent rather than empty before the first drill, because a panel explaining that it has nothing to say is worse than the space it takes.
- 2026-08-06 — **`drillPerformance` counts an answer with no readable tense in the totals but gives it no row.** — A later drill over something that is not a tense would otherwise invent a row labelled `undefined`. The totals stay honest; the breakdown stays about tenses.
- 2026-08-06 — **Sorting weakest-first is presentation, not scheduling.** — §14's deferral of review scheduling stands: nothing orders a deck by history, and no drill result moves a review date or a Leitner box. The owner reads the weak tense and decides what to do about it.
- 2026-08-06 — **Verified across three commits with the complete serial suite at each step (924/924 across 77 files at close), the production build, `git diff --check`, three deliberate red/green proofs, and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout.** — The proofs: routing `logDrill` through the `view` type reddened the exact-event-set assertion; normalizing before the exact comparison reddened both accent tests; dropping the two drill types from `KNOWN_TYPES` reddened the drill-day test with `expected undefined to be 2`. The phone check ran a seeded `sacar` through both modes on an origin verified empty first. Reveal logged one `drill_pass` carrying tense, slot, `mode: "reveal"` and `verdict: "self"`, and abandoning with nine cards unplayed left exactly that one event. A full typed deck produced all three verdicts against the real table, including the case that justifies the whole design: `sacó` typed as `saco` marked **accents**, where `saco` is itself the present-tense yo form — normalizing first would have marked the present correct for the preterite. Estadísticas read 91% of 11 answers with 2 accent slips, Present 5/6 before Preterite 5/5, every figure matching an independent recount of the raw log; the calendar cell read 12 events, and filtering the log down to drill events alone still returned a streak of 1, proving a drill-only day holds it in the app rather than only in a test. No screen exceeded 375px and the console stayed clean. Fixtures were deleted afterward and the origin left empty; no owner data was inspected and nothing was deployed.
- 2026-08-06 — **The drill's grade buttons measure 168×38px, matching `ReviewSession` and the shared `Button` rather than the 44px minimum used for icon-only controls.** — Recorded rather than silently changed: the 44px rule has been applied to bare icon targets like `SpeakButton`, while every text button in the app is `px-3 py-2`. Making these two taller alone would leave them inconsistent with the identical pair one screen over, so the choice was consistency; raising the shared component is a separate decision the owner can take across every screen at once.
- 2026-08-07 — **The post-push review corrected four breaches of already-recorded Phase 10/11/13 behavior: cloze reads only `SIMPLE_TENSES`, scheduled-session dictionary reads follow aliases, every persisted drill answer refreshes the app-level event snapshot, and growth walks through the newest populated week.** — These are corrections, not new product scope: perfect auxiliaries must not become answers; a replaceable dictionary id must not cost a card its reference material; persistent navigation must not leave event-derived statistics stale; and a future-dated imported item must not disappear when older vocabulary exists. Four regressions were first proved red against the reviewed code, then all 104 focused tests and the complete serial suite (928/928 across 77 files) passed, along with the production build and `git diff --check`; there is no schema, backup, event-shape, scheduling or stored-counter change. A disposable browser check reached a verified-empty origin, but the browser connection stalled before fixture setup, so no browser behavior pass is claimed.
- 2026-08-07 — **Entry cards on the three hub surfaces (home, words/phrases, pages) get a left edge colour tab per personal content kind, phrase titles go italic, and page titles go bold (800) with letter-spacing; the home list and Repaso stop showing tag chips.** — Owner-requested from a mockup, to make word/phrase/page cards recognisable at a glance without opening one. Two owner calls made up front: Repaso (which reuses `ItemCard`) also hides tags rather than diverging from home, and the page tab is one fixed colour rather than echoing the page's per-focus role colour, since the role pills already carry that signal. Three side-by-side tab/title treatments were shown on a disposable 375px page before any code changed; the owner picked the 6px tab with a rounded outer edge and the more widely tracked (0.035em) bold title. Purely visual — no schema, event, or component-structure change — so this skipped the plan-first loop per the visual-changes agreement.
- 2026-08-07 — **Word keeps `--color-pen` rather than a duplicate `--color-accent-word` value; phrase (`#7B93D1`) and page (`#B4860B`) are new tokens.** — Word's accent was already the app's blue; naming a second token at the same value would be the drift the tokens file exists to prevent, so `--color-accent-word: var(--color-pen)` aliases it instead. Page's gold is deliberately darker than `--color-hi` so the tab never reads as the tricky-word highlighter.
- 2026-08-07 — **Verified with the targeted suite (`ItemCard`, `Cuaderno`, `Repaso`, `LexicalHub`, `PageHub` — 78/78) and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout, not the full serial suite.** — A purely-visual change with no behaviour path touched by the other ~850 tests; computed-style checks on three seeded fixture entries (a five-tag word, the idiom phrase, and a long grammar-page title) confirmed each card's border-left width/colour, the phrase's italic title, the page's 800-weight/0.035em title on both the home and pages-hub cards, tag chips present on the words/phrases hub and absent on home, and no horizontal overflow. Fixtures were written directly into the dev database's `items` store and left in place — a local dev-only IndexedDB, not owner data.
- 2026-08-07 — **Entry cards on the three hub surfaces share a restrained two-layer shadow, chosen by the owner between the shown soft-diffuse and paper-lift treatments.** — The slight lower-right lift separates the white cards from the paper background without making every row look like a floating panel. One `--shadow-entry-card` token keeps Cuaderno, Words & phrases, and Pages visually identical; generic `Card` panels remain flat. Purely visual, with no component structure, behavior, data, schema, preference, backup, or event change.
- 2026-08-07 — **Verified the shared entry-card shadow with the affected component suite (78/78), the production build, `git diff --check`, and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout.** — Computed styles on visible Cuaderno, Words & phrases, and Pages cards all resolved the same 8% near layer plus 14% lower-right layer at 343px card width; the document remained exactly 375px wide with no horizontal overflow and the console stayed clean. The temporary lexical fixture was deleted afterward; the two pre-existing local page fixtures were left untouched.
- 2026-08-07 — **Repaso cloze reads only the final, verb-bearing token from a multi-word simple conjugation cell and searches the whole sentence for the exact vocabulary term before trying inflections.** — The reported `No sé qué decir para consolarte` card blanked `No` because every token of negative imperatives such as `no digas` had been admitted as a form of *decir*; object-pronoun cells such as `lo paso` exposed the same fault, while a first-match scan could choose the noun in `ayuda … ayudar` before the exact lemma. Three regression tests first failed against those behaviors, then passed after the correction. An audit of all 1,771 shipped conjugation tables found no leaked `no`/object-pronoun helpers and no stock example where an earlier inflection beat a later exact term; the focused suite passed 57/57, the complete serial suite 931/931 across 77 files, the production build and `git diff --check` passed, and no schema, dictionary-data, event, scheduling or storage change was needed. A disposable 375×812 browser check drove the real shipped *decir* entry: the unrevealed card read `No sé qué [blank] para consolarte`, reveal restored `decir`, the document stayed exactly 375px wide, and the console stayed clean. The lexical item and downloaded reference fixture were removed afterward; five fixture events remain on that disposable localhost origin because deleting an item deliberately retains its history, and no owner data was inspected or changed.
- 2026-08-07 — **Lexical grammar labels use English abbreviations everywhere; an attached dictionary noun contributes its gender to the personal detail heading, and the per-entry activity strip starts behind one collapsed Stats control.** — `n.` replaces Spanish `s.`, while dictionary-only categories use English forms such as `phr.` and `prop. n.` from one shared formatter. Gender remains replaceable reference metadata (`n. · f.` / `n. · m.`), not a copied personal field, so an uninstalled or orphaned attachment makes no unsupported claim. Stats still replays the same event and review derivations; collapsing it records nothing and leaves the tricky control immediately available. No schema, backup, event, scheduling or stored-data change.
- 2026-08-07 — **Verified the entry-header cleanup with the focused suites, the complete serial suite (936/936 across 78 files), the production build, `git diff --check`, and a disposable 375×812 browser flow against the shipped `ropa` dictionary record.** — Search and dictionary detail read `ropa n. · f.`; adding it produced the same personal heading, with the attached dictionary tile matching. Stats began at `aria-expanded=false` with no activity text in the DOM, expanded to opened/last opened/added/review state, and collapsed cleanly. The document had no horizontal overflow and the console stayed clean. The temporary lexical item and 22.7 MB dictionary were removed through the UI afterward; four deliberately retained create/view/delete history events remain on that disposable origin, and the pre-existing schema-4 origin was observed but never changed. No owner data was inspected or changed, and nothing was deployed.
- 2026-08-07 — **OWNER'S DECISION: Page cards replace their gold left stripe with the selected warm-folder treatment on both Cuaderno and the Pages hub.** — The owner chose the balanced middle option from three disposable phone-width variants: a pale cream body, muted manila tab, and gold outline. Pages are containers for Notes, Vocabulary, Source, and Grammar content, so the silhouette adds meaning while leaving word and phrase cards—and the shared entry-card shadow—unchanged. The tab is CSS decoration on the existing open-page button, with no component-structure, behavior, data, schema, preference, backup, or event change.
- 2026-08-07 — **Verified the Page-folder treatment with the affected component suite (78/78), the production build, `git diff --check`, and a disposable 375×812 browser closeout.** — Two pages created through the UI, including the 39-character “Subjunctive after expressions of doubt,” resolved to 343px-wide cards on both Cuaderno and the Pages hub. Computed styles confirmed the chosen cream body (`rgb(255, 250, 240)`), gold outline (`rgb(202, 168, 63)`), 122×18px manila tab, retained shared shadow, clean two-line title wrapping, and the tab itself hit-testing to the open-page button; `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375` and the console had no warnings or errors. The isolated port-5175 server was stopped afterward; its two local test pages remain on that disposable origin, and no owner data was inspected or changed.
- 2026-08-07 — **Page-folder cards reserve 8px of breathing room above the decorative tab, beyond merely clearing its overhang.** — The original top margin compensated for the 15px tab but left the same visible gap as ordinary cards, making the tab crowd the preceding entry. Increasing the shared CSS margin keeps the correction identical in Cuaderno and the Pages hub without changing markup or behavior.
- 2026-08-07 — **Verified the added Page-card spacing with the affected component suites (25/25), production build, `git diff --check`, and a disposable 375×812 mixed-card browser flow.** — A page between two lexical cards resolved to a 24px top margin and a 9px visible gap from the preceding card to the tab, while every card stayed 343px wide, `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375`, and the console remained free of warnings and errors. The disposable entries lived only on the fresh port-5180 origin; a separate legacy fixture origin was observed but left untouched.

## Phase 14 — Conjugation Gym (2026-08-07)

- 2026-08-07 — **OWNER'S DECISION: Phase 14 reverses the deferred-history ordering at the tail of Phase 13's §14 reading and its “presentation, not scheduling” decision only when the owner explicitly starts an Adaptive Gym session.** — History may rank that chosen deck; it never creates a background queue, reminder, due date or stored mastery score. No drill event enrols a word, changes a Leitner box or changes a review date.
- 2026-08-07 — **OWNER'S DECISION: Saved and Core conjugation performance is one lemma-level skill history.** — Every new answer records the exact resolved lemma and a `lemma:<NFC-lowercase>` `verbKey`, preserving accents and ñ. `verbKey` is a learning-group key only, never personal identity, dictionary identity or a link target. Saved/Core remains a filter describing where the card came from.
- 2026-08-07 — **Phase 13's personal-only `itemKey` rule is scoped for Core practice.** — Saved cards keep their personal id. A Core card carries one only when exactly one surviving personal lexical item is attached to the same resolved dictionary entry; zero or ambiguous matches use `null`, never a `dict:` key. Core writes only through `logDrill`, and practising it never creates or edits a personal item.
- 2026-08-07 — **OWNER'S DECISION: deleting a saved verb does not rewrite aggregate conjugation ability.** — Interpretable events remain in overall, tense, person, diagnostic and activity history, extending Phase 11's owner-centric exception to skill-centric performance. The deleted record leaves Saved coverage, current-verb rows, suggestions and action targets; a lemma still in Core can be practised only as Core. Legacy deleted events without a recorded lemma cannot invent a verb row.
- 2026-08-07 — **Core 20 and Core 50 are fixed lemma curricula, resolved through exact form-shard postings rather than hardcoded dictionary ids.** — A rebuild may change an id and the shipped alias map is not yet populated by real diffing. Exact lemma + verb + conjugation-table uniqueness is rebuild-safe and package-verifiable; a zero or ambiguous answer is named unavailable rather than guessed.
- 2026-08-07 — **Type becomes the Gym's session-only default; Reveal remains one tap away.** — The owner's headline is the last 50 objectively marked initial answers, so the stronger retrieval task must be the path of least resistance. Like Phase 13's control, nothing is remembered.
- 2026-08-07 — **Retries are recorded learning actions but never inflate first-attempt accuracy.** — An initial typed failure is persisted before one unrevealed retry; every initial miss may appear once in an optional end round. `stage` distinguishes them. All events continue colouring owner-centric activity, so a worst-case 20-card typed session can write 60 events and reach a calendar intensity threshold sooner; the day-based streak is unchanged. Personal-scale append-only history and per-write snapshot refresh remain deliberate.
- 2026-08-07 — **Phase 14 keeps schema v5 and adds no preference, stored counter or personal content type.** — The existing drill event types carry additive session, source, stable verb, stage and diagnostic metadata. Backups already preserve it byte-for-byte and allow null `itemKey`; every number is derived at render.
- 2026-08-07 — **Typed first attempts are the Gym's sole primary accuracy denominator; reveal self-grades, immediate retries and missed-round attempts are displayed as separate evidence.** — The distinction is encoded by `mode`, `stage` and `promptId`, not inferred from event order. A wrong initial response is persisted before its unrevealed retry, and the response text itself is never written. This keeps the headline honest while retaining recovery as useful learning history.
- 2026-08-07 — **Phase 14 is implemented locally across six code commits and one closeout commit; it is not deployed.** — The complete serial suite passes 975/975 across 83 files, the production build and `git diff --check` pass, and three deliberate proofs first failed on retry inflation, deleted-history aggregation and Saved/Core coverage de-duplication. A disposable 375×812 browser origin verified Quick, Focus and Adaptive setup, a diagnosed `sacar` failure plus exact retry, mood-qualified drill/stat labels, expandable person rows, both stats handoffs and zero horizontal overflow or console warnings. An independent raw-log recount found five typed initial attempts with two passes (40%) while two retries and one missed-round event were excluded, exactly matching the screen. The isolated fixture remains only on that stopped port-specific dev origin; no owner data was available or inspected.

## Phase 15 — Gym correctness, Focus depth and accent semantics (2026-08-09)

- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the reviewed Phase 15 direction is approved for implementation in its written delivery sequence.** — The phase keeps schema v5, the existing drill event types and owner-started sessions while correcting Gym measurements, turning Focus handoffs into real target-centred decks, and adding two exact-lemma reference-only pattern packs. No deployment is included.
- 2026-08-09 — **Phase 14 and every later feature named after it in README's Status section are deployed, not local-only.** — The Phase 14 closeout commit `bd75dcc` is an ancestor of `origin/main`; the Pages workflow deploys every push to `main`; and the live site returned 200 with a 2026-08-09 build whose app bundle contained Conjugation Gym, Core 20, Adaptive, Practice next and the missed round. The Phase 14 direction/report and those README labels now describe that verified state; the original closeout remains historically clear that it performed no deployment itself.
- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER'S DECISION / AMENDMENT: an accent-near answer passes only when its tidied spelling is not exactly another cell in that verb's paradigm.** — A true keyboard slip such as `comeriamos` for `comeríamos` remains a named `accents` pass. A tense-changing collision such as `hablo` for `habló` is instead a `wrong` verdict with diagnosis `accent_collision`, then follows the ordinary retry, missed-round, Error-pattern and Adaptive paths. This keeps the three-value verdict contract, event types, schema and primary denominator unchanged while making the tense measurement honest.
- 2026-08-09 — **Focus actions carry target dimensions into deck ordering instead of shrinking the selected verb/tense/person supply.** — An exact cell starts the deck, then expands across its verb and tense, then its verb and person, before balanced fill; a verb-, tense- or person-only action prioritizes that whole dimension. Everyday and all people remain the handoff defaults, an out-of-pack target tense is added while the target is active, and the target is visible and clearable. Setup derives the unique answerable-cell count and names a short deck before Start without blocking it. No setting or target is persisted.
- 2026-08-09 — **Adaptive's 40/30/30 structure now reads a bounded learner history: unresolved misses from the prior 90 days and rolling last-10 typed-initial accuracy per cell, tense and person.** — A later typed initial pass on the same cell resolves older failures between sessions, while prompt-id retry/missed recovery still resolves within a session and reveal misses still qualify. Lifetime exposure counts and latest-at timestamps continue to order exploration; the ≥3 and <80% weak threshold is unchanged. Three tests were written first and each failed for its intended old behavior—same-cell failure remained first, a four-month-old miss remained first, and a lifetime-weak/last-ten-perfect cell entered the first seven—then all three passed after the bounded derivation. The owner must still explicitly start Adaptive; no score, schedule or queue is stored.
- 2026-08-09 — **Stem changers and Irregular preterites are exact-lemma, reference-only Gym pools built on the existing Core path.** — The approved lists contain 17 and 16 lemmas respectively; overlaps with each other and Core 50 de-duplicate to 56 packaged curriculum lemmas. Every one resolves to exactly one conjugable verb entry and a shipped table in the current raw dictionary, and packaging now fails if a future rebuild breaks that promise. Pool events keep `source: "core"` and identify the selected pack through `curriculum: "stemChangers"` or `"irregularPreterites"`; no item, schema, event type or preference is added.
- 2026-08-09 — **Phase 15 is implemented and verified locally; it is not deployed.** — The complete final-tree serial suite passes 1,021/1,021 across 86 files, the production build and `git diff --check` pass, and a raw-package check resolves all 56 unique curated lemmas to exactly one conjugable entry and table. A disposable 375×812 origin using the shipped dictionary verified the five pool controls, target-centred Focus, the `hablo`/`habló` collision and retry, Error-pattern reporting, Adaptive's bounded-history copy and ordering, the entry-leave confirmation, Everyday stats defaults and row actions with no horizontal overflow or console warnings/errors. That pass exposed a Strict Mode library-load cancellation missed by the fixture tests; its new regression failed on the old lifecycle and passed after completion, rather than launch, became the reload marker. One earlier full run had an unrelated `AiCard` timing miss at 1,019/1,020; its file passed 8/8 immediately and both the subsequent 1,020-test run and the final 1,021-test run were green. The disposable dictionary and events were cleared and the server stopped; no owner data was available or inspected.

## Phase 16 — Review depth and one vocabulary-card engine (2026-08-09)

- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER'S DECISION: §1, §2, §3 and §5 of the Phase 16 direction are approved as written, and the written delivery sequence is approved for implementation.** — The owner explicitly chose four-grade scheduled review, objective typed vocabulary recall, an event-free scheduled recovery pass, and both Vocabulary-page practice modes. This task authorizes local implementation and verification, not deployment.
- 2026-08-09 — **Scheduled review exposes all four grades already reserved by §7: Again resets to box 1, Hard holds, Good climbs one, Easy climbs two capped at box 5.** — Hard is a `review_pass`, so it counts as a successful review and restarts the current interval without climbing. Retirement still requires a pass while already in box 5; Easy from box 4 lands in box 5. Replay reads only a valid numeric grade and maps malformed or missing legacy passes to Good and failures to Again, preserving every pre-Phase-16 box.
- 2026-08-09 — **`logReview` requires an explicit grade and remains the sole scheduled-review writer.** — Again chooses `review_fail`; Hard, Good and Easy choose `review_pass`; `metadata.grade` is applied last so caller details cannot overwrite it. The existing event types and append-only authority remain sufficient, so `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 5.
- 2026-08-09 — **Typed vocabulary recall uses only `checkTypedAnswer`'s exact/accent-aware verdict, never the Gym's paradigm-collision diagnosis.** — Reverse cards have the term as their deterministic answer and cloze cards have the gap; a plain term→meaning card stays reveal-and-grade because a free-text meaning has no honest automatic mark. A wrong typed scheduled answer immediately grades Again. Exact and accent-near success still ask Hard/Good/Easy because correctness is objective while effort is not. Scheduled events add only `mode: "typed"` and the verdict; the raw response is shown during the visit and never persisted.
- 2026-08-09 — **A scheduled missed-card round is recovery, not a second review.** — After the primary graded pass, cards graded Again may run once more in shuffled order using the session's reveal/typed mode and two local outcomes. It writes no event, moves no box and reports its own recovery count. Logging it would either double-move the ladder or make replay depend on session stage.
- 2026-08-09 — **More than 20 due words offers 10 / 20 / All, defaulting to 20; 20 or fewer keeps the control hidden.** — A chunk is the head of the existing most-overdue-first order. Completion re-derives the queue after writes settle and offers the next current chunk rather than persisting a deck, cursor or preference.
- 2026-08-09 — **Scheduled review and free practice share one vocabulary-card engine, while their shells keep different authority.** — Plain, reverse and cloze faces, reveal/context state, typed marking and grade/mark strips now have one implementation. Scheduled review owns event writes and box/reason metadata. Hub and Collection practice own in-memory Again/Got it and missed-only rounds. Session preparation may enrich cloze cards from the optional dictionary and verb tables, but a missing installation, stale key or failed read always falls back to a usable personal card.
- 2026-08-09 — **Hub free practice gains session-only direction, cloze and typed controls under the same card rules.** — Forward / reverse / mixed and Reveal / Type default to the existing familiar forward/reveal path and are not remembered. A typed miss becomes local Again and joins the existing missed round; no free-practice action enters the event log.
- 2026-08-09 — **A Vocabulary page keeps its in-place reveal skim and adds a separate launchable practice session over the whole Collection or one chosen group.** — The existing target icon remains the quick skim; a distinct primary play icon opens the full setup. Limits, shuffle/Collection order, direction, typed mode, incomplete-entry counts and missed rounds reuse the hub engine. Collection order is visible group order followed by ungrouped members. The entire session remains event-free, so §14's persistent Collection history/scoring/scheduling deferral stands.
- 2026-08-09 — **Phase 16 is implemented and verified locally; it is not deployed.** — Seven feature commits follow the approved order. Six deliberate proof batches failed against the missing behavior before passing. The complete serial suite passes 1,044/1,044 across 87 files, the Vite production build and `git diff --check` pass, and a disposable schema-v5 375×812 origin verified the 21-card chunk control, 44px four-grade grid, scheduled typed face, both Collection entry points and scopes, event-free Collection recovery, and hub typed cloze with no horizontal overflow or console warning/error. The event count moved only for opening the Collection (21→22) and stayed 22 through both free-practice flows. The generated fixture file was removed, the viewport reset, the tab finalized and the server stopped; no owner browser data was available or inspected.
- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER'S DECISION: Hard at box 5 holds the box rather than retiring the word.** — The direction's §1 carried an unnoticed ambiguity: "Hard → hold the current box" against "retirement requires a pass logged while already in box 5". Implementation resolved it toward any-pass-retires; on the post-implementation review the owner ruled the other way, because a word barely recalled after the 16-day interval has not earned leaving the queue. Ruled immediately after closeout, before any Hard-at-box-5 event existed, so the derived replay change rewrites no lived schedule. The new regression failed against the shipped rule and passed once Hard short-circuits before the retirement check; brief §7 and §12 and the direction's §1 carry the matching inline amendments.
- 2026-08-09 — **Phases 6 (first slice), 10 through 13, 15 and 16 are deployed, not local-only.** — Local `main` and `origin/main` are identical, the Pages workflow deploys every push, and the "Close out Phase 15" and "Close out Phase 16" runs both completed successfully (2026-08-10T00:07Z and 01:37Z). README's labels and both phases' direction/report status lines now describe that verified state; each closeout entry above stays as written because it was true of its moment. The Phase 15 status correction fixed only Phase 14 and later because that was all its review had flagged — this entry closes the rest of the gap.

## Phase 17 — Gym recognition lanes (2026-08-09)

- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER'S DECISION: implement the Phase 17 direction in its written delivery sequence.** — The request approves two owner-started recognition lanes, Tense usage and Endings, beside Forms; the curated wording remains correctable row by row without redesigning the mechanic. This authorizes local implementation and verification, not deployment.
- 2026-08-09 — **Usage and Endings are stable in-code reference content with stable card ids, not generated session text.** — Nineteen endings rows and 35 usage cards are pure constants. A shipped-data proof reads hablar, comer, vivir and haber directly from the packaged dictionary and stem/infinitive-strips every endings row; corrupting Present `-ar` `amos` to `emos` failed exactly that proof before restoration.
- 2026-08-09 — **One injectable-rng builder balances recognition decks by canonical tense and owns four distinct options per ask.** — Curated confusables fill first, also-acceptable answers are forbidden, option order changes per ask, consecutive equal answers and option sets are avoided where the supply permits, and a missed round preserves the same four choices while changing their order. Custom sessions require four selected tenses so the UI never needs an out-of-scope distractor.
- 2026-08-09 — **Recognition reuses `drill_pass` / `drill_fail` with `skill`, `cardId`, canonical `tense`, `mode: "choice"`, stage and a miss-only `chosen`; it carries no item, verb or slot identity.** — Reuse keeps schema v5 and the event vocabulary stable while additive metadata remains independently replayable. Form Performance has an explicit skill guard, Adaptive requires the absent verb/slot dimensions, and Leitner ignores drill types. Removing the Performance guard reddened its isolation proof; the restored final tree proves all three consumers unchanged.
- 2026-08-09 — **A recognition miss teaches immediately and does not retry immediately.** — The correct answer is already visible after a tap, so retrying would test short-term echo rather than recognition. The card advances normally and may return once in the optional reshuffled missed round; first-attempt and missed-round tallies remain separate in-session and in Performance.
- 2026-08-09 — **Recognition Performance is global across Saved/Core and filtered only by the existing tense packs.** — Recognition has no verb pool or source identity. The screen says so, reports Usage and Endings separately, places recognition and Forms counts together per tense, and derives directional `tense → chosen` confusion counts directly from misses.
- 2026-08-09 — **Grammar-guide links are derived from active Grammar-focused page titles through shared normalization, capped at two, with English and natural Spanish tense-title terms.** — The direction's success case requires a page titled “Pretérito” to match the persisted `Indicative/Preterite` key even though the app's display label is English. A small display-only alias list bridges that language difference without storing a personal id in reference content; no match renders nothing, and opening requires the same second-tap session-ending confirmation as other Gym links.
- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER-APPROVED content corrections from the post-implementation review: the ojalá and para-que usage cards gain tense anchors and accept the imperfect subjunctive, and the reveal note's dialect label only appears for the Preterite alternative.** — "After ojalá" alone was answerable with the imperfect subjunctive (ojalá tuviera), which sat in the card's own confusable set and could be offered as a distractor — exactly the ambiguity the one-correct-option rule exists to prevent; a bare "para que" had the same flaw against a past main clause. Both prompts now anchor to the still-possible/present-tense case and list `Subjunctive/Imperfect` as also acceptable, so it can never be offered. "Mexican Spanish note" was accurate for the Preterite alternative but wrong for the general-Spanish `[also: Future]` card; the label now reads "Also natural" except in the dialect case.
- 2026-08-09 — **Phase 17 is implemented and verified locally; it is not deployed.** — Six feature commits follow the approved sequence. The complete serial suite passes 1,073/1,073 across 93 files, the production build and `git diff --check` pass, and the two deliberate proofs above reddened as intended. A disposable 375×812 origin verified 301×44 choice chips, zero horizontal overflow, objective miss feedback, a 10-card Usage tally, reshuffled missed options, separate missed-round statistics, the Conditional whole-infinitive cue, the honest no-dictionary Endings reveal, per-lane/per-tense/confusion Performance, and a derived “Pretérito” guide link with its second-tap confirmation. The console had no warnings/errors; the fixture page was deleted, the viewport reset, the tab finalized and the server stopped. No owner browser data was available or inspected.
- 2026-08-09 — **Phase 17 and its review corrections are deployed.** — The push moved `origin/main` to the corrections commit (verified by matching SHAs) after the complete serial suite passed 1,074/1,074 across 93 files, and the Pages run for that push completed successfully. README's label and the Phase 17 direction/report status lines now describe that verified state; the closeout entry above stays as written because it was true of its moment.

## Tooling — serial-suite revisit (2026-08-08)

- 2026-08-08 — **REVISITED at the owner's request: `fileParallelism: false` stands, confirmed against fresh measurements rather than re-argued from the log.** — The question was whether the serial suite's cost had bought a negligible improvement. Measured today at 978 tests across 83 files: forcing parallelism back on (`npx vitest run --fileParallelism`) failed two of three runs, with 1, 4, and 7 spurious 5000ms timeouts respectively — no longer confined to `App.test.jsx`'s Phase 5a navigation test but spread across `App.test.jsx`, `ComposablePage.test.jsx`, `Cuaderno.test.jsx`, and `DetailAlias.test.jsx`. The contention has grown with the suite (827 tests at the original decision, 978 now), and a parallel run is now red more often than green, every red a false alarm. Serial runs took 226s and 271s against parallel's 79–85s, so the roughly 3.3× cost the decision accepted has held steady, and all six full-suite closeouts recorded since the change (928/928 through 977/977) passed. One caveat stays on record: one of three serial runs today reported a single uncaptured failure between two clean runs — the same signature the Phase 11 closeout logged on 2026-08-06 — so serial is not yet proven perfectly deterministic; if it recurs, capture the failing test's name before rerunning. If the duration ever genuinely bites, the experiment worth running is bounded parallelism (`maxWorkers: 2` with file parallelism restored), measured with the same flake-probing loop that produced today's numbers — not a plain revert.
- 2026-08-08 — **Verified compact page sections with the complete serial suite (978/978 across 83 files), the production build, `git diff --check`, a deliberate red/green proof and a disposable 375×812 browser flow.** — The proof forced every disclosure open and reddened the new empty-default test before the correct state was restored. The browser created a Grammar guide through the visible UI, added Source through Customize, and confirmed every empty top-level section was a 46px collapsed row; empty Grammar subsections were closed; adding content kept the populated guide open; its read card exposed one pencil and no delete button; the editor exposed Delete section; and no `Section 1 · 0 examples` label remained. The document measured `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 360` inside the 375px viewport and the console had no warnings or errors. The fixture was deleted through the UI and the disposable server was stopped; no owner data was available or inspected, and nothing was deployed.

## Plus-action labels drop the word "Add" (2026-08-08)

## Section spine (2026-08-08)

- 2026-08-08 — **OWNER'S DECISION: every page section's content hangs off a 2px vertical spine in `--color-section-spine` (#7B93D1), with a 6px node marking each Vocabulary group.** — The only signal that a long section was still going was the absence of the next header band, and absence is a weak cue mid-scroll. The line is placed 21px from the section's left edge so it is centred under the header chevron and reads as dripping from the band; content sits 16px right of it. Nodes were chosen over a second indent level for groups because a phone cannot spare the width: cards go from 327px to 304px at 375px as it is. The spine lives in `PageSectionDisclosure`, so Vocabulary, Source, Grammar, Notes, Connections, Tags and Media links all thread the same way and every trailing plus-action already inside a section is reached by the line. A collapsed section draws nothing, since its content wrapper is `hidden`.
- 2026-08-08 — **The spine colour is its own token rather than an alias of `--color-accent-phrase`, whose value it currently shares.** — Unlike `--color-accent-word`, which aliases `--color-pen` because the two are meant to stay in lockstep, a section's margin rule and a phrase card's left-edge tab are unrelated roles that should be free to move apart.
- 2026-08-08 — **Verified Section spine with the complete serial suite (993/993 across 86 files), the production build, `git diff --check`, a deliberate red/green proof and a disposable 375×812 browser flow.** — Removing the group node reddened the new spine regression before it was restored. The owner chose the treatment from three variants rendered side by side on a scratchpad page outside the repo, using a collapsed group, a 40-character group name over an empty state, and a long phrase with a long gloss. In the running app the content wrapper measured `border-left-width: 2px` in `rgb(123, 147, 209)` at `margin-left: 21px` and `padding-left: 16px`; all four group nodes centred on the line to within 0.1px and on their heading's first line to within 0.1px (4px low for the one wrapped heading); the spine's bottom edge met the trailing Group button's bottom exactly; cards measured 304px; and the document measured `scrollWidth === innerWidth === 375`. Collapsed sections resolved to `display: none`, drawing no line. The fixture was seeded into an empty personal database and cleared afterwards, the disposable server was stopped, no owner data was available or inspected, and nothing was deployed.
- 2026-08-08 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the spine moves in to 10px from the section's left edge, superseding the 21px chevron alignment decided the same day.** — Seen in the running app rather than the mockup, aligning the line with the header chevron read as too far inside the page; closer to the margin is what a ruled notebook does. The 16px gap between line and content is unchanged, so cards recover to 315px at 375px and the group nodes, whose offset derives from that padding, stay centred on the line without adjustment.
- 2026-08-08 — **OWNER'S DECISION: a page card's type moves onto the folder tab, and only the page's own focus is named there.** — The tab was empty decoration while the type pills took the card's first line, so a Grammar-and-Vocabulary page spent two pills on what its count line already said. The tab keeps the one manila gold on every card with the existing pastel role badge riding on it, rather than tinting per type: folders stay uniform and no colour has to survive being enlarged to tab size. Secondary roles are dropped from the card outright — `3 sections · 5 examples` means Grammar and `1 item · 0 groups` means Vocabulary, and the page itself carries the full picture. Which role is primary needed no new rule: `enabledPageRoles` already returns the saved `pageFocus` first, so the tab reads `roles[0]` with no stored state, no priority constant, and no untyped case. A tab deliberately does not re-label itself to match an active hub filter — it means one thing in every view. No schema, event, preference or backup change.

The same commit gives the Vocabulary and Grammar badge colours real tokens — they were bare hex inside `PageHubCard`, the drift the tokens file exists to stop — and collapses the label/icon/colour map and the count-line builder, which the two folder cards had each declared separately and had already let diverge, into one `pageRoleMeta` module. - 2026-08-08 — **OWNER'S DECISION: entry cards in Cuaderno, Words & phrases, and Pages lose their drop shadow, and visible list gaps become three-quarters of their prior size.** — The approved mockup showed that the cards' border, fill, lexical accent and page-folder silhouette already separate entries without added depth. Removing the shared shadow makes the hubs flatter; changing the 12px dedicated lexical-hub gap to 9px and the Cuaderno root's 10px gap to 7.5px increases scan density without touching internal card padding or spacing between pinned/index sections. Page folders are the special case: their overhanging 34px tab owns the visible gap, so the card's top clearance moves from 39px to 36.5px, reducing the space above the tab from 10px to 7.5px while preserving its 5px overlap with the card. Dictionary results already had no shadow and inherit only Cuaderno's tighter list rhythm, so personal and reference results remain visually distinct through their existing solid versus dashed borders.

Every personal card resolved `box-shadow: none`; all three surfaces held `scrollWidth === innerWidth === 375`, and the console had no warnings or errors. All four fixtures were deleted through the visible UI afterward, the temporary viewport was reset, no owner data was available or inspected, and nothing was deployed.

The folder treatment is the A5 combination: a die-cut sloped right edge on the tab (a skewed pseudo-element cap carrying border and background, so no clip-path and no lost outline), a faint top-light gradient with a hairline seam shadow so the tab reads as physically in front, and a sliver of white sheet peeking above the folder's mouth right of the tab. The back-panel variant was deliberately left out: three cues agree, four is a costume.
- 2026-08-08 — **Each page type's folder wears its chip family: near-paper body, pastel tab, family outline, tab label straight on the tab in the family ink.** — Vocabulary is the anchor and keeps the original manila trio unchanged, since the gold family is its chip colour; Notes, Source and Grammar each get their own body/tab/line trio as tokens (`--color-page-folder-notes`/`-source`/`-grammar` plus `-tab`/`-line`). The badge pill dissolved — on a tab tinted with the role's own family a pale pill vanishes into its background. The trio a card wears follows its primary role (`enabledPageRoles` first entry, the same rule that names the tab); a page with no role to name, the Diario entry in the mixed list, falls back to manila. Colour flows through two custom properties (`--folder-tab`, `--folder-line`) set inline on the card root and inherited by the tab and its pseudo-elements, so the CSS holds one declaration; gradient and shadow shades derive via `color-mix` from the trio rather than adding eight more tokens.

The cap's right edge (134px) clears the paper peek's 150px start on every card, no horizontal overflow at 375px, console clean. Fixtures deleted afterwards; origin left empty; not pushed.

## Hub titles fit by measurement, not character count (2026-08-08)

- 2026-08-08 — **OWNER'S DECISION: a hub card title holds 18px unless it genuinely does not fit, and steps to 17px when it does.** — `hubTitleSize(text)`, a `length > 18` character count returning a Tailwind class, was a guess about width rather than a measurement of it: at 375px a 22-character phrase needed 212px and had 269px, and shrank anyway. It is replaced by `useHubTitleSize`, which measures the title's natural single-line width in the face it actually renders in and compares it to the element's own width. Measurement uses a shared canvas 2D context rather than a probe element, so asking the question cannot itself trigger the reflow it exists to decide about, and — the load-bearing part — the measurement is independent of the size currently applied, which is what stops the two sizes oscillating. A `ResizeObserver` re-runs the fit when the viewport changes. Both hub cards move together: leaving `PageHubCard` on a character count would have meant two competing rules for one visual decision. The owner's earlier question — whether removing the bookmark would let long titles stay full size — was measured rather than assumed: the pin reserves 40px, which only a 30-character-plus title actually needs, so the pin stays and the guess goes.
- 2026-08-08 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (994/994 across 86 files, the known `App.test.jsx` flake included this run), the production build, and a 375px numbers pass on a verified-empty origin.** — Five phrase fixtures measured 8/18/22 characters holding 18px, a 30-character phrase stepping to 17px on one line, and a 46-character phrase at 17px wrapping to two, with `scrollWidth === innerWidth === 375`. The hook degrades to the full size in jsdom, which has neither `ResizeObserver` nor a canvas 2D context, and no test crashed on either absence. Vite HMR logged stale `hubTitleSize` export errors during the edit; `grep` confirms no reference remains and the production build is clean. All five fixtures were deleted afterwards (0 items remaining), no owner data was inspected, and nothing was deployed at verification time.

## Deeper paper (2026-08-08)

- 2026-08-08 — **OWNER'S DECISION: `--color-paper` deepens from #FAF9F4 to #F6F4EC; cards stay #FFFEFC.** — Chosen from a four-way comparison of card/paper pairings on real card content, per the visual-changes variant rule. The owner had asked whether a card colour existed between pure white and #FFFEFC; the honest answer was no — #FFFEFC is already the midpoint of that range, leaving only a one-channel step nobody could see. Separation therefore had to come from the other side of the pair. Deepening the paper roughly doubles the card-against-paper contrast (1.045 → 1.093) without touching a single card, and it is what makes the cards read as objects resting on a surface rather than as tinted regions of it. The deeper #F1EEE2 variant was shown and passed over: it reads as a distinctly older-paper notebook, which is an aesthetic commitment rather than a refinement.
- 2026-08-08 — **The paper token's two hand-mirrored copies moved with it.** — `--color-paper` cannot be referenced by the `theme-color` meta in `index.html` or by `background_color`/`theme_color` in `vite.config.js`, both of which are read outside the cascade, so all three changed together and the built `dist/manifest.webmanifest` was checked to confirm it emits #F6F4EC rather than trusting the edit. `docs/mi-cuaderno.jsx` still carries the old value and was deliberately left alone: the shared guide states it is historical, not current, and updating it would blur exactly the line it exists to draw.
- 2026-08-08 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (994/994 across 86 files, no flake this run), the production build, and a 375px numbers pass on a verified-empty origin.** — Five fixtures covering a lexical entry and all four folder families measured the app background at `rgb(246, 244, 236)`, the entry card holding 1.093 contrast against it and the folder bodies 1.058–1.075, with `scrollWidth === innerWidth === 375`. Worth watching on real content: the folder bodies are cool near-whites against a now-warmer paper, so they may read faintly blue; at this depth it appeared acceptable, and #F1EEE2 is where it would have demanded warming the four folder tints too. All five fixtures were deleted afterwards (0 items remaining), no owner data was inspected, and nothing was deployed at verification time.
- 2026-08-08 — **OWNER-REQUESTED: the entry card's meaning steps up again, 14px→15px, on both browsing cards.** — Against an 18px headword, and 17px where a title has stepped down, so the gloss stays subordinate in every case.
- 2026-08-08 — **BUG, found while verifying the above: `useHubTitleSize` measured once at mount and never again, so the fit check it exists to perform did not run at all in normal use.** — Every hub mounts inside a `display: none` route, so at mount the title's `clientWidth` is 0; the fit bailed on that (correctly, since a size measured against nothing is meaningless) but was keyed to `[text, weight, letterSpacing]`, none of which change when the route becomes visible, so it never asked again. Long titles therefore held full size regardless of fit — a 30-character phrase rendered at 18px in 254px of space. **The commit that introduced this (`af23673`) was verified green, and that verification was wrong**: that session clicked a filter after opening the hub, which re-mounted the cards while visible and made the measurement run. The feature never worked on a plain first visit. Two corrections: the fit now runs on every render (`useLayoutEffect` with no dependency array, the body wrapped in `useCallback`), because the render that matters is the one where the route stops being hidden, and settling on the same size is a no-op since React discards an identical state update; and the `ResizeObserver` is replaced by a `window` `resize` listener, because the observer was attached to an element with no box and never delivered once it had one.
- 2026-08-08 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (994/994 across 86 files, no hang or loop from the dependency-free effect), the production build, and a 375px pass on a verified-empty origin.** — On a clean first visit with no filter interaction — the exact path that was broken — `madrugar` held 18px and `me da igual lo que digan de mí` correctly stepped to 17px at 269px available. Widening to 430px returned it to 18px at 324px available, narrowing restored 17px, and five further resize events changed nothing, so it settles rather than oscillating. Temporary `window.__fitDebug` instrumentation was used to prove the effect body never executed, and `grep` confirms it is gone. Note for future browser checks: the preview tool's viewport override does not fire a `resize` event, so that path needed an explicit `dispatchEvent`; a real rotation does fire it. Both fixtures were deleted afterwards (0 items remaining), no owner data was inspected, and nothing was deployed at verification time.

## Page sections wear their own family colour (2026-08-09)

- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER-REQUESTED: a section inside an open page takes its heading and spine colour from the page type it represents — Notes blue, Vocabulary gold, Grammar purple, Source green — and the sections belonging to no page type take the app's red.** — Keyed by section rather than by page, because one page shows several: a Vocabulary page with Notes enabled has a gold Vocabulary section and a blue Notes section, and each should say which kind of content it holds. The four typed families reuse the chip and folder-line values their folders already wear, so a Grammar section and a Grammar folder are the same purple rather than two purples that drift. `sectionFamily()` in `pageRoleMeta.js` is the single map; `PageSectionDisclosure` takes a `family` prop defaulting to Notes, which is what every section wore before, and `SectionSpineNode` takes the same so the line and the nodes hung on it cannot disagree.

Second, the request said heading and spine, but recolouring only the heading text would have put gold type on the existing pen-pale blue band; the band's fill and outline move to the family too, matching the folder tabs. Either is a small revert if the owner disagrees.
- 2026-08-09 — **`--color-section-spine` and `C.sectionSpine` are deleted.** — Each family now supplies its own spine, leaving the token with no consumer; a palette entry nothing reads is exactly the drift `index.css` exists to prevent. Its value (#7B93D1) survives as the Notes family's folder line, which is what the spine had always matched.
- 2026-08-09 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (994/994 across 86 files), the production build, and a 375px pass on a verified-empty origin.** — One fixture page with all four structures plus tags, a media link and a connection rendered all seven sections at once, each resolving its own band, border, heading ink and spine: Notes `rgb(36, 63, 133)` on `rgb(237, 241, 250)` with a `rgb(123, 147, 209)` spine, Vocabulary `rgb(105, 91, 39)`/`rgb(202, 168, 63)`, Source `rgb(62, 107, 68)`/`rgb(121, 161, 127)`, Grammar `rgb(87, 70, 118)`/`rgb(156, 136, 198)`, and Connections, Tags and Media links all `rgb(179, 64, 46)` on `rgb(247, 236, 234)`. The Vocabulary group's spine node sampled the same gold as its spine, and the document held `scrollWidth === innerWidth === 375`. Both fixtures were deleted afterwards (0 items remaining), no owner data was inspected, and nothing was deployed at verification time.

## Repaso stats become one chalkboard (2026-08-09)

- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER-PICKED: the four Repaso stat tiles (day streak / items / opens / tricky) merge into one classroom chalkboard — green slate (#2F4038) in a two-tone wood frame whose bottom edge is a distinct 42px chalk tray holding two chalk sticks and a felt eraser, headed "Mis estadísticas" over a dashed chalk rule.** — Chosen through four mockup iterations in chat (green vs near-black slate, shallow vs deep tray, eraser styling); the green board sits better against the warm paper page. The 2×2 layout survives inside the board for the same 375px reason the tiles used it. All fourteen new colours are `--color-chalk-*` tokens in `index.css` with `C.*` handles; decoration is `aria-hidden` while the numbers stay ordinary text.
- 2026-08-09 — **The chalk faces are self-hosted npm fonts, not a CDN link.** — Gloria Hallelujah (numbers) and Caveat (labels, heading) via `@fontsource`, latin subsets only, imported in `main.jsx`; a Google Fonts stylesheet would break the offline PWA. `vite.config.js` adds `woff2` to the precache globPatterns so the service worker keeps the three font files (~60 KiB) offline with the rest of the shell.
- 2026-08-09 — **Verified: Repaso + App component tests (41/41), production build with 3 woff2 assets precached, and a 375px pass — board colours, both fonts resolved via `document.fonts.check`, dashed heading rule, 42px tray, `scrollWidth === 375`.**

## The review ladder joins the chalkboard (2026-08-09)

The count digit takes the same tone as its bar so the two read as one mark.
