## Actividad becomes a planner page with a trend view (2026-08-09)

- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER-PICKED: the 16-week heatmap strip is replaced by two views behind one toggle — a month calendar (default) and a trailing-16-week trend — chosen from three mockup rounds in chat.** — The strip was answering two questions at once and neither well: which day was which, and how the habit is holding. Splitting them lets the month page be a page the owner recognises (`‹ agosto 2026 ›`, day numbers, weekday columns) while the trend keeps the long view. Per-day tap-through detail was mocked up and rejected by the owner as something they would rarely open, which is also what freed the month cells from needing 44px tap targets.
- 2026-08-09 — **Activity on the month page is a hand-drawn pen circle in three weights, not a filled cell; the five heat levels collapse to three (1–2 light `heat-2`, 3 `pen`, 4 `pen-dark`).** — The month view's question is "did I show up", and a person marking a wall calendar circles the day rather than colouring the square. Three weights is what one pen can distinguish at a 34px cell; the five-level gradient survives untouched in the trend view, which is where the owner said they would look for volume.
- 2026-08-09 — **Today is a red rule under the number, not a red circle.** — The activity mark is already a circle, and two concentric circles on the busiest cell of the page read as one smudged mark. A different gesture in a different colour cannot be confused with ink.
- 2026-08-09 — **The circles are CSS divs with irregular `border-radius` and a small rotation, keeping the divs-only calendar rule (2026-07-31).** — Three wobble shapes indexed by day number, so the same date always looks the same and no two neighbours look identical. SVG would have bought smoother curves at the cost of the per-cell `title`/`aria-label` that the grid gives for free.
- 2026-08-09 — **`monthGrid` always returns six rows, and paging clamps to [first month with activity, current month].** — A grid that resized as the owner paged would move the whole screen under it; an arrow that never disables invites hunting through blank paper from before the cuaderno existed. `earliestActivityMonth` is the lower clamp.
- 2026-08-09 — **The trend view is columns of day-dots, not three small calendars.** — The owner's framing: at trend zoom the calendar part carries nothing while the streak shape carries everything. One dot per active day means a full column is a perfect week, and the dots stay tinted by `heatLevel` so the gradient the strip used is not lost.
- 2026-08-09 — **`Segmented` moves from `ConjugationGym.jsx` into `theme.jsx`.** — Second consumer, unchanged markup; two screens with two different-looking toggles is exactly the drift the theme file exists to prevent.
- 2026-08-09 — **Verified: dates/stats/Estadísticas/Gym tests green, the complete serial suite, the production build, and a 375px pass on the dev origin with 35 seeded events across three months.** — Ink weights resolved 1.5px `rgb(147,169,219)` / 2px `rgb(45,78,160)` / 2.5px `rgb(36,63,133)`, today's rule `rgb(179,64,46)`, 42 cells at 34px, nine labelled days in a nine-day-old month, paging through julio and junio with the back arrow disabling at junio, the trend view showing 16 week columns and four distinct dot tones, and `scrollWidth === innerWidth === 375` in both views. The paging clamp was broken on purpose first and the test went red. Seeded events were deleted afterwards (1 pre-existing event remaining), and nothing was deployed.

## Crecimiento becomes a hand-plotted graph (2026-08-09)

- 2026-08-09 — **OWNER-PICKED: the growth chart keeps its cumulative question and its geometry, and is redrawn as a graph plotted by hand — dashed graph-paper grid, an unsteady ink line, a mark on every week, and the running total riding above the last point.** — Picked from three variants shown against three realities (a two-week history, a single point, and 104 weeks). Bars and a words/frases split were mocked up and set aside: the Actividad trend view already answers "how consistent have I been", and two weekly-column charts one after the other would have read as the same chart twice. Cumulative stays the complementary question — how far the cuaderno has come.
- 2026-08-09 — **The pen-pale area wash is gone.** — It was the only thing giving a two-point chart any body, but at two years it becomes a large flat field that dominates the card, and the graph-paper rules give the plot its structure without filling it. `C.penPale` now has no consumer in this file.
- 2026-08-09 — **The total moves off the axis and onto the last point; the top dashed rule goes with it.** — On a cumulative line the top rule and the total are the same number, so the axis was labelling the obvious and holding a left gutter to do it. Anchored `start` beside the point, flipping to `end` within 40 units of the right edge so the label cannot leave the viewBox — the case the existing viewBox test already guards, re-verified by breaking it.
- 2026-08-09 — **The line wobble is derived from the point index, not random, and its amplitude falls as the weeks crowd (1.6 → 1 → 0.5 units past 12 and 40 points).** — A fixed wobble at two years reads as fur rather than as a hand, and a random one would redraw the line differently on every render. The first and last points stay exact because the caption quotes their numbers.
- 2026-08-09 — **Week marks are drawn only up to 26 points.** — Past that they merge into the line and thicken it into a smudge; the line carries the shape alone from there.
- 2026-08-09 — **Verified: Estadísticas tests (27/27), the complete serial suite, the production build, and a 375px pass on the dev origin across all three shapes.** — 21 weeks drew 12 grid lines (11 dashed, solid baseline), 21 marks, a 1.8px `rgb(45,78,160)` line and no area path; 62 weeks dropped the marks to 0; one word rendered `M34.0 24.0` with no NaN and the total anchored `start` at x=40. Every label sat inside the viewBox in all three, and `scrollWidth === 375` throughout. Fixtures deleted afterwards (0 items, 1 pre-existing event remaining), and nothing was deployed.
- 2026-08-09 — **Flaky Collection bulk-add test fixed by stamping fixture items with distinct explicit timestamps, not fake timers.** — Back-to-back `createItem` calls can share a millisecond, and equal `updatedAt` values make the Add-vocabulary sheet's recency ordering fall back to IndexedDB's random `user:<uuid>` key order, so the two staged phrases swapped between runs. The order-sensitive assertion stays (group order is meaningful for Collections); the input is now deterministic, per the suite's no-fake-timers rule.
- 2026-08-09 — **Two owner-flagged visual bugs fixed: the Actividad today-rule and the Verb Pool segmented labels.** — The today-rule (red underline) sat at the same vertical band as the rotated ink oval's bounding box, so it read as cut by the circle; moved it 3px lower (clear of the oval) and gave it `zIndex: 1` as a paint-order guarantee. `Segmented` button labels had no line-break allowance, so the unbroken word "Irregular" in "Irregular preterites" pushed past the button and container borders at 375px; added `break-words text-center` and trimmed padding. Verified in the running app (375px, seeded fixture dictionary): no bounding-box overlap remains at the today cell, and every Verb Pool button's `scrollWidth` matched its rendered width with the group staying within its 359px box. Estadísticas and ConjugationGym suites stayed green (38/38).

All ten pages were deleted through the visible UI, the temporary viewport was reset and the isolated server stopped; the disposable origin's append-only create/view/delete events remain, no owner data was available or inspected, and nothing was deployed.

## Tooling — bounded Codex subagents (2026-08-10)

- 2026-08-10 — **Codex may use at most two subagents only for substantial work that splits into at least two independent read-only investigations; the primary agent retains all writing and final verification.** — Phase audits, requirement/code checks, test-coverage reviews and independent failure hypotheses can benefit from parallel context, while this project's shared checkout, sequential feature commits, tightly coupled data boundaries and deliberately serial full suite make parallel writers or competing test runs more risky than useful. Model selection remains unpinned so Codex can balance task difficulty, speed and cost; project-specific agent profiles stay deferred until repeated evidence justifies them.

One shared `StudySessionFrame` now owns the quiet Finish/title/count row, animated progress rule, scrollable teaching area and bottom thumb-zone action dock across scheduled review, free practice, Forms, Tense usage choice/recall, and Endings choice/production. Short cards center in the remaining space; long answer content scrolls above actions that stay put.

## Phase 19 — Page organization and formatting (2026-08-10)

- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-APPROVED: Phase 19 begins with the observed Indicative/Subjunctive Grammar-guide friction and remains the umbrella for later related Page organization/formatting increments.** — The grouping avoids inventing a new phase number for every closely related Page refinement. It is not blanket scope approval: each later behavior still needs an owner-approved concrete boundary, decisions, verification and any independently required schema/export-first path.
- 2026-08-10 — **Grammar section Overview adopts the existing safe notebook Markdown dialect without renaming its stored `explanation` string; Grammar blockquotes become accessible labeled Note callouts only in that reader.** — This supplies headings, lists and emphasized cautions without a new content field or general block model. Search consumes only visible text, while Key idea, Pattern, example language and example notes stay plain. The formatting slice can ship on schema v5.
- 2026-08-10 — **Schema v6 adds mandatory `parentId: null | grammar-section:<uuid>` to the existing flat Grammar section array and permits exactly one subsection level.** — A flat same-page parent reference preserves current section/example IDs and keeps cleanup, search and mutation loops iterative rather than recursive. Existing sections migrate to roots with null; self, dangling, cross-page, cyclic and child-of-child parents are invalid.
- 2026-08-10 — **Grammar section names become sibling-unique instead of guide-wide.** — A comparison can naturally contain `Overview` or `Examples` beneath both Indicative and Subjunctive, while normalized duplicates beside one another remain ambiguous and blocked. Array order remains sibling order; mutation saves use canonical depth-first order without rejecting an otherwise valid interleaved backup.
- 2026-08-10 — **The owner selected continuous guide spine treatment A.** — Lightweight subsection nodes continue the existing page/Grammar spine instead of nesting a second padded card, preserving useful width at 375px. Top-level collapse hides the mounted child subtree; children retain independent disclosure state, long names wrap and every action stays at least 44px.
- 2026-08-10 — **The v5→v6 migration and backup upgrader are pure additions that clone Grammar structures before forcing every legacy section to `parentId: null`.** — The export-first gate must return the untouched validated source envelope; mutating nested objects while validating/upgrading would silently contaminate the owner's recovery file. Schemas 1–6 validate and upgrade sequentially, v6 round-trips exactly, and newer versions remain blocked.
- 2026-08-10 — **Hierarchy-aware organization remains explicit and atomic.** — It can reorder siblings, promote/reparent subsections and move examples, but blocks grandchildren, a parent becoming a child while it owns children, and deletion while children or examples remain. Changed saves log one page `edit`; Cancel/no-op and automatic dependent cleanup remain event-free.
- 2026-08-10 — **Named Grammar structure is never summarized as Empty merely because its content fields are blank.** — Starter and copied section names are meaningful organization and must remain discoverable from the collapsed guide header. Grammar disclosure, Page cards and Customize Page distinguish roots, subsections and examples while omitting zero categories.
- 2026-08-10 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (1,145/1,145 across 99 files), the production build, `git diff --check`, four deliberate red/green proofs and two disposable 375×812 browser origins.** — Visible creation covered formatted Overview search, a labeled Note, child creation/examples, independent disclosure, reparent/promote, fresh-ID structure copy and phone geometry. A raw schema-v5 fixture remained behind the export-first gate until backup request and acknowledgement, upgraded from IndexedDB v50 to v60 without content loss, accepted a new child, exported, wiped and restored 1 Page plus 2 events with its hierarchy intact. Both origins measured `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 360` inside `innerWidth === 375`, logged no warnings/errors and were cleared afterward. No owner browser data was available or inspected, and nothing was pushed or deployed.

### Structured Notes increment (owner-approved 2026-08-10)

- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-APPROVED: every Page keeps `body` as its permanent Notes Overview and schema v7 adds a flat `noteSections[]` outline with exactly one subsection level.** — The owner needs durable named organization on Notes-led Pages and inside Vocabulary, Source and Grammar Pages without converting existing prose or introducing an enable switch, arbitrary blocks or recursive trees. Each row stores a stable `note-section:<uuid>`, same-page `parentId`, trimmed sibling-unique name and safe-Markdown body.
- 2026-08-10 — **A nonempty Notes outline is durable Page organization for the Pages/Diario boundary; body length alone remains irrelevant.** — A dated record is Diario only when Vocabulary, Source and Grammar are disabled and `noteSections[]` is empty. The deliberate asymmetry means a long dated body can remain a journal moment while even one named outline section keeps the record in Pages: the name establishes reusable organization rather than merely more prose. Dating an outlined Page, importing one, or disabling its last optional capability must keep it in Pages; deleting its final outline section warns and lets canonical routing move an otherwise unstructured dated record to Diario. Diario gains no outline editor in this increment.
- 2026-08-10 — **Grammar and Notes share one parameterized one-level hierarchy engine and structural organizer controls.** — Their IDs, content fields and domain wording remain distinct, and Grammar alone keeps example movement, but hierarchy reading, canonicalization, sibling movement, reparenting, breadcrumbs and parent/name validation must not drift into parallel implementations or grow into a universal block model.
- 2026-08-10 — **Confirmed deletion of a leaf Notes section removes its own Markdown body; a root that owns subsections remains protected.** — The body is the section's own prose, unlike a Grammar example, which is an independently structured child and still must move or be deleted first. This difference is intentional rather than an omitted guard.
- 2026-08-10 — **Schema v7 is narrow and fenced.** — V1–v6 backups must reject a premature `noteSections` field, v7 requires and deeply validates it, and the pure v6→v7 migration adds only an empty array to each Page. No speculative future Page fields are batched merely to avoid a later export-first gate; another concrete schema change can join v7 only if separately approved before implementation begins.
- 2026-08-10 — **Notes-section search remains Tier 6 with the explicit reason “in a Notes section”; Page-card labels distinguish guide sections from note sections and may wrap at phone width.** — Section bodies search their visible Markdown projection, not syntax. Context-specific disclosures keep shorter labels, while mixed Page summaries trade a little height for unambiguous retrieval rather than truncating counts.

## Phase 20 — Global tag management (2026-08-10)

- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-APPROVED: Ajustes becomes the one global home for exact tag rename, merge and removal across words, phrases and all Pages, including Diario entries.** — The prior 2026-08-05 deferral is now resolved as its own phase. Tags remain exact strings on items: normalized lookalikes are suggested but never rewritten together, rename to an unused destination preserves position, and rename to an existing destination becomes an explicitly confirmed one-source merge whose overlap keeps the destination's position. Multi-source merge, aliases, stable IDs and a stored tag registry remain outside scope.
- 2026-08-10 — **Global tag maintenance is timestamp-neutral but still writes one ordinary `edit` per actually changed item.** — The owner chose to preserve Recently touched order and Diario's Continue choice. Tags remain content, so their event history and existing owner-activity consequences stay honest; there is no batch metadata or new event type. Items, events and the tag-colour preference share one transaction so any failure rolls the whole operation back.
- 2026-08-10 — **Rename carries the source colour; merge keeps the destination colour including Plain; removal permanently deletes the source colour key.** — The destination is the surviving identity during a merge, and explicit deletion should not let an obsolete colour reappear if the spelling is recreated later. Merge and removal require confirmation and offer the existing validated backup download as an optional, non-gating safety action.
- 2026-08-10 — **Phase 20 keeps schema v6 and the current backup shape.** — Existing `tags[]`, `edit` events and the backed-up `tagColors` preference already hold every durable result. Persistent undo, saved tag views and activity grouping would require separate product decisions rather than hidden storage added for this operation.
- 2026-08-10 — **Verified with the complete serial suite (1,168/1,168 across 101 files), the production build, `git diff --check`, explicit rollback/backup proofs and a disposable 375×812 browser origin.** — The visible flow renamed a two-entry tag to a deliberately long exact spelling, merged it into a 2-entry destination with one overlap, then removed the 3-entry survivor. Event counts advanced 6→8→10→13, source/destination colour ownership held, a stale Cuaderno filter widened safely and recreating the removed spelling started Plain. Settings and sheets measured 360/360 inside `innerWidth === 375`, every action stayed 44 px, no element overflowed and browser logs contained no warnings/errors. All fixture records were deleted afterward; no owner browser data was available or inspected, and nothing was pushed or deployed.

The quiet state deliberately uses no family ink, so the owner's planned recolour of the neutral family will not touch it. Purely visual — no behaviour, markup semantics, storage or schema change.
- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-PICKED: the page families retint to southwest earth tones — marigold Vocabulary, sage Source, dusty-mauve Grammar — while Notes keeps denim blue and the base layer (paper, ink, pen, highlighter) stays untouched.** — Chosen as the families-only panel from a four-panel scratchpad sampler (current / desert families / full adobe with terracotta pen / full adobe with turquoise pen). Families-only is the low-regret depth: reversible token edits that carry forward unchanged if the owner later goes to a full adobe base. If the base swap is ever taken, turquoise beats terracotta for the pen — a terracotta accent sits dangerously close to the Again/danger red on the grade row.
- 2026-08-10 — **Source stops borrowing the semantic success green: `--color-role-source-pale`/`-ink` are its own pair, wired through `PAGE_ROLE_META` and `SECTION_FAMILIES`.** — With Source going sage, keeping `greenPale`/`green` would have coupled a page family to the error/success layer; success green stays reserved for Easy grades and confirmations.
- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-PICKED: neutral page sections (Connections, Media links, Tags) drop the error red for driftwood gray — new `--color-section-neutral-band`/`-spine`, with border and ink reusing `--color-line`/`--color-mut`.** — Chosen over "neutral wears the pen" because Notes sections already wear pen blue and the two would have been indistinguishable; gray reads as chrome rather than a fifth family, and red returns to meaning only danger.
- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-PICKED: Diario's identity surfaces wear a new clay accent (`--color-diario*`) — the Today card, JournalHome's pen/clock/sparkle/archive icons and the editor's writing-prompt card. Navigation, links and buttons on those screens stay pen blue.** — The Diario tab was the only major surface with no colour of its own, dressed throughout in borrowed pen blue. An earlier idea, clay folder cards for Journal entries, was dropped because journals are excluded from empty-query Cuaderno browsing, so the folder treatment is almost never seen; the accent lives where the diary actually lives. Clay marks the diary, not the app: chrome stays pen so these screens still read as the same app.
- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-PICKED: the tag swatches retune to the talavera set and grow to eleven — the eight ids keep their identity with desert values (denim, cactus, chile, cempasúchil, bougainvillea, talavera, driftwood), and clay, leather and espresso join as new browns.** — Chosen as the brighter of two scratchpad retunes (dusty vs talavera): the families whisper, so tags can be small folk-art accents. Espresso's ink is the darkest owner-pickable colour in the app (#2C1B15, owner-picked over two lighter roasts); the browns separate by character — yellow, saturated orange, desaturated tan, near-black coffee — because hue alone does not survive a squint. Ids are the stored identity, so every previously assigned tag colour follows automatically and no data migrates. Caveat, accepted: a backup whose `tagColors` names clay, leather or espresso will not validate on a pre-desert build of the app.
- 2026-08-10 — **Tokenized the stray hardcoded hex/rgba literals flagged during the desert-theme work: `--color-on-accent`, `--color-scrim`, `--color-dot-grid`, `--color-hi-border`, `--color-hi-ink` and `--color-hi-pale` join the palette, and DictionaryCard's `#F7ECEA` plus SchemaUpgradeGate's near-duplicate `#F8E8E4` both consolidate onto the existing `--color-red-pale`.** — Same rule as the 2026-08-05 chip-border/danger-border/disabled naming: components had already converged on identical values independently (the sheet scrim in six files, the tricky-word border/ink in two), which is exactly the drift `index.css` exists to catch before a future palette or base-theme change would have to hunt them down by hand. No rendered pixel changed except the two consolidated red-pale sites, which move by less than a shade. Mechanical naming, not a new colour choice — no scratchpad variants needed.
- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-PICKED: uniform 48px rhythm between a page's structured sections.** — From three scratchpad scales (36/48/64px) shown against the current 20px-then-28px spacing, the owner chose 48px (Tailwind `12`) everywhere from the header card through the Notes outline and each enabled Source/Grammar/Vocabulary section, evening out the previous inconsistency where the first gap was tighter than the rest. The Connections/Media links tail keeps its own already-approved 44px hairline rhythm, untouched. Purely visual — no behaviour, markup semantics, storage or schema change.
- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-MARKED: three lexical-entry action labels trim down, from screenshots the owner marked up.** — The Connections "add link" button (Detail.jsx and CollectionPage.jsx's visible-text, non-empty-state variant) drops "something"/"something related" to just "link"; the empty-state icon-only header action keeps its fuller aria-label unchanged, since it carries no visible text for the shortening to apply to. The Meanings "Organize meanings" trigger shortens to "Organize" (the organizer sheet's own heading, once opened, still reads "Organize meanings"). "Add to Collection" becomes an icon-only `IconButton` with `aria-label="Add to Collection"` — same accessible name, so nothing behavioural changed — with `mb-3` added so the button doesn't crowd the collection list once it grows past a bare `+`. All three are Button→IconButton/text-only copy changes; no behaviour, data or component structure moved.
- 2026-08-10 — **OWNER-REQUESTED: Grammar guide's nested spines moved left, indentation reduced.** — A root section's card sat ~16px right of the Key idea card and a subsection's dot-marker sat off-centre on its own spine; `.grammar-guide-spine`/`.grammar-guide-root::before` and `.grammar-guide-subsections`/`.grammar-guide-subsection::before` in `src/index.css` now use roughly half the previous margin/padding, so a root section sits ~9px right of Key idea (was ~16px) and each connector still touches its spine exactly (measured via computed styles at 375px: root tick overlaps its spine border, subsection dot centres on its spine border within 0.04px). Purely visual — no behaviour, markup semantics, storage or schema change.

Verified at 375px: all six converted pencils plus the two reference ones measure `border-style: solid; border-width: 0px` and a transparent background at 44×44, with no overflow; `SourceSection`, `GrammarSection`, `StructuredNotesSection`, `MeaningsSection` and `Detail` test files pass 58/58. Purely visual — no behaviour, markup semantics, storage or schema change.
- 2026-08-11 — **OWNER-PICKED: Notes' header actions get a compact softened treatment, in-content add buttons get compact and quiet.** — From three scratchpad treatments (A/B/C) shown against Current, the owner chose A for the once-per-section header (`StructuredNotesSection.jsx` Organize/+ Section) and C for the recurring in-content adds (`GrammarSection.jsx` + Example/+ Subsection/+ Vocabulary, `SourceSection.jsx` + Vocabulary): all five drop from the shared `Button`'s 44px/text-sm sizing to a bespoke 30px-tall/text-xs pill (px-2.5 py-1.5, icon 12–13px). The header's "+ Section" keeps a blue identity but softens from solid `C.pen` fill to a pale `C.penPale`/`C.penDark` outline; the in-content adds go further, using `C.card`/`C.mut` (muted grey text) so the single header action still reads as more prominent than the repeated per-item ones. Measured at 375px: all five buttons render at 30px height, 12px font, correct colours, no overflow; `GrammarSection`/`SourceSection`/`StructuredNotesSection` tests pass 30/30. Purely visual — no behaviour, markup semantics, storage or schema change.
- 2026-08-11 — **OWNER-REQUESTED: Grammar guide's header and Vocabulary's empty-state add button blend in like Notes' header.** — Grammar's Organize/+ Add-section (previously 44×44 icon-only `IconButton`s) and Vocabulary's empty-state "+ Vocabulary" (previously the shared `Button`'s solid-blue 44px pill) now use the same bespoke 30px compact-pill recipe as Notes' header: Organize/+ Vocabulary/+ Section all read text+icon, with the primary actions (+ Section, + Vocabulary) softened from solid `C.pen` fill to a pale `C.penPale`/`C.penDark` outline. `GrammarSection.jsx` drops its now-unused `IconButton` import. `GrammarSection.test.jsx`'s assertion that "Add grammar section" was icon-only (`textContent === ""`) is updated to expect its new visible "Section" label. Verified at 375px: both converted buttons render at 30px height with the same colours as Notes' header, no overflow; affected suites pass 54/54. Because every section heading (Notes, Grammar, Source, Vocabulary, Connections, Media links, Tags) shares this one component, the change is uniform everywhere with no per-section work. Verified at 375px across four section types: all measure `10px 12px` band padding and a 4px title-to-summary gap, no overflow. Verified at 375px across two stacked cards in the Pages hub: both the first-card and inter-card gaps measure exactly 16px, no overflow. Source's header (a different existing pattern: icon-only Organize, solid-blue + Capture) and Vocabulary's Practice/Start-practice-session actions are untouched — not part of this request. `GrammarSection.test.jsx`'s "Add grammar section" assertion reverts to expecting empty `textContent` (icon-only), matching its state before the two-turns-ago text-label change. Verified at 375px: all four converted buttons' backgrounds exactly match their heading band's background colour, empty visible text, no overflow; affected suites pass 60/60. Purely visual — no behaviour, markup semantics, storage or schema change.

## Phase 6 — AI assistant, second slice: persisted Diario feedback (2026-08-11)

- 2026-08-11 — **OWNER'S DECISION: the latest Diario review is persisted on the entry it judged, reversing the 2026-08-06 "session-only, held in component state and nowhere else" decision.** — The session-only design destroyed the review at exactly the moment it becomes useful: tapping Edit unmounts the reader, so acting on the feedback threw it away. Persistence also makes the review visible inside the editor, where the fixing happens. This supersedes that entry's storage rationale; its event rationale survives unchanged below.
- 2026-08-11 — **The review is schema v8's `feedback` field on the page record — a field on an existing content type, not a third type, so §7 stands on a brief amendment rather than an exception.** — `null` is the valid absent state and the field is always present on a v8 page, matching how `noteSections` shipped in v7. One review per entry: Ask again replaces it, Remove clears it to `null`. The migration is a pure root-field addition; `isJournalEntry` ignores root fields, so a stored review can never move a page across the Pages/Diario boundary, and a review left on a page that later gains structures is harmless dormant metadata.
- 2026-08-11 — **Saving a review moves no `updatedAt` and logs no event — `saveEntryFeedback` patches only the field, outside `updateItem`.** — The Phase 6 reason holds: requesting feedback is not opening the entry and must not move the lookup counts that decide what Repaso enrolls; and it is not an edit of the owner's writing either, so Recently touched order and Diario's Continue choice stay honest. `updateItem` always stamps `updatedAt`, which is why the writer is separate.
- 2026-08-11 — **Staleness is a djb2 content hash (`reviewedHash`) of the reviewed-text projection — trimmed title plus `plainTextFromMarkdown(body)` — never a timestamp comparison.** — The feedback save itself and formatting-only autosaves would both corrupt an `updatedAt` signal. One projection function in `lib/diarioReview.js` serves the request path and the staleness check so they cannot diverge, with the deliberate property that a `**bold**`-only edit does not mark a review stale. A review with no hash counts as stale.
- 2026-08-11 — **Feedback validation lives in `backup.js`'s `validateItem` via `validateStoredFeedback`, not in `validatePageStructures`.** — The review is not a page structure: `normalizePageStructures` never produces it, and putting it in pageKinds would drag the AI vocabulary (`VERDICTS`, `ITEM_CATEGORIES`) into the structure module. This also keeps the version gate in exactly one place: schema v1–7 envelopes reject a present `feedback`, v8 requires the field.
- 2026-08-11 — **The reader's Feedback button now renders when the AI feature is ready *or* a stored review exists; the request actions inside the panel still require the feature.** — The 2026-08-06 "button whose only outcome is an error" rule is preserved in its intent: opening now shows the owner's own stored data, and Send/Ask again render only when a request could succeed. A restored backup therefore shows stored reviews read-only until the key is re-entered.
- 2026-08-11 — **Ask again routes back through the §9 disclosure instead of straight to the network.** — With persistence, the panel can mount directly into a stored review in a session where the disclosure has never been shown; a one-tap resend from there would transmit without the interface having stated what leaves the device. Cancel from that disclosure returns to the stored review rather than closing the panel.
- 2026-08-11 — **A successful review is written immediately, but a failed write still displays the result; a failed replacement request leaves the stored review untouched.** — The owner paid for the tokens either way. The editor renders `entry.feedback` read-only below the body — no request controls, no Remove — with staleness computed live against the draft as it is typed.
- 2026-08-11 — **The App test "creates reflection as a separate current-day moment" now dates its fixture relative to today.** — Found while verifying: the hardcoded `2025-08-03` fixture depended on `priorYearMemory`'s ±7-day window, which walked past that date on 2026-08-11 and failed the suite on a clean tree. The fixture now uses the same month-day one year back, which sits at distance zero permanently.
- 2026-08-11 — **The page-header kicker ("Grammar guide", "Vocabulary collection", …) is a pill in its focus family's pale/ink pair instead of muted gray.** — Owner picked the pale-pill treatment from three scratchpad variants (tinted text, pale pill, folder-tab pill). The pill reuses `PAGE_ROLE_META`'s existing background/color pair keyed by `pageFocus` — the same pair the section heading bands wear — so the header names the page type in the colour its sections and folder already carry, with no new tokens. Unknown focus falls back to the Notes pair, matching the heading text's own fallback.
- 2026-08-11 — **Hotfix: `buildPreupgradeBackup` strips `aiApiKey` from the exported preferences, exactly as `buildBackup` does.** — Found by the owner at the live v7→v8 gate: the pre-upgrade path reads the prefs table raw through the legacy connection and then validates through `validateBackup`, which refuses any envelope carrying the key — so the first schema gate hit with a key stored could not download its safety export at all. The key stays in the database and survives the migration; only the file omits it, which is the documented restore contract. The pre-upgrade tests now seed the key and flag for every legacy version, assert the export excludes the key while `aiEnabled` rides along, and prove the key row survives.
- 2026-08-11 — **The notebook Markdown dialect now renders https images and hyperlinks; everywhere else it stays exactly as narrow.** — Owner wants Wikimedia/GitHub-hosted reference images visible in-app. The rendering machinery already existed and images were deliberately switched off, so this is a rendering-only widening: no schema, storage or backup change, revertible by reverting rendering code. Raw HTML stays stripped, non-https URLs fall back to readable text, and `plainTextFromMarkdown` still drops image nodes — so search, previews, the AI request path and the Diario `reviewedHash` staleness check are all untouched (an image-only edit does not stale a review, matching the bold-only rule).
- 2026-08-11 — **Images render block-level always, height-capped, tap opens the source in a new tab — placement in the sentence never matters.** — On the 375px primary device an inline image can only break the line, and a block-always rule means the owner never has to remember placement syntax. New-tab tap gives full-size pinch-zoom (dense table images) without building a lightbox; `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"` plus `loading="lazy"` keep third-party fetches private and cheap. All of it lives in one `MediaImage` component with mandatory fallback (alt text / nothing) so a dead or hotlink-blocked URL degrades to readable text, never a broken glyph.
- 2026-08-11 — **Media links preview inline when the URL has an image extension, below the unchanged link row.** — Extension allowlist (`jpg jpeg png webp gif svg`) plus load-error fallback: a share-page URL or dead link is simply today's link card, so nothing regresses. The label row and delete control stay where they were; the preview is additive.
- 2026-08-11 — **The editor toolbar gained an Image action that inserts `![selection](https://)` with the placeholder URL pre-selected.** — Typing `![](` on a phone keyboard is the real barrier to using the feature on the primary device; pre-selecting the placeholder makes paste-over-it a one-gesture flow.
- 2026-08-11 — **Picture fronts are a fourth question face in the shared card engine, applied whenever a forward card's item has a direct-image media link — never on reverse, and outranking cloze.** — Owner chose both surfaces, always-when-available (the cloze precedent: a face applies when the card supports it) and typed answering. The face rides the same session-start snapshot as cloze; the owner curates per word by keeping or removing the image link. Image outranks cloze because it is the rarer, owner-curated signal; reverse stays excluded because that card's back already asks for the term.
- 2026-08-11 — **Only the image URL reaches the card; the prompt renders unlinked with neutral alt and no caption.** — The link's label is usually the term, so carrying it would leak the answer through the caption, alt text or a screen reader. MediaImage gained `link={false}` for card fronts: the reader's tap-to-open anchor would let one tap exit mid-session, which the 2026-08-10 session-anatomy rule reserves for a confirmed second tap.
- 2026-08-11 — **A picture that fails to load degrades to the plain term front before reveal, and to nothing after.** — Offline or a dead URL must never strand a graded card; MediaImage's existing error fallback renders the ordinary forward question instead, and after reveal the answer side already shows the term so the fallback vanishes rather than duplicating it.
- 2026-08-11 — **Scheduled grades log `face: "image"` as additive metadata; nothing reads it.** — Same contract as `"cloze"`: Leitner replay reads only `metadata.grade`, so the history records how the question was asked without any scheduler or statistic depending on it. `isDirectImageUrl` moved to `lib/mediaUrls.js` so the card engine (lib) never imports from components.

## Documentation status sweep (2026-08-11)

- 2026-08-11 — **README.md and docs/IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS.md are synced to deployed reality: stale "implemented locally; not deployed" claims become "deployed", `SCHEMA_VERSION` is corrected from 7 to 8, and the three deployed 2026-08-11 increments (persisted Diario feedback, inline media rendering, picture-front flashcards) gain README Status bullets.** — The read order starts at README's Status section, and it had drifted badly enough to mislead: local `main` and `origin/main` both sit at `9503f96` and the recent GitHub Pages runs (31521297935, 31536559254, 31539984496) all succeeded, so Phase 18, Phase 20, the Phase 19 callout/blank-line increments and everything since are live while the docs still called them local. The Phase 6 bullet's "session-only" sentence is amended to point at the persistence reversal rather than silently contradicting it. Historical entries (phase reports, IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS document history) keep their original wording; only present-tense status claims changed.
- 2026-08-11 — **OWNER-APPROVED: the agent guide's deploy habit now includes syncing the status docs — a push to `main` is incomplete until `README.md`'s Status section (and `SCHEMA_VERSION` paragraph) and any affected `docs/IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS.md` statuses are updated in the same session.** — Status facts are duplicated across README, the agent guide and the ideas doc with no forcing function, and the fast-cadence 2026-08-11 increments outran the README exactly there: three deployed features and the v8 bump were recorded in `DECISIONS.md` but not in README Status, which is step 1 of the read order. The rule closes the loop at the moment the drift is created rather than relying on a later sweep.

## Editor upgrades — list continuation, preview toggle, auto-grow (2026-08-11)

- 2026-08-11 — **OWNER-APPROVED: Enter in the Markdown editor continues bullets, numbered items (next number, same `.`/`)` delimiter, no renumbering of later lines) and block quotes; Enter on an empty item removes its marker and ends the list; composition keydowns (`isComposing`/keyCode 229) are ignored.** — Phone-first typing win: re-typing `- ` on a phone keyboard was the biggest friction in list writing. The Enter rules and `prefixLines`' toolbar rules now share one set of hoisted line regexes (`BULLET_LINE`/`ORDERED_LINE`/`QUOTE_LINE`) so the toolbar and the Enter key can never disagree about what a list line is. `> [!NOTE]` marker lines continue with `> ` naturally, which is the right behaviour for typing a callout body. The composition guard means the worst case under a predictive-text IME is today's native newline — never data loss. Routed through the existing `onChange(full string)` flow, so nothing changes in what is stored or when Diario autosave fires.
- 2026-08-11 — **OWNER-APPROVED: the editor toolbar gained an Eye preview toggle that swaps the textarea for the read-mode `MarkdownText` render of the draft; formatting buttons stay rendered but disabled while previewing.** — Checking formatting previously meant saving and leaving the editor, which is a long round trip on the primary device. The textarea is hidden (`hidden` attribute), never unmounted: that preserves `selectionStart/End` so the caret is restored exactly on the way back, keeps `textareaRef` valid for the toolbar, and never re-fires the `autoFocus` that Detail, JournalEditor and StructuredNotesSection pass. The preview box inherits the textarea's measured `offsetHeight` as a floor so nothing jumps, and an empty draft renders a muted placeholder rather than a bare bordered box. Disabled-not-hidden formatting buttons keep the toolbar's tap targets stable at 375px. `value`/`onChange` are never touched by the toggle, so Diario's 650ms autosave and its at-most-one-`edit` contract cannot be affected.
- 2026-08-11 — **Grammar passes an explicit `calloutBlockquotes` prop rather than the preview inferring intent from `quoteLabel="Note callout"`.** — The label is display text; reusing it as a rendering switch would make a future copy edit silently change how Grammar previews. The prop carries the same name as the `MarkdownText` prop it feeds, so the mapping (`noteCallouts` → `explicitNoteCallouts`, `blankLines` → `blankLines`, `calloutBlockquotes` → `calloutBlockquotes`) now lives in one place instead of being wired by hand at each call site.
- 2026-08-11 — **OWNER-APPROVED: editor textareas auto-grow with their content via CSS `field-sizing: content`, merged into the caller's own `className`; `resize-y` is dropped from the call sites and `resize-none` set in the component.** — A fixed box on a 375px screen means writing through a keyhole, and the drag handle that used to relieve it is unusable on touch. Pure CSS was chosen over a `scrollHeight` effect because the audience is Chrome on phone and desktop (shipped since Chrome 123): no per-keystroke JavaScript or layout thrash, nothing to special-case in jsdom, and callers' `min-h-*` classes and `Detail.jsx`'s inline `minHeight` become the floor with no code. In a browser without support the failure mode is today's fixed height. Manual resize is off because a dragged height pins the box and defeats the growth — Tailwind's preflight sets `resize: vertical` on every textarea, so dropping `resize-y` alone was not enough. Verified in Diario at 375px: twelve lines grow the box to 336px with `clientHeight === scrollHeight` (no inner scrollbar), one line sits at the 320px `min-h-80` floor, and the document does not scroll horizontally.
- 2026-08-11 — **The preview toggle restores the caret from an effect, not from its click handler.** — Found in the browser while verifying: a hidden element cannot take focus, so calling `focusSelection` immediately after `setPreviewing(false)` ran before the commit that unhides the textarea and silently lost the caret. The effect runs after that commit. The regression test blurs the textarea first, because jsdom — unlike a real browser — leaves focus on an element it has just hidden, so without the blur the test passes even with the restore deleted.
- 2026-08-12 — **OWNER-APPROVED: `docs/IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS.md` reorganizes into Active / Deferred / Implemented bands, and implemented entries compress to summary-plus-links — amending the document's original keep-full-history rule.** — At 1,739 lines, roughly half the file was delivered-work narrative duplicating `DECISIONS.md` and the phase reports, and the two oldest still-open ideas sat buried between 150–270-line histories. Compression keeps every still-open evidence list, watch item and question in place, moves nothing out of git history, and never rewrites Document history entries. The same pass added the missing Owner-centric stats section, re-reviewed Provenance (recording the Phase 6 feedback-field precedent as the first live AI-provenance decision) and Saved views (its Phase 8 evidence window is open), promoted Confusion-pair drills out of Learning depth's not-built list into its own Active entry, and added cross-references between the sibling ideas (frequency coverage/weighting, the two drill families, tag hubs/saved views).
- 2026-08-12 — **OWNER-AUTHORED: the brief gains a Secrets rule — API keys and other secrets never reach exported or backup payloads, logs, or fixtures, and any new backup-envelope field needs an explicit test asserting no secret keys are present.** — The 2026-08-11 pre-upgrade hotfix is exactly the failure this prevents: `buildPreupgradeBackup` read the prefs table raw and carried `aiApiKey` into the envelope, which `validateBackup` then refused, so the first v7→v8 schema gate with a key stored could not download its own safety export. `buildBackup` had stripped the key all along — the rule turns that one function's care into a contract every future writer inherits, and the required test makes the omission provable rather than remembered. It sits between section 10's storage-durability bullets and section 11.

## Phase 21 — What to notice and conjugation families (2026-08-12)

- 2026-08-12 — **OWNER-APPROVED: dictionary verb details teach before they invite discovery.** — The existing Conjugation card starts with at most two plain-language, contrastive notices and a local disclosure for more; regular verbs get only the appropriate hablar/comer/vivir anchor. A qualifying notice may then show sibling verbs. Personal-entry lessons, a family browser, and Gym changes stay outside Phase 21.
- 2026-08-12 — **One pure analyzer classifies actual shipped paradigms for the pipeline, verifier, and browser; the catalog is gated against the corpus before packaging.** — Spelling can narrow a candidate rule but never establish it without its required cells. The first measured sweep covers 1,795 conjugable entry rows / 1,771 distinct lemmas: 1,148 fully regular, 591 with concrete notices, 32 lower-frequency unclassified, and all top 100 receiving either a notice or regular summary. The lone u→ue lemma is jugar, so it teaches without pretending to have siblings.
- 2026-08-12 — **Dataset r3 materializes discoverable family membership in a fifth primary-key reference store instead of adding a multi-entry index or scanning tables at lookup.** — This follows the reference database's existing rule that the pipeline precomputes reverse indexes and runtime uses `get`/`bulkGet`. Entry rows retain verified non-indexed pattern IDs; family members are deduplicated and ordered once at build time. Runtime derivation over the whole dictionary was rejected because it would repeat classification and sorting on the owner's phone.
- 2026-08-12 — **Reference declaration v2 is additive, not a personal migration; personal `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 8.** — Opening r2 creates an empty family store and keeps teaching notices available without sibling warnings. The A/B installer fills r3 atomically. Because an older app could mark r3 installed while silently ignoring its unknown fifth store, the newer app compares manifest and physical family counts and offers a same-version repair when they differ.
- 2026-08-12 — **Visible teaching evidence never uses collapsed vosotros.** — The default contrast uses yo, nosotros, and ustedes/ellos; tú appears only when the command itself is the lesson. The pedir present/preterite/gerund observations deliberately coalesce into one e→i lesson (`pido · pedimos · pidieron`) rather than three tense-shaped facts.
- 2026-08-12 — **Stem and gerund emphasis is anchored to the analyzer's structural replacement index, never recovered by searching the displayed word; pronominal paradigms are compared without their clitics but keep their original forms for teaching.** — A post-closeout audit found 128 of 620 stem/gerund evidence rows pointing at a repeated vowel in the ending (`pidieron`, `volvemos`, `diciendo`) even though their ranges were valid and visible. Exact example tests, a full-corpus position invariant, and an independent package-verifier check now encode where the change belongs. Slot-aware finite and gerund normalization adds regular anchors for *personarse*, *quejarse*, and *entrometerse* and the e→ie/e→i family for *arrepentirse*; *escabullirse* remains in the measured, deferred i-absorbing set.
- 2026-08-12 — **An overlapping sibling belongs only to the highest-priority displayed notice, and teaching copy never asserts a mutable family count.** — This is the approved anti-repetition rule implemented by `claimedLemmas`, not a deviation toward global duplication. The u→ue lesson now describes the stress contrast neutrally; the package's measured family row decides whether siblings exist.
- 2026-08-12 — **Only the manifest-selected r3 dictionary package remains in the public tree.** — Installed r2 dictionaries continue to work from IndexedDB and the v2 declaration still opens them gracefully, but retaining 22.7 MiB of unreachable r2 chunks caused Vite and the Pages workflow to copy dead files into every deployment artifact.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 21 is implemented, verified, and deployed.** — After the emphasis/pronominal correction, the final serial suite passes 1,310/1,310 tests across 107 files, the production build and `git diff --check` pass, and the independent r3 verifier passes 34/34 package invariants. The required classifier, ordering, and structural-index failures reddened and recovered. A fresh disposable 375×812 origin installed the regenerated r3 package and proved `p[i]dieron`, pronoun-preserving *arrepentirse* evidence, the regular *quejarse* anchor, 44px targets, zero overflow, and no console warnings/errors; the earlier closeout also covers disclosures, familiar ordering, sibling navigation, and Atrás. The disposable dictionary was removed, viewport reset, tab closed and server stopped; no owner data was available or inspected. The owner then requested the verified branch be fast-forwarded to `main`, which triggered the Pages deployment.

## Phase 22 — Knowledge consolidation (2026-08-12)

- 2026-08-12 — **OWNER-APPROVED: Phase 22 ships in three ordered slices — phrase↔word containment, conservative same-meaning proposals, then confirmed-link recall.** — All three derive from current entries, optional reference tables, or ordinary links. Personal schema stays v8; no personal field, preference, backup shape, event type, dictionary package, score, schedule, or automatic queue is added. Implementation approval does not authorize a push or deployment.
- 2026-08-12 — **Containment reuses cloze's ñ-preserving whole-token and conjugation-form rules but suppresses reference-wide ambiguous inflections and a fixed high-noise function-word list.** — *Fui* must not assert both *ir* and *ser*; *me fui de casa* may therefore show neither. Clitic-attached *dármelo* also stays silent because splitting inside a token would revive `casa`/`casada`-class false matches. Both are accepted v1 misses, not permission for a later substring shortcut. Missing/unresolved reference data falls back to term matching and writes nothing.
- 2026-08-12 — **Same-meaning proposals compare individual personal glosses with exact normalized content tokens, a 0.5 overlap gate, and at most three explained rows; POS rejects only when both compared meanings supply known conflicting values.** — `posOverride` and entry POS are sparse, so the guard intentionally degrades to inert rather than guessing. Pages, blank meanings, duplicate headings, self, and already-connected pairs are excluded. The heuristic may still be wrong; evidence is shown and no proposal creates authority.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 22 stores no suggestion-dismissal memory.** — A rejected *bank*-style proposal may reappear on later visits. The owner accepted that bounded annoyance for v1 rather than introducing a preference, backup/validation contract, and hidden durable behavior. Any later dismissed-pairs list needs its own product and durability decision.
- 2026-08-12 — **Derived containment and proposals live together in a read-only “From your cuaderno” section immediately before, but never inside, ordinary Connections.** — An explicit Link as Similar meaning action alone calls the existing stored-once writer; ordinary link timestamp semantics remain, no activity event is written, and the confirmed row then moves into the existing Similar meaning group.
- 2026-08-12 — **Similar-meaning recall uses only direct owner-confirmed edges, never raw proposals or transitive closure, and begins empty by design.** — A↔B plus B↔C does not make A↔C an answer. The hub action appears only after at least one eligible direct edge exists; reveal plus Again/Got it and one missed round remain entirely visit-local because an unrecorded synonym may still be valid.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 22a implements one exact-first matcher shared by cloze and containment, and reads ambiguity through bounded form-shard postings rather than scanning the dictionary.** — `matchTermInText` preserves cloze's original offsets while exposing exact/inflected evidence; `getEntriesForForms` bulk-reads only already-observed normalized surfaces. The lexical detail effect discards a completed result after navigation, and every reference failure returns exact-only personal rows. The first focused test run failed because the containment module did not exist; after implementation the containment/cloze/reference/detail boundary passes 77/77 tests across five files.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 22b keeps gloss scoring pure and routes the only authority-bearing action through the existing stored-once link writer.** — Individual meaning rows are tokenized independently, sparse POS is only a known-conflict rejection, exact set equality and shared evidence determine the capped deterministic order, and every existing connection direction suppresses a proposal. The deliberate component proof first failed with no “You also know…” surface; after implementation 64/64 focused tests across five files prove explained navigation before Connections, no dismiss memory, no pre-confirmation write, one ordinary Similar meaning annotation after confirmation, and no activity event beyond Detail's established view.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 22c snapshots one prompt per lexical endpoint with a direct confirmed Similar meaning neighbor, then shuffles a 10/20/All selection into a dedicated event-free session.** — The graph derives through the existing conceptual-connection reader, so stored-once backlinks are symmetric and reciprocal legacy copies de-duplicate without making A↔B↔C transitive. Small decks suppress redundant size choices, reveal labels answers as confirmed connections rather than exhaustive truth, and Again/Got it can produce exactly one missed-only round. The deliberate direct-edge proof first failed because the recall module did not exist; after implementation the graph, setup, session, hub, and relationship boundary passes 50/50 focused tests across five files.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 22 is implemented and verified locally on `codex/phase-22-knowledge-consolidation`; it is not pushed or deployed.** — The final unchanged tree passes 1,341/1,341 serial tests across 113 files, the production build transforms 2,100 modules, and `git diff --check` passes. The first full run had one asynchronous AiCard assertion after 1,340 passes; that untouched file passed 8/8 alone and the complete rerun passed. A disposable seeded 375×812 origin proved `dar` ↔ `me da igual` through `da`, reference-wide `fui` ambiguity suppression, a proposal before Connections, one stored-once Similar meaning edge with no new event, a two-prompt direct-link recall and one missed round, 44–48px actions, zero horizontal overflow, and no console warnings/errors. The origin returned to zero personal rows, its fixture reference stores and active pointer were cleared, the viewport was reset, the tab closed, and the server stopped; no owner browser data was available or inspected.
- 2026-08-12 — **An inferred containment form requires one form-shard lemma that matches the attached entry; empty, mismatched, or multi-lemma postings stay silent.** — This makes the existing conservative implementation explicit: stale or incomplete optional reference evidence may hide a true derived row but can never assert the wrong one. Exact personal-term containment remains available, and focused regression tests pin both empty and mismatched-posting cases. The same review restored two accidentally indented pre-Phase-22 brief lines and made cloze's internal tokenizer private; neither cleanup changes product behavior. The corrected complete suite passes 1,343/1,343 across 113 files.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 22 is deployed at `022e1b6`.** — The owner approved the reviewed branch for release; local `main` fast-forwarded through all four Phase 22 commits and the GitHub Pages workflow (run 31661654898) completed successfully. README Status, the direction/report, and both affected Improvement Ideas statuses were synchronized immediately afterward; schema remains v8.

## Phase 23 — Contexts and wandering (direction, 2026-08-12)

- 2026-08-12 — **OWNER-APPROVED: Phase 23 direction — 23a the word's biography, then 23b neighborhood browsing — recorded decision-complete in `docs/PHASE-23-DIRECTION.md` after a structured decision workshop.** — The owner selected the two ideas from the consolidation brainstorm for detailed breakdown, then answered eleven scope questions. One phase with sequenced slices was chosen over separate phases because 23a's prose-scanning extension of the Phase 22 containment matcher is the shared foundation; building it once under one direction keeps the engine's ñ-safety, stop-list and intentional silences from forking.
- 2026-08-12 — **Biography decisions: a sub-view entered from Detail's learning strip (Estadísticas-in-Repaso pattern), milestones-only narrative, display-only derived rows, and Diario as its own clearly labeled section.** — Detail's Phase 5e scan-first contract survives untouched; a grade-by-grade log already exists elsewhere and would bury the story; derived facts stay derived so v1 touches no write path; and journal exposure is deliberate-and-disclosed rather than ambient, honoring the deliberate-search rule's spirit for the one owner the data serves.
- 2026-08-12 — **Wandering decisions: typed links + Phase 21 families as the v1 edge set, uniformly random starts, no events from the wander surface, Diario present only as unopened count stubs, entry via a quiet Cuaderno root card.** — Curated edges make small high-signal neighborhoods before noisy derived adjacency is considered; weighting would quietly turn wandering into curated resurfacing, a different product; browsing is not lookup, so hops must never reshape Repaso enrollment (the Diario-prompt reasoning), while leaving into ordinary Detail keeps Detail's existing logging; the stub choice was the owner's own middle path — presence without exposure — over full exclusion; and the root card meets the idle moment where wandering actually happens.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 23a extracts one optional-reference enrichment sequence for both lexical containment and owner-prose containment.** — Subject-side attached words resolve read-only, conjugations load in one batch, tentative whole-token matches identify bounded form postings, and only a sole posting for the attached lemma survives; every failure reruns exact-only. The original Phrase-containment test file remains untouched, so its passing suite proves the refactor did not fork Phase 22's ñ, stop-list, ambiguity or clitic boundaries.
- 2026-08-12 — **Prose habitat scans only the visible text projection and the same enabled structures search exposes.** — Non-journal Notes Overview and named Notes sections always participate with Note markers removed; enabled Source capture text and enabled Grammar Overview prose participate; disabled structures do not; Diario bodies use their existing plain dialect and sort newest first. Rows are derived per open, keep short original-text snippets, navigate to their owning Page, and create no stored relationship or event.
- 2026-08-12 — **Historia is a lexical-only local Detail swap whose milestones and habitat remain display-only.** — The existing Detail mount owns the ordinary view boundary, so entering, navigating within, and closing Historia adds no new writer or repeat lookup. Milestones reuse the scheduler's single review replay through an additive callback, first-reached boxes omit boxes skipped by Easy, tricky toggles pair into episodes, and each false→true graduation becomes a retirement. Cuaderno threads the already-loaded event log only to the selected Detail; replay happens inside the mounted Biography rather than at the root.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 23a's focused boundary passes 112/112 tests across eight files.** — Coverage includes unchanged Phase 22 containment, optional-reference prose fallback and intentional silences, every milestone kind and ordering, disclosed Diario snippets, read-only navigation, the lexical strip swap, scan-first restoration, and Cuaderno event threading. `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 8; Slice 23b and whole-phase verification remain pending, and no push or deployment is claimed.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 23b samples uniformly from every personal lexical item and derives each neighborhood from typed edges plus loaded Phase 21 family rows.** — Words and phrases may start a walk; Pages never do. Typed connections preserve both stored directions, their owner notes and labels, while Diario targets are removed. Saved family siblings honor the installed alias map without rewriting a personal `dictKey`, exclude the center, and retain notebook order.
- 2026-08-12 — **Wander resolves dictionary exits read-only and keeps aliased relationship annotations attached to their raw stored keys.** — Calling the ordinary alias-healing reader would violate this surface's zero-write promise, while handing a canonical entry straight to the old connection reader would lose an old-key note. The dedicated pure join resolves each raw key for display, exits through the canonical id, and suppresses a canonical target when two explicit legacy aliases disagree. Reference or family failure hides only that optional group.
- 2026-08-12 — **“Pasear por mi cuaderno · al azar” is the quiet full-width idle-root launcher, and each hop is a real session-trail route.** — The launcher uses the existing refine-control visual grammar, stays hidden while a query is typed, and disappears when no lexical start exists. Lexical and non-journal Page neighbors hop; dictionary and “What to notice” rows are marked exits; the Diario count is inert. Route de-duplication now compares screen as well as tab and id, so opening the full Detail for the current center works while existing same-screen repeats remain no-ops. Wander mounts only for its active route and writes no event; ordinary Detail retains its established view boundary.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 23b's focused boundary passes 43/43 tests across four files.** — Pure coverage pins every sampled index, lexical eligibility, alias-aware family siblings, typed-note preservation and Diario filtering. Component and App coverage pins all three groups, inert Diario presence, zero item/event mutation while wandering, reduced Page centers, idle-only launch, same-id full-entry opening, and Back through every hop. Whole-phase verification remains pending; `SCHEMA_VERSION` remains 8 and no push or deployment is claimed.
- 2026-08-12 — **Phase 23 is implemented and verified locally on `codex/phase-23-contexts-wandering`; it is not pushed or deployed.** — The final unchanged tree passes 1,372/1,372 serial tests across 118 files, the production build transforms 2,105 modules, and `git diff --check` passes. Explicit spaced fixture timestamps now pin the intended order; the formerly flaky case passes 10/10 in fresh processes, the App/Biography focus passes 23/23, and the corrected complete serial suite passes 1,372/1,372 across 118 files in 327.85 seconds. - 2026-08-13 — **OWNER-DECIDED: the next dataset rebuild ships sense-level synonyms and antonyms, sense topic labels, first-sentence etymology, filtered Wiktionary sense-attached examples, and dormant derived/related family data; IPA and syllable breaks stay out.** — Decided in a four-question workshop over a measured census of every Kaikki field the pipeline drops for the shipped lemma set (recorded under "Unused dictionary fields" in `docs/IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS.md`). The selections total ~+660 KB gzipped (~3.96 MB bundle), knowingly past the plan-era ~3.5 MB guideline, which the brief never fixed; every field comes from the already-attributed CC BY-SA dump, and entry IDs stay additive-stable, so the §5 alias map stays empty and nothing orphans. Trimmed etymology was chosen on measurement (144 KB vs 321 KB full, 9,025 entries keeping a clean first sentence); examples were chosen for sense-alignment after gap-filling proved marginal (~35 of the 415 zero-example entries, ~3 KB).
- 2026-08-13 — **Boundaries of that decision: family data ships without UI, exclusions may rejoin later, and this is dataset scope, not implementation approval.** — Derived/related data rides now so the unapproved word-family explorer phase finds it installed (the timing its idea entry recorded); IPA and syllable breaks can join a later rebuild if pronunciation display earns a place. The 175-sense parent-gloss fix (most-specific gloss instead of `glosses[0]`) rides the rebuild regardless. Display placement, exact example filters, the etymology trim rule's edge cases, and new `check.mjs` assertions belong to the implementation plan, which still follows the §2 plan-first agreement.

## Android share target (2026-08-13)

- 2026-08-13 — **OWNER-APPROVED: the installed PWA registers as an Android share target with dispatch by content, and long shared prose lands whole in the search box.** — The owner chose the improvement idea's option 3: a shared URL opens New Source notebook creation with the link and shared title prefilled; any other shared text lands in the existing two-layer search for the owner to trim. First-word guessing and a word-picker screen were both declined for v1 — the search box degrades gracefully and adds no UI. Nothing is saved implicitly; a share only opens a screen, and every write stays behind the existing explicit create actions.
- 2026-08-13 — **The share target uses a GET action inside the app's scope, and startup consumes the `share_*` params into the ordinary in-memory route trail — no URL router is added.** — A no-server SPA cannot receive a POST share, and the service worker's existing `navigateFallback` already serves the app shell for in-scope GET navigations. `App` reads the params once while building its initial trail (the idea doc's "manifest-only" framing predated noticing the app deliberately has no router), then strips them with `history.replaceState` so refresh or Back never replays a share the owner already dismissed. Distinct `share_title`/`share_text`/`share_url` param names keep share navigations unmistakable.
- 2026-08-13 — **Text that is exactly one http(s) URL dispatches as a URL share; prose containing a link stays a text share.** — Chrome for Android shares a page by putting the bare URL in `text`, so ignoring that shape would break the most common case; but prose with an embedded link was shared for its words, and guessing otherwise would hijack a vocabulary capture into page creation. Validity reuses `isHttpSourceUrl`, so `javascript:` and other non-http schemes are never treated as links. The prefilled Source starter deliberately preselects no format — a shared link may be an article, video, or podcast, and that choice stays the owner's.
- 2026-08-13 — **The Android share target is implemented and verified locally; it is not pushed or deployed.** — Dispatch unit tests cover both param shapes, prose with an embedded link, non-http schemes, and title-only shares; app-level tests prove a text share seeds the search box, a URL share opens the prefilled New Source notebook sheet creating nothing, and the params strip. A deliberate dispatch break turned all four share arrival tests red before being reverted. The complete serial suite passes 1,397/1,397 across 120 files, the production build emits the `share_target` manifest block inside scope, and a 375×812 dev-server flow confirmed both arrivals, no horizontal overflow, and that refresh replays nothing. The share-sheet entry itself exists only on an installed PWA, so the real-device check waits for a deploy.
- 2026-08-13 — **The Android share target is deployed at `d0ceb27`, alongside the interjection-override increment.** — The owner's "push both" sent the two local commits; local and remote `main` match, GitHub Pages run 31734839152 completed successfully, and the live `manifest.webmanifest` serves the `share_target` block with the in-scope `./` action and `share_*` params. README Status gained entries for both increments and the improvement-ideas record moved to "deployed" in the same session. The remaining evidence is owner-side: the share-sheet entry appears on the phone once the installed PWA picks up the new manifest, which may require a reinstall — Android registers share targets at install/update time.

## Phase 25 — Historia word families (2026-08-13)

- 2026-08-13 — **OWNER-DECIDED: Historia gains two word-family habitat sections — Phase 25a conjugation family, Phase 25b derivational family — scoped in a seven-question workshop; implementation has not started.** — The owner selected both enrichments from the Historia-strengthening discussion. The seven decisions, recorded in full in `docs/PHASE-25-DIRECTION.md`: derivational rows show saved relatives only (the unsaved-relative growth reading stays with the unapproved word-family explorer); the conjugation section mirrors the wander card's family group (saved Phase 21 siblings plus the What-to-notice teaching exit, shared derivation); Historia only, leaving the wander card's v1 edge set untouched; no suppression of relatives already in Connections — each section states its own kind of fact; words only, phrase biographies unchanged; attachment required on both endpoints with no exact-term fallback inference; and a disposable repo-side quality audit of the shipped `relatedWords` data gates 25b before any UI work while leaving 25a independent. Everything derives at render; schema stays 8; Historia stays display-only with link promotion still its own future decision. Planning is not implementation approval — the §2 plan-first agreement still applies, and a push remains a further separate approval.
- 2026-08-13 — **The Phase 24 `relatedWords` dormancy narrows: Phase 25b's saved-relative biography rows become its only sanctioned reader.** — Phase 24 recorded that dormant `relatedWords` never renders pending a display decision; this workshop is that decision, deliberately narrow. `DictDetail` still never renders the field (the `CASA_FAMILY_SENTINEL` proofs must stay green), no explorer UI exists, and matching is exact whole-term through `normalize.js` on attachment-resolved entries in both directions — never stemming, substring, or string-similarity, because spelling similarity produces false relatives and a wrong family member teaches a false connection.
- 2026-08-13 — **OWNER-APPROVED: implement Phase 25a only; Phase 25b stops at its mandatory quality gate and r4 `relatedWords` remains dormant.** — The shipped-package audit found 8,125 resolvable mentions among 45,955 listed terms (17.68%); 17.06% of resolved mentions landed on a lemma with multiple dictionary entries, and deterministic samples exposed broad semantic relations, pronoun paradigms, accent-collapsed homographs, and wrong sense/POS targets. Even reciprocal evidence retained false claims. R4 flattened Kaikki `derived` and broad `related` rows together as plain lemma strings, so exact matching cannot recover relationship kind or target identity. A future derivational-family feature needs a separately planned reference-data redesign; no 25b UI, schema, event, preference, backup, or package change is authorized now. A push remains separate approval.
- 2026-08-13 — **Phase 25a is implemented and verified locally on `codex/phase-25-conjugation-family`; it is not pushed or deployed.** — One shared, alias-aware preparation in `src/lib/wordFamilies.js` now serves Wander and Historia, requires word endpoints, preserves notebook order, stays quiet on incomplete reference evidence, and never heals or writes personal data. The shared row component opens saved sibling Detail or the marked dictionary teaching view; a zero-sibling family keeps the teaching exit, while phrases and unattached words show no section. The final tree passes 1,403/1,403 serial tests across 121 files, the 2,108-module production build and `git diff --check`; a temporary event-write probe failed the intended Historia log assertion before removal. A disposable 375×812 flow proved the alias-attached *sacar* family, *buscar* and teaching routes, phrase/unattached exclusion, 44–56px actions, zero overflow and zero console issues, then returned the isolated origin to zero personal/reference rows. Personal schema remains v8; full evidence is in `docs/PHASE-25-REPORT.md`.
- 2026-08-13 — **Phase 25a's word-only rule deliberately narrows the deployed wander card, and decision 3's original wording is amended to match.** — Putting the rule in the shared preparer stopped an attached phrase from qualifying as a family sibling and stopped a phrase center from carrying a family section at all, so the workshop's "the wander card's v1 edge set is untouched" was true of edge *kinds* but not of that edge's contents. The narrower rule is kept on both surfaces because a phrase is not a member of a verb's inflectional paradigm, while `DictAttachment` renders for every non-page item, so an attached phrase is ordinary to own. The real defect was the proof: no wander test exercised a phrase endpoint, so the untouched-tests refactor argument could not have reddened here — the project's own "could this test have failed?" habit. `Wander.test.jsx` now pins both endpoints, each half verified to fail alone against the pre-Phase-25 boundary before restoration.

GitHub Pages run 31749800168 succeeded on that SHA, and the live `assets/App-Cnng_LDn.js` — the same chunk name this session's local production build emitted — contains **Familia de conjugación**, **Saved in your cuaderno**, and **What to notice**. ## Continue-with-this-video flow (2026-08-13)

- 2026-08-13 — **OWNER-APPROVED: after a chooser action, an ephemeral continuation pill keeps the shared video available — "Add to another item" reopens the full chooser, "Done" forgets it.** — One TikTok often yields several outputs (a new meaning, examples, a Grammar attachment, a new phrase), and the chooser previously released the share after one action, forcing a reshare per output. The owner chose the minimal pill over targeted per-type quick actions: each action already lands on the item where those composers live, and real use should identify which shortcuts deserve promotion. The state is in-memory only by design — no schema field, no preference, no event, no reload survival: content writes deserve events, workflow state stays silent (§7's spirit).
