## Personal twin merge (2026-08-13)

- 2026-08-13 — **OWNER-APPROVED: an ordinary dictionary connection whose entry has an attached personal twin gets a prompted per-row merge offer on Detail, non-vocabulary Collection pages, and Diario's Más; one tap re-points the physical `dict:` key (and its alias raw keys) at the twin, carrying the annotation.** — The LinkPicker has prevented new doubles since Phase 2e, but nothing revisited links made before the personal entry existed, and the owner's real notebook now holds such a twin — the evidence the v3→v4 migration deliberately waited for before repairing topology. Automatic machinery still never rewrites (the "does not treat a dictKey attachment as an ordinary link" proof is unchanged and green); only the explicit owner tap merges, and the merged link's target then renders the dictionary entry through its attachment, so nothing reachable is lost.
- 2026-08-13 — **The merge is bookkeeping: `mergeLinkedEntryIntoTwin` raw-updates only `linkedKeys`/`linkAnnotations` in one rw transaction, no `updatedAt`, no event — the `resolveLinkedEntryConflict` precedent.** — Re-pointing a link the owner already made is canonicalization, not content; recency ordering and Repaso behavior must not move because a key changed namespaces.
- 2026-08-13 — **An existing personal edge in either physical direction is honored: outgoing just drops the dictionary keys, incoming writes the surviving annotation reoriented on the twin's row — no reciprocal key is ever created — and conflicting explicit descriptions never auto-pick; a per-row resolver modeled on AliasConflictResolver shows both and stores the owner's edited survivor.** — Links are stored once (§7), so the twin may own the pair's only physical edge, and `linkItems` refuses reciprocal copies for exactly this shape; silently discarding either explicit annotation would delete owner data, the same rule the alias-conflict seam enforces. The database re-derives the conflict inside the transaction, so a race returns candidates instead of writing.
- 2026-08-13 — **Vocabulary-enabled Collection pages suppress the offer; JournalReader's reader body and DictDetail stay read-only.** — On a vocabulary page an outgoing lexical link IS membership, so merging would silently enroll the twin as ungrouped vocabulary — a membership change disguised as link repair, deserving its own owner decision if wanted later.
- 2026-08-13 — **The personal twin merge is implemented and verified locally; it is not pushed or deployed.** — New `src/lib/personalTwins.js` derivation (alias-aware on both the linked-key and attachment sides, self-twin excluded), the db merge with all three write branches, the card offer, and the conflict resolver are covered by 27 new tests across lib, db, component, and jsdom integration files; a deliberate `updatedAt` stamp in the merge write turned the recency tests red before removal. The complete serial suite passes 1,442/1,442 across 124 files. A 375×812 dev-server flow verified the offer by numbers (44px action, token colours resolved, zero horizontal overflow), one tap re-pointing the stored key with the annotation carried, zero edit events, unchanged `updatedAt`, and the row re-rendering as the personal card; the disposable fixture was cleared back to zero personal and reference rows.
- 2026-08-13 — **The personal twin merge is deployed at `88d3abc`.** — The owner's push approval sent three pending commits together (this feature's, plus `4b64bf8` and `15c2917`); `git rev-parse HEAD origin/main` returned one SHA twice, GitHub Pages run 31768258956 completed both build and deploy jobs successfully, and the live root returned HTTP 200 serving `assets/index-NIuk1pgN.js` — the same asset this session's local production build of that tree emits. README Status was synchronized in the same session. Remaining evidence is owner-side: open the real twin's item and take the offer once.

One shared lazy index tokenizes contexts once and batches optional reference reads; dictionary failure falls back exact-only, and `normalize.js` remains untouched.

Historia discloses the real evidence and offers navigation only.

Per-item duplicates collapse, the current item is excluded, and inline disclosures create no route or write.
- 2026-08-13 — **The unimplemented connection roadmap is recorded without duplicating existing graph ideas.** — Dictionary-backed synonym/antonym proposals require unique target and sense/POS evidence and never use rejected `relatedWords`; Page proposals require strong active-context or exact-URL evidence; the existing cluster/graph family remains confirmed-edge-first; optional AI review is user-started, disclosed, transient and confirmation-only, with AI-authored notes blocked on provenance.

## Apuntes — a second box on Diario entries, schema v9 (2026-08-14)

- 2026-08-14 — **OWNER-REQUESTED: each Diario entry gains one optional free-markdown Apuntes box for outside feedback and notes to self, stored as schema v9's `apuntes` on the page record.** — The owner pastes feedback from other tools (a Gemini review, in the motivating case) and had been appending it to the entry body, where it poisons everything derived from the body: "Ask again" would send it to Claude as the owner's own writing, the staleness hash flags the stored review though the Spanish never changed, and search and reading treat it as entry text. A field on an existing type — §7's two-content-type rule stands on a brief amendment, exactly as `feedback` did in v8. Deliberately **not** a `noteSections` entry: a nonempty outline flips a dated page out of Diario.

"Margin notes" was rejected because FeedbackReview already renders that exact heading for the AI review's item list.
- 2026-08-14 — **Saving Apuntes is an ordinary owner edit: `updateItem` on the editor's autosave path, `updatedAt` bump, the same at-most-one `edit` event per visit.** — Deliberate contrast with `saveEntryFeedback`'s no-event bypass, which exists because an AI review is not the owner's writing. Apuntes are.
- 2026-08-14 — **Whitespace-only Apuntes persists as `null`, the field's absent state; the reader renders the section only when non-blank.** — The field is always present on a v9 page, like `feedback` (v8) and `noteSections` (v7).
- 2026-08-14 — **A fresh draft materializes when body or Apuntes has text; a title alone still creates nothing.** — Pasted feedback must never be silently dropped by the body-only creation gate.
- 2026-08-14 — **Apuntes joins both search surfaces: a tier-6 "in the Apuntes" reason below body and structure matches (contained-vocabulary offsets shifted down one), and the Diario haystack.** — Owner-chosen over exclusion; the known wrinkle is that result cards preview the body, so an Apuntes hit shows its reason but not the matching text.
- 2026-08-14 — **Excluded from AI requests and the staleness hash by construction, not by new code.** — `reviewedTextFor` and the request path name `{title, body}` explicitly; the amendment pins this in §9 so the exclusion is contract, not accident.
- 2026-08-14 — **Validation lives in `src/lib/journal.js` (`validateApuntes`) and is composed by `backup.js`'s `validateItem` version gate, mirroring the `feedback` precedent.** — Keeps the version gate in exactly one place; `updateItem` needs no validator because the editor owns the only writer and the value is owner-typed text.
- 2026-08-14 — **v8→v9 is a pure root-field addition; the migration test proves the no-mutation, shared-reference, lexical no-op and untouched-timestamp properties, and the deliberate break failed it before restore.** — Same discipline as the v7→v8 bump.

The owner's device will pass the one-time export-first gate (schema 8 → 9) on next open; the export omits the AI key by design. Detail never had this bug — `KnowledgeConsolidation` already chooses "Appears in N of your phrases" or "Built on words you know" from `item.form` — so the fix restores one vocabulary for one relationship rather than inventing a second. Short "Built on" rather than Detail's full sentence because `SectionTitle` renders uppercase beside short noun-phrase neighbours (Collections, Seen together, Connections) on a 375px phone. `derivePhraseContainment`, row order, navigation and the `Matched as {surface}` inflection disclosure are all unchanged; no schema, storage, link or reference-seam involvement.

## Attach-later for unattached words (2026-08-14)

- 2026-08-14 — **OWNER-REQUESTED: an unattached lexical item's Detail card now shows a quiet "Attach dictionary entry" control that opens the existing DictPicker pre-filled with the term; picking attaches through the existing `attachTo` write.** — The §5 seam has always defined attachment as an optional, reversible relationship the owner may add at any time, and the machinery existed — but `DictAttachment`'s never-attached state rendered nothing, so a word created without its entry (the owner's real *razonar*) had no path to attach later. The orphan Re-attach flow already proved the picker; this exposes the same flow one state earlier. ## Backup pos validation accepts the app's own vocabulary (2026-08-14)

- 2026-08-14 — **Entry-level lexical `pos` validates as any string, never against an enum; only non-strings fail.** — The owner's real v8→v9 export-first gate failed closed on four words: `newLexicalFromEntry` has always copied the dictionary's own part-of-speech vocabulary (pron, prep, intj, …) straight onto items created from entries, and the display layer renders all of it, but backup validation checked the pos *editor's* six-value list. A validator narrower than the app's own writers rejects real owner data, and any replacement enum recreates the same lockout the next time the dataset vocabulary grows. The editor's selector list, the meaning-level `posOverride` closed allowlist, and the 2026-08-13 interjection decision's selector intent are all unchanged — only the validator boundary moved, and the old test pinning the narrow check now pins the writer-faithful one.
- 2026-08-14 — **The pos-validation fix is verified locally and unblocks the gate without touching stored data.** — The regression test walks intj/pron/prep/conj/phrase/interjection and the empty string through `validateBackup` and still rejects a numeric pos; restoring the enum deliberately turned it red before the fix went back. The gate reuses `validateBackup` (`preupgrade.js`), so one boundary serves both. The complete serial suite passes 1,486/1,486 across 129 files, and the running dev app validated a v8-shaped envelope carrying `pos: "intj"` while rejecting `pos: 7` by the new message. No migration, no data rewrite: the four affected items are correct as stored.

## Vendored agent skills from `mattpocock/skills` (2026-08-14)

- 2026-08-14 — **Five productivity skills are vendored into `.agents/skills/` (tracked) and `.claude/skills/` (gitignored): `grilling` with its `grill-me` alias, `handoff`, `wait-what`, and `writing-for-agents`.** — The editable `npx skills add` route was taken over the Claude Code plugin deliberately: the plugin installs a managed read-only bundle that auto-updates, and a bundle free to start contradicting `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md` between sessions is precisely the drift that guide exists to prevent. Vendored copies are ours to edit and are pinned by `skills-lock.json`. `grilling` is the one that earns its place on merit — its design-tree rounds, its numbered questions each carrying a recommended answer, and its refusal to act before the owner confirms shared understanding are the brief §2 plan-first agreement made procedural rather than aspirational. `handoff` writes to the OS temp directory rather than the workspace, so it does not compete with the `docs/PHASE-NN-DIRECTION.md` → report convention. `teach` and `to-questionnaire` were installed, read, and rejected: `teach` claims the current directory as a teaching workspace and wants `MISSION.md`, `RESOURCES.md`, `NOTES.md`, `./lessons/`, `./reference/`, `./learning-records/` and `./assets/` at the repo root, against the `docs/PHASE-NN-*` layout; `to-questionnaire` exists to pull knowledge out of a second person, and this is a one-owner project with nobody to send it to.
- 2026-08-14 — **`writing-for-agents` is kept but demoted to `disable-model-invocation: true`, and `wait-what` now points at `docs/mi-cuaderno-project-brief-v3.md` instead of the `CONTEXT.md` this repo does not have.** — `writing-for-agents` shipped model-invocable behind a description that triggers on any edit to `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md`, so it would fire automatically on the files whose conventions are most deliberate. Its line 74 holds that steering by prohibition is a failure mode and the positive should be prompted instead — the direct opposite of the Tripwires section, which is built from hard prohibitions on the things the guide itself calls easy to violate and expensive to discover later. `AGENT-GUIDE.md` line 5 requires a rule conflict to be fixed rather than silently sided with, and an auto-firing skill resolves that conflict inside a single turn with nobody left to raise it. The demotion keeps the craft content — the no-op test, the cache-versus-source-of-truth rule, progressive disclosure — available on request and removes the ambush. `grilling` is now the only skill in this repo that can invoke itself. `wait-what`'s `CONTEXT.md` reference was a convention of the `domain-modeling` skill, which was not installed; the brief is this project's equivalent contract.
- 2026-08-14 — **`npx skills remove` switched the working checkout from `skills-trial` to `main` mid-command; skills are henceforth removed by deleting both directories and editing `skills-lock.json` by hand.** — The reflog recorded `checkout: moving from skills-trial to main` inside that invocation, with no checkout issued by the session. Nothing was lost, and the incident happened to demonstrate both halves of the install's shape: the tracked half lived in a branch commit and returned intact on switching back, while the gitignored `.claude/skills` half was untouched by the checkout because git will not act on ignored paths. The guide's existing "check `git status -sb` before your first commit" rule — written because Codex and Claude share this checkout — now covers third-party tooling as well. Related standing cost: editing a vendored skill invalidates its `computedHash` in `skills-lock.json`, so `npx skills update` will see drift on `wait-what` and `writing-for-agents` and may offer to overwrite both fixes.

## Suppressed-containment confirmation tier (2026-08-15)

- 2026-08-15 — **OWNER-APPROVED: inflected containment matches the Phase 22 ambiguity oracle suppresses become explicit confirmable proposals in Detail's "From your cuaderno" card; the oracle, matcher, stop-list and clitic silences stay untouched.** — Prompted by the owner noticing *Creo que* never shows *creer*: the matcher finds `creo`, then correctly declines to assert it because `creo` is also *crear*'s first person. A diagnostic over the owner's real 2026-08-15 export (42 words, 143 phrases) measured 83 containment rows shown and 48 suppressed: 28 were one normalization artifact (*dé* → `de` proposing *dar* under every phrase containing the preposition), roughly 16 were genuine misses a human resolves instantly (*creer*, *decir* via *dicho*, *hacer* via *hecho*, *ser* via *es*/*son*), and roughly 4 were correct suppressions (*al vino, vino* is the noun). Recovery therefore goes through owner confirmation rather than any weakening of the oracle — the app still asserts nothing; the assertion becomes the owner's, which preserves the recorded "may hide a true derived row but can never assert the wrong one" conservatism at proposal level too.
- 2026-08-15 — **A candidate must carry a form posting that names the attached lemma among others; stop-word surfaces never propose; already-connected pairs and every reference-failure path propose nothing.** — The multi-lemma-including-attached rule is what separates *creo* (creer **and** crear — genuinely worth asking) from an empty or mismatched posting, where stale optional reference data could otherwise put a wrong proposal in front of the owner. The surface filter reuses the existing fixed `CONTAINMENT_STOP_WORDS` list and kills the entire 28-row *de* → *dar* artifact class before it reaches the card. No dismissal memory, matching the recorded Phase 22 decision: a wrong proposal (*al vino, vino* → *venir*) may reappear, and any dismissed-pairs list still needs its own durability decision.
- 2026-08-15 — **Confirmation writes the one ordinary stored-once `found_in` edge with the word as subject, logs no event, and the row then lives in the existing Found in/Contains Connections groups everywhere Connections render.** — Word-side confirmation stores `found_in`/owner; phrase-side stores the same conceptual edge as `found_in`/target, so both perspectives label correctly through the existing reorientation machinery. `linkItems` semantics are unchanged — link bookkeeping logs no `edit` event, exactly like Phase 22b's Similar-meaning accept, and Historia needs no change because its Connections section reads the same groups. One behavior nuance in the new block only: the candidate list resets on an entry change but re-derives in place on an items refresh, so a rendered confirm action is never unmounted mid-tap — the transient-empty reset pattern would otherwise detach the button under the tap that confirms it, which is also how the component test first failed.
- 2026-08-15 — **The confirmation tier is implemented and verified locally; it is not pushed or deployed.** — The three touched focused files pass 51/51, including a deliberate first failure: the phrase-side Detail proof initially clicked a confirm button that an items-refresh had detached, which drove the re-derive-in-place fix rather than a test workaround. The complete serial suite passes 1,510/1,510 across 130 files and the production build passes. A disposable random-port 375×812 origin seeded with a creer/crear fixture sharing `creo` showed the proposal with its `also a form of crear` evidence, measured both actions at 44px and document geometry at 375/375, and proved one tap wrote exactly one `found_in`/owner annotation on the word, nothing on the phrase, and no event beyond the mount's view; the row then rendered under Connections → Found in and the candidate disappeared. The only console warning was Dexie patching the hand-seeded fixture database's missing index — a seeding artifact, not app code. The disposable origin retains its fixture because browser safety requires confirmation before deleting local data; no owner browser data was available or inspected.
- 2026-08-15 — **The suppressed-containment confirmation tier is deployed at `39c0e90`.** — The owner asked to push; `main` moved from `a03a414` in one commit, `git rev-parse HEAD origin/main` returned the same SHA twice, and GitHub Pages run 31917078425 completed both jobs successfully. The live root returns HTTP 200 and serves `assets/index-BywqOKI0.js`, the asset emitted by the verified local production build, and the deployed application chunk `App-Ah1Lv3RH.js` containing **Possibly appears in your phrases** returns HTTP 200. README Status, both Improvement Ideas statuses and the document history were synchronized in the same session. Personal schema remains v9.

## Broader part-of-speech vocabulary and the hub lens (2026-08-15)

- 2026-08-15 — **OWNER-APPROVED: the entry and meaning part-of-speech lists become one list, extended with `pronoun`, `preposition`, `conjunction` and `interjection`.** — Prompted by the owner asking how to hold a word the dictionary splits by part of speech (*como* as adverb, preposition and conjunction). The recommendation was one personal item per word-as-known, with the roles carried by its meanings, and separate items reserved for true homographs (*cerca* the fence versus *cerca* the adverb) joined by an `often_confused` connection — so the extension is what lets a meaning name the role it actually plays. The two lists had been separate and unequal (only the meaning offered `interjection`), which let a word carry a part of speech its own meanings could not restate; `MEANING_POS_OPTIONS` and `LEXICAL_POS_OPTIONS` are now the same value. The dataset's remaining labels — determiner, article, numeral, contraction, particle — fold into `other` as grammarian categories rather than shelves the owner would browse, and `phrase` maps to nothing because `form` already tells a phrase from a word. Storage shape, events, backup and schema are unchanged: `SCHEMA_VERSION` remains 9, and `backup.js` deliberately does not enum-check `item.pos`, so existing exports stay valid in both directions.
- 2026-08-15 — **A dictionary entry's part of speech is translated through one table at the seam, and an unrecognized tag seeds nothing rather than a raw abbreviation.** — `lexicalSeedFromEntry` translated only `adj` and `adv`; `prep`, `conj`, `pron` and `intj` were copied in raw and then silently dropped by a select offering no such value, so saving *como* as a preposition lost the part of speech without saying so. About 300 of the 10,278 shipped entries were affected. The table lives in `partOfSpeech.js` beside the abbreviations, applied once at the seam rather than per caller. A value the owner cannot see in the select is worse than no value, because only one of the two is visibly missing.
- 2026-08-15 — **OWNER-REQUESTED: Words & phrases adds a `Part of speech` refinement, innermost after the tag lens, matching on the item's own `pos` OR any meaning's `posOverride`.** — The disjunction is the point: matching only the entry level would hide a one-item *como* from a `conjunction` filter and push the owner toward splitting entries to stay findable — the storage shape bending to the filter rather than the reverse. Options and counts describe only what the narrowed view holds, in option order rather than by count so they do not reshuffle mid-use, and an impossible selection clears itself, following the existing exact-Page and tag lenses. A word counts once per distinct value and appears under both when its entry and a meaning disagree. Narrowing carries into search and the Practice source together, which is what makes "practice all my conjunctions" fall out of the filter rather than needing its own feature.
- 2026-08-15 — **Two stale copies of the old five-value list were found and sourced from the option list instead.** — `PART_OF_SPEECH_ABBR` held the dictionary's short keys but not the new spelled-out ones, so the running app showed a card reading "preposition" beside one reading "adv." — visible only in the browser, and now guarded by a test asserting every choosable option abbreviates. `meaningSuggestions.js` restated the five values as `KNOWN_POS`, where an unrecognized value reads as "not recorded" and lets a mismatched pair through: the same-meaning proposal guard would have quietly stopped separating the four new classes the moment they became storable. Both now read from `LEXICAL_POS_OPTIONS` rather than repeating it.
- 2026-08-15 — **The part-of-speech work is verified locally before release.** — The complete serial suite passes 1,529/1,529 across 130 files and the production build passes. A deliberate break removing the meaning-override half of the matcher failed exactly the four tests written to catch it, in both the helper and component files, before restoration. A disposable 375×812 origin seeded with nine items — including a single *como* carrying preposition and conjunction meanings — measured every card abbreviating consistently, the lens offering `noun · 2, verb · 2, adverb · 1, preposition · 2, conjunction · 2, interjection · 1` in option order, `preposition` returning exactly *sobre* and *como*, `conjunction` returning *pero* and *como*, the selection releasing itself to `any` when the Phrases chip made it impossible, the entry select offering all nine choices, a 44px control and 375/375 document geometry. The disposable origin retains its fixture because browser safety requires confirmation before deleting local data; no owner browser data was available or inspected.
- 2026-08-15 — **The part-of-speech work is deployed at `0de63dd`.** — The owner asked to commit and push; `main` moved from `9e0cc92` in one commit, `git rev-parse HEAD origin/main` returned the same SHA twice, and GitHub Pages run 31918541094 completed both jobs successfully. The live root returns HTTP 200 and serves `assets/index-Cz-JKOsP.js`, the asset emitted by the verified local production build; the deployed application chunk `App-B0SqprAI.js` returns HTTP 200 and contains **Part of speech**, `preposition`, `conjunction` and **None recorded in this view**. Two notes for future deploy checks: grepping only the assets referenced from `index.html` misses `App`, which is dynamically imported, so the first check found nothing and looked like a failed deploy; and a `cd dist` from an earlier command persisted into the closeout command, which appended this entry to a stray `dist/DECISIONS.md` and made `git add README.md` fail — the Bash tool's working directory survives between calls, so closeout commands should `cd` to the repo root explicitly.

## Browser-backed navigation continuity (2026-08-18)

- 2026-08-18 — **OWNER-APPROVED REVERSAL: Phase 5a's in-memory-only navigation and reset-on-tab-switch rules are superseded by shallow browser history with one remembered stack per primary tab.** — The earlier rule was intentionally conservative when the app had few destinations. Cuaderno, Diario, Repaso and Ajustes now contain enough stable hubs and drill-downs that Android Back should behave like a normal phone app, tab changes should restore context, and opening a word from filtered Repaso performance should not discard the originating view.
- 2026-08-18 — **`history.state.mcNavigation` v1 is a validated stable-location snapshot, not persistence for visit work.** — Its allowlist admits only the active tab, navigation depth, stable screen names, item ids, visit keys and fixed Back-label metadata. Search/share handoffs, new-editor seeds, filters, drafts, study progress and scroll positions stay in memory; refresh therefore restores safe stable locations while payload-only search, a new unsaved editor and an active session return to their launchers. No URL route, router dependency, `sessionStorage`, preference, event, backup or schema change is introduced; schema remains v9.
- 2026-08-18 — **Primary tabs mount lazily and remain mounted for the browser task, while browser Back, Forward and visible Back actions use one chronological path.** — Retention preserves Repaso filters and other visit-local state across canonical cross-tab item navigation without loading unopened tabs at startup. Selecting another tab pushes and restores that tab's stack; selecting the active tab pushes its root; Page↔Diario identity changes replace the active destination; missing items fall back to the nearest valid route.
- 2026-08-18 — **Active-work safeguards participate in browser history instead of bypassing it.** — Diario hardware Back reuses its existing save/date guard, tab switches quietly flush through the last-valid-date rule, and study sessions use a transient marker so hardware Back and Finish invoke the same completion behavior exactly once. Share parameters override startup once and are then stripped without erasing navigation state.
- 2026-08-18 — **Browser-backed navigation continuity is implemented and verified locally; it is not pushed or deployed.** — Pure navigation coverage and the focused App/component suites pass; the complete current-tree serial suite passes 1,576/1,576 across 132 files; the production build transforms 2,127 modules; and `git diff --check` passes. Deliberately unmounting retained Repaso reddened the filter round-trip at the expected `Saved` assertion, then restoring retention returned it to green. A disposable 375×812 origin verified visible and browser Back to exact `Saved` + `All tenses` performance filters, stable-detail refresh with transient filters defaulted, Cuaderno→Diario→Repaso Back/Forward chronology and actual-destination labels, active-tab root reset, hardware-style study Back consumed once, and final-root exit to a prior browser entry. The document measured `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 360` inside `innerWidth === 375`, no visible element overflowed, and browser warning/error logs were empty. ## Taller — Diario writing practice direction (2026-08-20)

- 2026-08-20 — **OWNER-APPROVED: the Taller v1 direction is workshopped and recorded in `docs/DIARIO-TALLER-DIRECTION.md`; planning may begin, and no code exists yet.** — A multi-round question workshop settled every layer: a Taller door on the Diario beside Write today (suggest-with-one-tap-change, rotating the three skill categories before data exists); drills in the ordinary editor with an easier/harder tier toggle, a shuffleable tema chip from an owner-edited interest preference, static word banks plus live dictionary conjugation help (endings always, lookup on demand), and mixed-pool offered words; keep-or-discard with a default-off option to include the prompt in the kept entry; one rich practice event either way (skill, prompt id, kept/discarded, offered word ids) with discarded drills counting as practice; a quiet derived timeline badge; record-don't-display tracking; and no AI in v1. This narrowly amends the 2026-08-03 visit-local prompt rule (practice events become storable; reflective entries stay unclassified) and the never-automatic-body-text rule (keep-time opt-in only) — both amendments get their own lines at implementation. ## Taller v1 — prompt-data growth (2026-08-20)

- 2026-08-20 — **Skill prompts carry their Taller drill data as optional static fields: `easier`/`harder` bilingual tier variants, a `tense` tag naming the exact `"Mood/Tense"` conjugation table key, and `offersWords` eligibility.** — Growth stays in the shipped, auditable, offline prompt library per the approved direction. Reusing `conjugation.js` table keys verbatim means weakness stats (`conjugationStats.js`) and the endings scaffold (`recognitionContent.js`'s `TENSE_ENDINGS`) need no mapping table, and a typo becomes a test failure rather than a silent mismatch. The reflective four categories carry none of these fields, keeping drill machinery visibly separate from reflective prompts; `tallerScaffolds.js` ships the per-category word banks with a general fallback so a reflective drill is not bare. ## Taller v1 — door, drill flow and the practice event (2026-08-20)

- 2026-08-20 — **OWNER-APPROVED AMENDMENT of the 2026-08-03 visit-local prompt rule: a Taller drill logs one `practice_write` event at keep-or-discard time, carrying skill category, prompt id, tier, kept/discarded, offered word ids and the tema.** — The narrow scope the direction doc states: ordinary "Need a prompt?" selection remains visit-local guidance and is never stored, reflective entries stay unclassified, and no page gains a stored practice field. The event is the first deliberate storage of prompt usage because every deferred future view (per-skill history, streak semantics, coverage map) derives from it at render; discarded drills log it too — writing happened — extending the Phase 13 drill-day precedent to future streaks.
- 2026-08-20 — **OWNER-APPROVED AMENDMENT of "never automatic body text": keeping a drill offers one default-off tap to include the prompt's Spanish at the top of the kept entry as an ordinary quote block.** — Nothing is inserted without that tap, the ordinary prompt flow is untouched, and the quote-block form uses the existing safe Markdown dialect so readers, search and the AI projection need no new marker.
- 2026-08-20 — **`SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 9 for the practice event; the tema string is recorded in event metadata; `kept` is stored explicitly beside the page reference.** — Backup validation (`validateEvent`) enumerates no event types, accepts a null `itemKey` and leaves metadata unconstrained, and `logEvent` already defaults `itemKey` to null (`search_miss` precedent) — verified before implementation and pinned by a new round-trip test, so no §5 migration machinery is touched. The tema rides along because the event is otherwise the only record of what nudged the writing, the string already lives in the backed-up private preference layer, and dropping it now would be unrecoverable later. `kept` stays explicit because deleting the page later leaves the event's `itemKey` dangling while kept-ness must survive.
- 2026-08-20 — **The Taller door is a quiet icon-plus-word action on the Diario Today card; its panel proposes the least-recently-practiced of the three skill categories, with day-of-year rotation before any practice data and all seven categories one tap away.** — The compact Cuidar/Pasear door register applies: no counts, no pressure. Both proposal rules are zero-storage render derivations over the event log; the stale/weak-skill proposal upgrade stays deferred with the display work. Within the chosen skill the prompt draw softly prefers (2/3 weighting) prompts targeting a tense the Gym's existing weakness rule marks weak, so a weak preterite biases Narrate without ever locking out the pool.
- 2026-08-20 — **A drill rides the existing Diario edit screen as a transient seed payload; the editor's drill mode suppresses every persistence path, and keep-or-discard replaces the save flow.** — One writing surface to maintain, per the direction. Keep writes one ordinary dated Journal page (ordinary `create` event) and lands on its reader, where linking an offered word uses the reader's existing affordances — that ordinary mechanism is the word's whole record of the offer, which itself creates no link and no event. Discard confirms in two steps, logs the subject-less event and keeps no text. Leaving mid-drill with writing routes through the same confirm; a blank drill leaves silently with no event, and a refresh drops the transient payload — drill text is transient by design. Kept practice entries show a quiet derived skill badge in the timeline eyebrow, stored nowhere.

## Taller v1 — tema layer and scaffolds (2026-08-20)

- 2026-08-20 — **The owner's interest list is the `tallerTemas` preference — trimmed unique strings on the existing generic preference/backup path — edited inside the Taller panel itself.** — The `pinnedLexicalIds` precedent: private personal data riding a path that already exports, validates and restores, with no schema change. Editing lives inside Taller (the direction's suggestion) so the feature is self-contained; backup validation gains the same narrow non-version-gated check tag colours have — absent is always valid, present must be the exact shape the app writes. The drill's tema chip is a nudge beside the prompt, never spliced into the Spanish sentence; the shuffle excludes the current tema so a redraw always moves, and with no alternative the current tema stays. The tier and tema showing at keep/discard time are what the practice event records.
- 2026-08-20 — **Scaffolds are transient drill furniture: the shipped word banks, the target tense's regular endings always visible via the Gym's existing `TENSE_ENDINGS` rows, and a live verb lookup through `getVerbTablesByLemma`.** — Reuse over new data: `TENSE_ENDINGS` already keys endings by the exact `"Mood/Tense"` table labels the prompts now carry, so the endings display needed zero new reference data. The lookup resolves a typed lemma at render through the reference layer and stores no `dict:` key anywhere, so the §5 orphan path can never be needed; with no dictionary installed the lookup is simply absent — "not installed" is not "orphaned" — while the word bank and endings still render. Nothing about a scaffold is stored on the page or the event beyond the prompt id.

Provenance, media annotations, templates, persistent disclosure state and unrelated deferred fields are not batched merely to avoid a later migration.

Meaning notes remain plain and meaning-specific, and every changed explicit save writes one ordinary `edit` while Cancel, disclosure and no-op organization remain event-free.

## Tooling — a second machine (2026-08-22)

- 2026-08-22 — **A headless Ubuntu server (the owner's old Dell XPS, on Tailscale) is a second checkout of this repository; `docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md` gains "Two machines, one remote", and `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` mark their shell notes as Windows-only.** — Claude Code and Codex run there as CLIs inside `tmux`, driven over SSH from the Surface Pro and phone, with Claude's Remote Control for the phone. The machine rules live in the shared guide rather than the tool files so both tools read one copy. The Linux clone sets `core.autocrlf=input` so it commits LF without rewriting the tree the Windows checkout (`autocrlf=true`) produced. `.claude/` being gitignored means the skills copy and `launch.json` must be recreated per machine; the Linux `launch.json` is only relevant to a tool with a browser pane, which the CLI lacks.
- 2026-08-22 — **The first XPS run of the serial suite passed 1,661/1,661 across 140 files in 358s and surfaced one unhandled `DatabaseClosedError` after `App.test.jsx`'s "routes tag twins to Ajustes with the duplicates review open" test.** — The test's existing mitigation — awaiting the "2 items ·" text before teardown closes the database (about line 1420) — is a proxy for the Ajustes storage refresh finishing, and on the slower CPU at least one Dexie query is still in flight when that text renders. No test fails, but Vitest counts the rejection as an error and `npm test` exits non-zero. The fix (await the refresh's real completion, or make the teardown tolerate a closed-database rejection from a query the test is done with; never a longer timeout) is deferred. Recorded so the next XPS run is not read as a regression.

## Test reliability — App.test.jsx teardown race (2026-08-22)

- 2026-08-22 — **"routes tag twins to Ajustes" now waits for the Dictionary card's settled render, not only the Backup card's items line.** — This closes the deferred item recorded under "Tooling — a second machine" above. The intermittent unhandled `DatabaseClosedError` after that test (reliable on the slow Core i5 Ubuntu server, rare on the laptop) was not the personal database, which teardown never closes. `afterEach → removeDictionary() → deleteSlot()` closes the *reference* slots, and the Dictionary card's mount refresh calls `pendingInstall()`, which opens the inactive slot `mi-cuaderno-ref-a` from scratch; on a slow CPU that open is still in flight when teardown closes it, and the card's fire-and-forget effect has nothing catching the rejection. The earlier `findByText(/2 items · /)` proved only that the personal-db counts had returned. The card's manifest notice ("Could not reach the dictionary files…", since jsdom serves no manifest) renders strictly after `pendingInstall()` returns, so it is the observable completion; its wait is bounded at 3 s inside the test's existing 10 s budget and `testTimeout` stays at 5 s. No app code changed.
- 2026-08-22 — **Reproduce teardown races by holding the slot open, and keep the worker alive past the file's last test.** — A temporary `db.on("ready", () => delay)` in `refDb()` plus a throwaway trailing test made the rejection deterministic on the laptop; without the trailing test the worker exits before the delayed rejection fires, which is why the laptop rarely shows it. Two tooling traps met on the way: the default reporter hides `console.log` in a non-TTY run (use `--reporter=verbose`), and adding a `process.on("unhandledRejection")` listener in a test file silences vitest's own unhandled-error report entirely.

## Landing — Recent list expansion (2026-08-23)

- 2026-08-23 — **The landing's Recent card now shows five items with a "Show more" toggle revealing up to ten (was a fixed three).** — The owner asked to see more than three recent items and listed four possible shapes: five entries, an expand/show-more control, a scrollable list, or a swipeable card. Five-plus-expand was chosen because it combines the two simplest: the landing stays compact by default, the deeper reach is one tap away, and it avoids a nested scroll region (awkward inside a card on the 375 px phone) and gesture-hidden items (swipe is undiscoverable and conflicts with vertical scrolling). The expanded state is in-memory only, so the landing always reopens compact; "Browse all" remains the door to the full list.
- 2026-08-23 — **Two pre-existing test failures observed on clean `main` on the XPS: `TallerDrill.test.jsx` "keep without the tap…" (deterministic, `onDrillKept` never called) and `JournalEditor.test.jsx:356` `created.prompt` (intermittent, full-run only).** — Verified against a stashed working tree before committing the Recent-list change, so they are not regressions of it. The 2026-08-22 run passed 1,661/1,661, and the failing Taller test — unlike its neighbours — injects no `now`, so the leading hypothesis is the real clock leaking into the drill keep flow. Diagnosis and fix deferred to their own task; recorded so the next run's counts are read correctly.

## Test reliability — Diario settle-wait races (2026-08-23)

- 2026-08-23 — **Both failures recorded above were settle-wait races against the Diario save flows, not clock leakage; the tests now wait for each flow's terminal observable instead of its first database row.** — TallerDrill's keep test waited for the page row, which `keepDrill` writes *before* its practice event and the `onDrillKept` callback, then asserted the callback synchronously; an 80 ms delay injected into `logPracticeWrite` reproduced the exact reported failure (page present with the right body and date, callback at 0 calls) plus a cascade in which the still-in-flight event landed after the next test's `clearAllPersonalData` and satisfied that test's "one event" wait with the wrong metadata. JournalEditor's prompt test — and two same-pattern neighbours, the first-nonblank-body and Apuntes-materialization tests — waited for `allItems()` length 1, but the page materializes from an early keystroke's 15 ms autosave, so the assertions could read a partial snapshot: a full serial run caught `created.body` holding `"E"`. All fixes are test-only, using the file's own existing idiom (wait on `onDrillKept`/`onBack` being called, or on the final saved text); no `now` injection was needed because the keep flow never consults the clock, and the `"2026-08-20"` seed date was never compared against today. Verified: both files pass three consecutive paired runs, the Taller file passes 12/12 with the adversarial 80 ms delay still in place, and a full serial run passes with neither file failing.
- 2026-08-23 — **A third, load-only flake is recorded rather than fixed: RecognitionDrill's missed-round test failed one full serial run on the XPS unable to find its "Done" button, and passes 3/3 in isolation.** — Same load-dependent family, different file and surface; deferred to its own task so this fix stays scoped to the two verified failures. Until then, a full-run failure of exactly this test should be read as this flake, not as a regression.

## Contrasts lane — ser/estar and por/para (2026-08-24)

- 2026-08-24 — **OWNER-APPROVED: the first non-verb grammar drill is a fourth Gym lane, Contrasts, offering ser/estar and por/para as four-choice cloze over curated original sentences; the Gym keeps its name.** — The captured Non-verb grammar drills idea wanted Diario AI feedback to pick the first pair; the owner chose these two directly while reviewing cloze/MCQ ideas against the app. Inside the Gym reuses setup, `StudySessionFrame`, the Phase 17 deck engine and the performance panel; a sibling practice home stays deferred until more non-verb lanes exist. The contract is `docs/CONTRAST-LANE-DIRECTION.md`.
- 2026-08-24 — **Ser/estar options are conjugated forms (`es / está / son / están`, with a few clear `era / estaba / fue / estuvo` cards), never bare `ser` / `estar` with filler verbs.** — The engine's four-distinct-options rule cannot be met by a two-way choice; forms test the contrast and agreement together, and the same-person form of the other verb is always the first distractor so the pedagogical target is always on the card.
- 2026-08-24 — **Contrast events reuse `drill_pass` / `drill_fail` with `skill: "contrast"`, `pair`, `answer`, `mode: "choice"` and miss-only `chosen`, and carry no `tense`.** — Writing `"es"` into the `tense` field would let any tense-keyed consumer misread it; omitting it keeps every existing tense derivation structurally blind to the lane. Usage and Endings metadata stay byte-for-byte unchanged, pinned by the existing test.
- 2026-08-24 — **Form Performance's recognition guard becomes structural: any `sessionKind: "recognition"` event or any recognition skill is excluded, replacing the two-name `usage`/`endings` blacklist.** — A red-first test showed a `skill: "contrast"` choice event leaking into Forms figures through the old guard, exactly the risk Phase 17's direction warned about ("guarded the same way before the first recognition event is ever written").
- 2026-08-24 — **Contrast guide links match only multi-word terms (`ser y estar`, `ser vs estar`, `ser/estar`, `por y para`, …).** — The shared title matcher is a substring test; a bare `para` would match a guide titled *Comparativos* and `ser` would match *Observaciones*.
- 2026-08-24 — **The Contrasts lane is implemented and verified locally; it is not pushed or deployed.** — Four commits on `main` (direction + structural Forms guard; content + engine; lane + reveal + events; statistics). The complete serial suite passes 1,681/1,681 across 142 files in 345s on the XPS (exit 0 — the known post-`App.test.jsx` `DatabaseClosedError` did not fire this run), `npm run build` transforms 2,138 modules, and `git diff --check` passes. Three deliberate failure proofs reddened as intended: a `skill: "contrast"` answer leaking into Forms performance under the old name blacklist; removing the per-card vocabulary intersection let a both-pairs deck offer `soy` on a por/para card; and dropping `alsoAcceptable` from the forbidden set reddened the shared deck test — the contrast-specific test stayed green because curated confusables fill all three distractor slots first, so that exclusion is defended by the shared engine test, not by the content test. No browser closeout by the agent: this checkout has no browser pane, so the owner's 375 px phone check against the `--host` dev URL is the visual acceptance.
- 2026-08-24 — **OWNER-REQUESTED follow-on: Connectors join the Contrasts lane as a third set (`pair: "connectors"`), not a new lane; the setup select is relabelled Set while `pair` stays the persisted key.** — The owner asked whether the lane covered syntactic-function distractors for conjunctions; it did not. A set inside the lane reuses the engine, the event shape and the per-set statistics block for free, and persisted keys are never renamed for display reasons (the Phase 17 rule). Distractor #1 is the opposite direction of the same relation (`porque` ↔ `por eso`, `aunque` ↔ `pero`) unless that connector is itself acceptable on the card.
- 2026-08-24 — **Connector cards are framed so one connector fits the slot (question–answer for `porque`, a full stop for `sin embargo`, sentence-initial `aunque`, a second subject for `en cambio`), and the set ships 26 cards rather than 32.** — Connectors are far more interchangeable than por/para, so the one-correct-answer rule cuts harder; every still-defensible alternative sits in `alsoAcceptable`. The guide-term rule now admits a single word of eight or more letters (`conectores`) beside multi-word terms, preserving its purpose of never matching a bare function word.
- 2026-08-24 — **The Connectors set is implemented and verified locally in one commit; still not pushed or deployed.** — Focused suites pass 65/65 across 7 files, the complete serial suite passes 1,682/1,682 across 142 files in 345s (exit 0), `npm run build` transforms 2,138 modules, and `git diff --check` passes. One deliberate proof: a connector distractor outside the set's vocabulary reddened both the vocabulary invariant and the opposite-direction distractor rule. The owner's 375 px phone check against the `--host` dev URL remains the visual acceptance.
- 2026-08-24 — **The Contrasts lane (three sets) is deployed from `main` at `bc5c053` on the owner's push instruction.** — Local and remote `main` matched at `bc5c0535a18404c8c918bc66e157a020946869ca` after the push, GitHub Pages run [32802784862](https://github.com/bpanozzo23-prog/mi-cuaderno/actions/runs/32802784862) completed successfully, and the live root returned HTTP 200 serving `assets/index-8nzu3WBd.js`, the same bundle name as the local production build. README Status was synchronized in the same session, per the guide.

## Forms Choose — multiple choice with morphological distractors (2026-08-24)

- 2026-08-24 — **OWNER-APPROVED: Forms gains a third answer mode, Choose — the answer plus three other forms of the same verb — beside Type and Reveal; not a lane.** — Chosen from the owner's cloze/MCQ survey after the Contrasts lane. A mode reuses the verb pool, tense packs, people, session kinds, deck builders and the Forms event shape; a lane would duplicate all of it. Type remains the session-only default. The contract is `docs/FORMS-CHOICE-DIRECTION.md`.
- 2026-08-24 — **Distractors are mixed by owner choice: one same-tense other-person form, one same-person confusable-tense form (the curated `RECOGNITION_CONFUSABLES` order), one more from either family; identical form strings and collapsed slots are excluded.** — Mixed families diagnose both agreement and tense/aspect confusion; a same-tense-only or same-person-only set would test one and hide the other. Reusing the Phase 17 confusable map keeps "confusable tense" one curated fact rather than two.
- 2026-08-24 — **A tapped wrong form is diagnosed by `diagnoseTypedAnswer`, unchanged, and the miss writes `chosen` (a dictionary form) beside the ladder `diagnosis`; Choose has no immediate retry.** — The typed ladder already classifies any form of the verb as wrong person / wrong tense / other form, so the drill gets the same diagnosis for free. `chosen` is one of four offered dictionary forms, not owner text, so the rule that the typed response is never written is untouched (the recognition precedent). The feedback names the answer, so a retry would test nothing (the Phase 17 rule).
- 2026-08-24 — **Choose is reported like Reveal: primary accuracy stays typed-only, `conjugationPerformance` gains a separate `choice` figure with its miss diagnoses, and every typed figure is pinned identical with Choose events present; Adaptive treats a Choose miss as targeting evidence and a Choose pass as exposure by its existing structure.** — The Phase 13/14 rule that different measurements never blend into one accuracy; recognition is easier than production and would inflate the headline. Adaptive already reads any non-typed initial miss as evidence, so no code changes and a pin records the fact.
- 2026-08-24 — **Forms Choose is implemented and verified locally in four commits; not pushed or deployed.** — The complete serial suite passes 1,694/1,694 across 143 files in 345s (exit 0), `npm run build` transforms 2,139 modules, and `git diff --check` passes. Three deliberate proofs reddened as intended: a `mode: "choice"` Forms event invisible to statistics before the separate figure existed; removing the answer-string exclusion let the answer appear twice among the four forms; folding Choose into the typed denominator broke the "every typed figure stays identical" pin. The Adaptive pin passed on first run, confirming Choose misses feed targeting by existing structure. No agent browser closeout: the owner's 375 px phone check against the `--host` dev URL is the visual acceptance, with four long perfect-tense chips the layout point to eyeball.
- 2026-08-24 — **Forms Choose is deployed from `main` at `f3e4d03` on the owner's push instruction.** — Local and remote `main` matched at `f3e4d03b07c943d1f94dccbffc30cfcd729289b7` after the push, GitHub Pages run [32804680746](https://github.com/bpanozzo23-prog/mi-cuaderno/actions/runs/32804680746) completed successfully, and the live root returned HTTP 200 serving `assets/index-DFM60-5l.js`, the same bundle name as the local production build. ## Transform lane — typed indicative → subjunctive (2026-08-24)

- 2026-08-24 — **OWNER-APPROVED: a fifth Gym lane, Transform — an indicative sentence, a subjunctive trigger with one blank, and a typed present-subjunctive form graded exactly; present subjunctive only for the first set.** — Chosen from the owner's cloze/MCQ survey after the Contrasts lane and Forms Choose. It is production, so not a Contrasts set; the prompt is a sentence frame, so not a Forms mode. Imperfect subjunctive and negative commands are captured as later sets. The contract is `docs/TRANSFORM-LANE-DIRECTION.md`.
- 2026-08-24 — **Answers are curated in-code and every one is reproduced from the packaged conjugation tables by a shipped-data test; every frame's verb is one of the 80 curated Gym lemmas.** — The Usage/Endings pattern: the lane works with no dictionary installed, and when it is installed `library.core` already carries the verb's table for diagnosis and the paradigm reveal, so no new lookup exists, no `dict:` key is stored and the §5 orphan path can never apply. The owner chose curated answers over session-time resolution so the lane never depends on the install.
- 2026-08-24 — **Transform events reuse `drill_pass` / `drill_fail` with `skill: "transform"`, `tense: "Subjunctive/Present"`, `mode: "typed"`, `verdict`, the typed ladder's `diagnosis`, session/prompt/stage fields and `sessionKind: "recognition"`; no typed string, verb key, slot, lemma, source or curriculum.** — Typed Endings' rule. Adaptive stays blind by structure (no verb/slot), Forms statistics by session kind with the skill set as the belt to those braces, recognition statistics by mode, review by type. `diagnosis` is additive so the report can count how often the indicative was kept.
- 2026-08-24 — **Grading reuses `diagnoseTypedAnswer` with the library table's forms when available and `[]` otherwise; one unrevealed retry; de-duplicated missed round; no accent bar.** — The typed ladder already yields accent collisions, wrong person and wrong tense (the indicative kept) for free when a table exists, and degrades to exact / accents / wrong without one. The accent bar stays owner-declined (`docs/IMPROVEMENT-IDEAS.md`, Deferred).
